# Milestone 9.5: Tuning

> First written 2026-09-22 against `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-22-adaptive-orchestrator-design.md` ("the spec" below). **Refreshed 2026-10-03 against `m9.3-keep-going` at `c8d77428`** (milestone 9.3's head, protocol 15), from two read-only audits of the stale brief: the code audit (findings F1–F243, "audit F<n>") and the follow-ups audit (items F1–F46 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-17-anthrex-foundation-followups.md`, "FU-F<n>"), and the controller's binding rulings on both ("Controller rulings" below, cited by id). Every `file:line` is on `c8d77428`, relative to `crates/` unless the path says otherwise, and every line count is `wc -l` there. Milestones M8a to 9.3 are merged code now, not briefs: names below are the merged ones. Besides the spec, these bind the milestone: the tiered-testing and PR-delivery spec (`2026-09-26-tiered-testing-and-pr-delivery-design.md`, "TT"; its §11 supersedes the spec's §11.3, §11.5 and §11.6 where the race relies on them), the keep-going spec (`2026-10-02-keep-going-design.md`, "KG"; §2.6 and §11), and the TUI spec (`2026-10-01-tui-end-to-end-design.md`, the 9.0.6 and 9.0.7 design kit). "M8a", "M8b", "M8c", "M9", "9.0.5", "9.0.6", "9.0.7", "9.1", "9.2" and "9.3" are the briefs in `docs/milestones/`.

The design rules that bind every decision here, never reinterpreted:

- **Only the orchestrator is an interactive PTY window.** Racers and test writers are headless sessions on `crate::headless`, like workers.
- **The engine stays deterministic.** Every new transition (a refit at start, a halving, a crown, a hand-off to an implementer) happens in the pure reducer on an event, with an injected clock.
- **Nothing learned changes a running run.** What history teaches is frozen into `RunLimits` when a run starts (decision 12).
- **Nothing is written into the user's repository.** `tuning.toml` lives in anthrex's data directory.
- **No new kill code, signal or probe** (ruling RR-2). Processes are stopped only through the existing `KillWindow` paths.
- **No blocking work under a lock** (AGENTS.md rules 2, 10 and 11). Every git call goes through `run::git`, off the engine lock, with a timeout.
- **Tests use `fake-agent` only.** Agents never run the real `claude` or `codex` (`.superpowers/sdd/M9.5-tuning/SAFETY.md`); a fact that needs the real CLI is handed to the user (task M9.5.1) and its decision's fallback holds until the user records it.

## Goal

Runs get better from their own history, and faster where the spec allows.

`anthrex run stats` shows, beside M8b's recorded medians, what the history has taught: every size class that has reached `min_samples` merged plan tasks (default 30) gets **budgets refitted automatically** to 2.5 times its median tool calls and working minutes, unless the user configured that class's budget, and **critical-path weights** from its median active time. New **line thresholds** for S and M, and **routing** changes for a class that escalates too often or never, are **proposed**, each with an id, the evidence and the exact change; `anthrex run stats --apply <id>` applies one after the user confirms it, and `--dismiss <id>` silences it. The user can also give **model lists** per class and per role, which win over everything learned. Everything learned is stored in anthrex's data directory and frozen into a run when it starts.

While a run works, a **rate-limit event** in a task session halves the number of writers that runtime may hold (never below one), and every `recover_after_mins` without another adds one back. The run inspector shows the time left (`est. left ~40m`) and how far the current round is from the best the graph allows (`1.2× the bound`).

A planner can opt a critical-path task into a **race**: two headless workers on different runtimes, each in its own standalone checkout `<task>.a` / `<task>.b`, each through every pre-merge gate; the first to pass them all becomes the task by a compare-and-swap of its branch, and the other is stopped and salvaged. It can opt a TDD task into **pairing**: a headless test writer commits the failing test and stops, and a different headless worker makes it pass in the same checkout. Both are off unless a task asks. Codex workers get **filtered test output**. All of it is drawn live in `C-b T`.

The milestone also folds in the follow-ups the user's live runs found: history and stats that are right before the refit reads them (X3), an orchestrator whose first turn cannot miss its tools or sit unseen at a prompt (X1), routing that skips what is not installed or just failed (X2), and five small fixes that ride the protocol bump (X4).

## Scope

In:

- History refit (§15): samples of plan code tasks with a per-(run, round) cap, budgets refitted automatically unless configured, critical-path weights, line-threshold and routing proposals, `tuning.toml`, `anthrex run stats --apply|--dismiss`, and the tuning block shown read-only in the TUI's stats screen and as a muted note in Settings.
- Freezing what was learned into `RunLimits` at a run's start (plan, goal, continued run), and using it in every round: task budgets, the rung-4 ceiling, class routes, critical-path weights, thresholds in decider prompts and in M9's planner context.
- The user's model lists, `[orchestrator.routes.<class-or-role>]` (decision 9a), with the installed check and the "failed in this task" skip (X2).
- The round-aware run estimate and bound ratio.
- Adaptive concurrency inside a run (§13 item 9), from task-session rate-limit events.
- The race (§4.1, §11.6) over standalone lane checkouts, and the test-writer-then-implementer pair (§4.1), each opt-in per task, through stages, tiers, the test slots, weakening signals, gate holds, `reach.rs` and the action menu.
- The output filter for Codex workers (by instruction unless the CLI's hook facts are recorded).
- Racer and test-writer rounds and the new fields in the run view and inspector, in 9.0.7's glyphs and roles.
- History, report and `run status` additions, `fake-agent` roles, and smoke stage `11g`.
- The folded follow-ups X1–X4 (decisions 33–48; "Follow-ups handled").

Out:

| Out | Why, and who owns it |
|-----|----------------------|
| Stub-then-fill | Deferred by spec §13's last paragraph until real runs show the need. |
| A merge queue wider than 1 | Same paragraph; candidates would no longer reflect the stage head. |
| A learned router | Spec §15: out until there are hundreds of records. Routing proposals are two fixed rules. |
| Refitting thresholds or routes automatically | Spec §15: they change what the planner may do, so they are proposed. |
| Changing a running run's limits after a refit | Frozen at start (decision 12); a round keeps the run's values (KG §2.6). |
| Coordinating writer caps across runs on one subscription | Each run adapts on its own signals. |
| Racing with more than two lanes, or two lanes on one runtime | Spec §4.1: two workers on different runtimes. |
| A hub race | Ruling RR-5: a hub runs alone. A hub may pair. |
| Rate-limit events from orchestrator, planner, scout and decider sessions (and a cap on readers) | Ruling RC-1: only task sessions count today; the others are filed. |
| The Codex usage limit's reset time (runtime unavailable until then; FU-F1) | Ruling RC-2: the reset text has no time zone and nobody has set the rule. Filed. |
| A new Alerts kind for a writer cap | Ruling RC-3: the cap is an attention line only. |
| Apply or dismiss from the TUI, or a new `ActionKind` | Ruling RH-6: the TUI shows the tuning block read-only with the CLI hint. |
| Reverts that land through GitHub in `pr` mode | Ruling RH-2: a squash or rebase merge cannot be mapped to a task merge; accepted (Risks). |
| Pruning `history.jsonl`, `tuning.toml` or `tuning.toml.bad-*` | M8b's pruning follow-up. |
| FU-F8: spill diff and `diff_so_far` after `resume --rebaseline` | A done-gate (M8a) correctness fix; rare; belongs to a gates pass with its own review. |
| FU-F9: slimming terminal runs in the snapshot | The measured bound (`SNAPSHOT_BOUND`) holds; do it when sizes demand. |
| FU-F18: stray tier step directories | Tiers housekeeping, outside tuning. |
| FU-F19: an `--ignored` tier | A tiers feature with its own profile key. |
| FU-F20: a hand-edited `run.json` with a stage gap | Restore validation, outside tuning; needs a ruling on refuse or recreate. |
| FU-F25: salvage refs that hold only setup output | Needs a design choice (diff against setup, or prune on accept). |
| FU-F28: the overview's second inspection per frame | No measured need. |
| FU-F29–F34: `pr`-mode delivery defects (merged stage's fix tasks, `tier 3 ◌` on an open PR, `gate.holds` in the digest, a CI bisect over the whole line, a double user push, the `ci_summary` intent's log) | Delivery hardening, outside tuning; recommended as milestone 9.6 in the follow-ups file. |
| FU-F38: a next goal while a `pr` run's PRs are open | A feature gated on user demand. |
| FU-F41: `anthrex tree` numbering vs the sidebar's | CLI/TUI polish for the next TUI pass. |
| FU-F42: smoke 11f's stale module doc | A one-line doc fix for any branch touching 11f. |
| FU-F44: the task edit form's brief in the editor | TUI polish for the next TUI pass. |
| FU-F45: an older binary rewriting a multi-round `run.json` | Downgrade is not a requirement. |
| FU-F46: the compact goal dialog's hint on short terminals | TUI polish for the next TUI pass. |
| A suggested chain length from history (KG §11) | Audit F243 item 24: needs chain statistics nobody records yet. |
| Delivery statistics from `StageLine`, per-origin fix-task accounting, `DeliveryLimits` against outcomes (9.2's "produces for 9.5") | Audit F243 item 25: delivery hardening first; then a stats pass. |
| Planner usage and planner-route refit in stats, orchestrator cost in stats, message and refresh effectiveness, configurable planner bounds, the integration-review exemption cap (M9's "produces for 9.5") | Audit F243 item 26: role statistics are a separate refit; this milestone refits task classes only. |

## Controller rulings (2026-10-03 refresh)

Binding; they override the audits where they differ. Every decision below cites the rulings it implements.

**Mechanical.** Every mechanical finding of the code audit is applied as it proposes: names, files, line counts, budgets, protocol 16, `HISTORY_VERSION` stays 5, and the hook points.

**Race.**
- **RR-1 (audit F68, F73, F74). Each lane is its own standalone task checkout.** Its checkout name is `<task>.a` / `<task>.b`, and every path helper keyed by task id is called with that name: repo dir, objects, engine dir, `TMPDIR`, `worker_git_roots`. One helper, `Task::checkout_name()`, returns the task id, or the crowned or adopted lane's name; every site that derives the task's checkout from `task.id()` goes through it.
  - **The crown.** The lane's head has already been imported at its gates. The crown is only a compare-and-swap creating `refs/heads/anthrex/<run>/<task>` at that head: `update-ref <ref> <head> <zero oid of the object format>`. There is no checkout step. A task branch that already exists at the lane head counts as done; one at another commit gives `RefMoved` and halts the run.
  - **Stale locks.** `clear_stale_locks` covers only what a worker can leave behind: `index.lock` and `HEAD.lock` in the lane checkout's own git dir. There is no common-dir ref lock.
  - **Reconcile.** The row becomes "task branch at lane head → `Crowned`".
- **RR-2 (F75). No new kill code, signal or probe.** The loser's racer and reviewer are stopped through the existing `KillWindow` path, unchanged. The lane's `RemoveWorktree` is emitted only after that kill has finished and every pending op on the lane has returned. If the racer is not known to have exited, the worktree is not removed: the lane is logged `kept <lane> checkout: its racer did not exit`, and salvage still runs. `kill_group` and `STALE_GROUP_WAIT` are dropped. ("Spec and earlier-brief defects" item 16 records how "finished" is read on a headless session.)
- **RR-3 (F71). Each lane runs the full pre-merge gates on its own:** done; proof; tier 1 scratch, or the check on an untiered profile; the 9.1 test-weakening signals; review. All of it goes through the test slots, so slot use doubles; racing is opt-in and costs twice. Tier 2 (the candidate) runs only for the crowned task, in the merge queue like any task.
- **RR-4 (F72).** `message`, `refresh` and an `amend_task` brief go to every live lane. `stop_and_wait` (`MessagePause`) on a racing task is refused with `task <id> is racing: it cannot stop and wait; message it or cancel it`, and the action menu shows the same reason muted *(the action-menu part is dropped by review ruling I8: the refusal is on the request path only)*. A lane's `task_note` is recorded with its lane.
- **RR-5 (F81). A hub cannot race.** Validation: `task <id>: race: a hub task cannot race, because a hub runs alone (rule 4.1.race)`. A hub may still pair.
- **RR-6 (F83, F85).** A `race` or `pair` set by the orchestrator mid-run, through an add or an amend, goes through M9's gate holds like any orchestrator task change. `reach.rs` counts lane b's runtime and the test writer's peer runtime. A race or pair amend counts as widening in `edits_may_widen`, so the project-trust check applies.
- **RR-7 (F84).** 9.3's earlier-round refusal comes before the race/pair refusals.
- **RR-8 (F82).** Reuse `edits_state::not_started` and the existing sentence shape: `task <id> has started: its race cannot change` (or `its pair`). *(Review ruling I9 replaces `not_started` with the stage rule's "started" predicate.)*
- **RR-9 (F78, F80).** Extend `ladder::worker_round` and the existing worker checks; there is no second notion. Writer slots count lanes: `writers_busy` counts a racing task as one per live lane.

**Pair.**
- **RP-1 (F88).** The red check is a red-only mode of the existing Proof (`run_proof_scheduled`), through the slots, with the `repo` field. There is no new runner.
- **RP-2 (F89).** A paired task's implementer has its weakening signals diffed from `red`, not from the stage head, so a weakened test raises a W-signal. `pair_reviewer_note` is one sentence pointing at those signals.

**History and refit.**
- **RH-1 (F30, F32).** Budget, threshold and route samples are records with `origin == Plan` and `kind == code` only. Engine-made fix tasks are never samples.
- **RH-2 (F31).** The "down" rule's quality evidence, any of which holds a class where it is: a revert, with revert-of-revert fixed (FU-F2); a 9.1 bisect culprit naming the task; a 9.2 CI or review fix task "whose `fixes` names the task" (applied as decision 4 states: CI and review fix tasks name a stage, not a task). In `pr` mode, reverts that land through GitHub cannot be mapped; accepted and documented under Risks.
- **RH-3 (F33).** The per-run cap is per `(run_id, round)`. Each round is a separate request.
- **RH-4 (F35, F36).** Keep `phases.working` and Risk 1. S's rung-4 ceiling becomes the effective, refit M budget, and rung 4 compares within the budget epoch.
- **RH-5 (F28). An explicit `[orchestrator.budget.<class>]` in config beats the refit.** The config read tells an explicit value from a default. With an explicit value, `run stats` shows `budget S: configured 40 calls 15m (refit would be 55 calls 18m)`, and the run log's tuning line says `configured`. The Settings screen shows a muted `refit: 55 calls 18m` beside the class when a refit exists and the class is not configured explicitly. Orchestrator route precedence: an explicit per-run `OrchestratorChoice` (goal form, `run promote --orchestrator`), then a continued chain's existing route, then `[orchestrator.routes.orchestrator]`, then `[orchestrator.agent]`. When the list overrides Settings' orchestrator default, Settings shows a muted `overridden by [orchestrator.routes.orchestrator]`.
- **RH-6 (F16).** The TUI stats screen shows the tuning block read-only, with the hint `apply: anthrex run stats --apply <id>`. No apply or dismiss in the TUI, and no new `ActionKind`.
- **RH-7 (F27, F110, F127).** The table is `[orchestrator.tuning]`, in `config/src/orchestrator/tuning.rs`. The types are `TuningProposal` and `TuningChange`. The stats heading is `tuning proposals:`.
- **RH-8 (F53).** `tune_for_start` is called from `build_delivered` only. Rounds (`Iterate`) never re-tune; a continued run (chain) tunes fresh, because it is a new run.

**Estimate.**
- **RE-1 (F56).** Round-aware: `elapsed` runs from the current round's approval; `bound` and `remaining` cover the current round's tasks plus earlier rounds' unfinished fix tasks.
- **RE-2 (F57).** In `pr` mode, subtract `human_review_secs` from `elapsed`. The estimate is `None` while a run is `delivering` with no task running.
- **RE-3 (F58).** Reader tasks count on paths (CP, RCP) but not in the work sum divided by `max_writers`.
- **RE-4 (F59).** Keep `paused_at`, `paused_secs` and `last_step_at`. On restore, derive the downtime from `Run.restored` where it is set.

**Concurrency.**
- **RC-1 (F61).** Rate-limit events are counted where they are today, in the task rounds: worker, racer, test writer, reviewer and research. "Every run session counts" becomes "every task session counts". Orchestrator, planner, scout and decider sessions are filed for later.
- **RC-2 (F63, FU-F1).** The Codex usage-limit reset time stays filed.
- **RC-3 (F64).** The cap is an attention line only; no new Alerts kind.

**Model lists.**
- **RL-1 (F45, FU-F24).** Rung 2 and `run retry` skip a candidate whose session in this task just failed for an environment reason, recorded with skip reason `failed in this task`. With no list, the same applies to the roster pick.
- **RL-2 (F46, FU-F10).** List candidates on a runtime not installed for the run are skipped with `not installed`. Onboarding scouts and deciders also get the installed check: a Codex-only user gets Codex ones, and with nothing installed, deciders fall back as today. *(Review ruling I6 says where the check's I/O comes from.)*
- **RL-3 (F47).** Orchestrator precedence is as RH-5.
- **RL-4 (F48).** Research tasks route by the `scout` list; review tasks by the `review` list.

**Follow-ups.**
- **RF-1.** Fold in, as extra tasks: **X3, history and stats correctness**, before the refit task (FU-F2, F37, F40, F43, F17, F15; F15 also sweeps `tuning.toml`'s `.tmp`); **X1, orchestrator first-turn reliability** (F12: the first turn waits until the anthrex MCP server is connected, a bounded wait, and the contract adds the `get_context` retry line; F13: a daemon attention line for a start prompt; F27: turn off the Codex orchestrator's user-question tool if the CLI allows it, task 1 verifies, otherwise detect the footer and raise `Attention`; F26: log why a wake waits; F11: an alert count under the plan review); **X2, routing** (FU-F10, F24, folded into model lists as RL-1, RL-2); **X4, small fixes riding protocol 16** (F23, F7, F21, F36, F39).
- **RF-2.** Everything else filed for 9.5 goes into the Out table with a reason. FU-F14, F16, F22 and F35 are marked fixed in the follow-ups file. A "9.6 delivery hardening" milestone for FU-F29–F34 is recommended in the follow-ups file, not added to the roadmap.

**Review of the refreshed brief (2026-10-03).** A read-only review of this brief at `89651bba` found 1 critical, 11 important and 12 minor findings. The controller ruled on the critical and important ones as below; every minor finding is applied (each listed under "Spec and earlier-brief defects", items 20–31). These rulings are binding and override the rulings above where they differ.
- **C1. The first-turn paste needs `mcp_ready` and `signals_seen`** (Claude orchestrators only, ruling T5a-2). A window that has sent no hook signal is never pasted into, whatever `mcp_ready` says: a quiet unsignalled window is exactly a start (or folder-trust) prompt, and the paste's `\r` would answer it. Past `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS` nothing is pasted: the daemon raises the start-prompt attention line (decision 39's `START_PROMPT`) and keeps waiting, and the paste happens once both have arrived. Decision 38; task M9.5.5a.
- **I1. The run view's rounds live in `tree/run_rows.rs`, not `tree/round_rows.rs`.** `display_rounds`, `rounds_with` and `role_rank` (`run_rows.rs:45-115`) move, move-only, to a new `tui/src/tree/task_rounds.rs` in task M9.5.3, and the lane splitting and ordering land there. Decision 29; File sizes; tasks M9.5.3, M9.5.20.
- **I2. The `CrownRacer` executor re-pins.** The op carries the lane checkout's path, and the executor in `driver/lane_ops.rs` calls `worktree::pinned::repin_own` right after a `Crowned` result; the test drives the op through the executor. Decision 21; Interfaces; task M9.5.15.
- **I3. A task owns the daemon model.** New task M9.5.3b adds `Race`, `Lane`, `Pair`, `Task.{race, pair, race_wait_since}`, `Run.concurrency` and the per-record `lane` fields, all `#[serde(default)]`, with a `run.json` round-trip test and an old-`run.json` test; later tasks use these types.
- **I4. Counting coverage follows its tasks.** M9.5.13's `every_task_session_counts` covers worker, reviewer and research sessions; racers are covered in M9.5.17a, test writers in M9.5.16.
- **I5. One `now` for the CLI block.** The block's date is `now = 1_790_500_000` (2026-09-27 09:06:40 UTC), the value M9.5.8 injects. Interfaces "CLI".
- **I6. The installed check reuses the run start's probe.** The onboarding scout and the decider take what `driver/build.rs::found` (the I/O behind `run/orch/installed.rs`, today called only by `RunService::make_planned`) finds, through a new `driver/build.rs::found_within(claude, codex, timeout)` on `spawn_blocking` with `INSTALLED_PROBE_TIMEOUT`, at the onboarding start and each time the decider picks a runtime. No daemon-wide cache. Decision 9a; task M9.5.10b.
- **I7. The tier-3 propagate alert names the red commit:** `propagate red at <sha7>`. Decision 45; task M9.5.6.
- **I8. `stop_and_wait` for a racing task is refused on the request path only** (the MCP `message` tool and `run message`, through `edits_orch::takes`); the action menu is not changed. Decision 19; task M9.5.17b.
- **I9. A race or pair amend uses the stage rule's "started" predicate** (`edits_amend.rs:72-83`): a task that is not `not_started`, or a blocked task with a `start_commit`, has started. Decision 17; task M9.5.14.
- **I10. Task sizes.** M9.5.10 is split into M9.5.10a (task-class lists, rung 2, reviewers, routing history, what the planner sees) and M9.5.10b (roles, the orchestrator, onboarding scouts, deciders and the installed probe, the failed-in-this-task skip). M9.5.15 and M9.5.17a stay whole, with their tests listed in parts so each fits one implementer.
- **I11. The Codex question footer is scanned on every manager tick**, for the orchestrator's window whatever its status, in `WindowManager::tick` (`manager/mod.rs:329`), not on the quiet tick. Decision 40; task M9.5.5b.

## Design decisions

Numbered and final; decisions 1–32 keep the numbers of the 2026-09-22 brief, so older references hold, and 33–48 are the folded follow-ups. If one proves wrong or impossible, stop work on it, record the evidence under "Implementation notes", and continue with the tasks that do not depend on it.

### Structure

1. **Where the code lives** (audit F21–F23). Daemon code is in `daemon/src/run/`:
   - `refit.rs` (samples, statistics, budgets, weights, proposals, apply and dismiss) and `refit_render.rs` (the tuning block's text);
   - `tuning_io.rs` (reading and writing `tuning.toml`);
   - `estimate.rs` (the run estimate and bound ratio);
   - `validate_patterns.rs` (the `race` and `pair` plan rules);
   - `route_pick.rs` (decision 9a's picking);
   - `contract_patterns.rs` (the test writer's contract and the pair texts);
   - `role_launch_patterns.rs` (the racer and test-writer `HeadlessSpec`s);
   - `model_tuning.rs` (new model structs, `#[path = "model_tuning.rs"] mod tuning; pub use tuning::*;` in `model.rs`, as `model.rs:19-49` does for its other parts); `RunLimits` additions go in `model_limits.rs`, `Task` additions in `model_task.rs`;
   - `snapshot_patterns.rs` (`RaceInfo`, `PairInfo` and `writer_caps` for the snapshot);
   - `engine/concurrency.rs`, `engine/race.rs`, `engine/race_end.rs` (crown, loss, adoption), `engine/pair.rs`;
   - `git/crown.rs` (the crown op and stale-lock clearing; not `git/race.rs`, because `daemon/tests/run_git_live_race.rs` already names an unrelated concurrency test);
   - `driver/tuning.rs` (loading, refitting and saving at a start and at `run stats`);
   - `driver/lane_ops.rs` (executing `CrownRacer`, with its re-pin, and the lane's `RemoveWorktree` variants, so `driver/ops.rs` only gains their match arms).

   The scheduler hooks are `engine/schedule.rs` and `engine/dispatch_launch.rs`, not `dispatch.rs`. **Pure** (no `std::fs`, `std::process`, `std::thread`, `tokio`, `SystemTime`): `refit.rs`, `refit_render.rs`, `estimate.rs`, `validate_patterns.rs`, `route_pick.rs`, `contract_patterns.rs`, `role_launch_patterns.rs`, `model_tuning.rs`, `snapshot_patterns.rs`, `engine/{concurrency,race,race_end,pair}.rs`. **I/O**: `tuning_io.rs`, `git/crown.rs`, `driver/tuning.rs`, `driver/lane_ops.rs`.
2. **Protocol** (audit F24–F26, §Q). `PROTO_VERSION` becomes **16** (header). Every field added to an existing struct is `#[serde(default)]` and every new variant is appended last, so a protocol-15 (9.3) `run.json`, snapshot and `history.jsonl` still deserialize. The wire changes, all in task M9.5.2:
   - `AgentRole::{Racer, TestWriter}` appended after `Decider` (`"racer"`, `"test_writer"` by the enum's `snake_case`).
   - New `proto/src/tuning.rs`: `RaceLane`, `LaneState`, `LaneInfo`, `RaceInfo`, `PairPhase`, `PairInfo`, `TaskPattern`, the tuning file types, `ClassTuning`, `RefitState`, `TuningChange`, `TuningProposal`, `TuningReport`.
   - `PlanTask.{race, pair}`, `PlanEdit::AmendTask.{race, pair}`, `RunRef.lane`, `ToolCall.lane` (after `chain`).
   - `RunInfo.writer_caps`; `TaskInfo.{race, pair}`; `AgentRoundInfo.{lane, failed_error, failed_until}` (decision 43); `ReviewInfo.lane`; `RunInfo.estimate_left_secs` and `bound_ratio_permille` are filled (they exist).
   - `RunRequest::Stats.{apply, dismiss, read_only}` (decision 48); `HistoryStats.tuning`; `TaskRecord.{pattern, race_winner, race_adopted, writer_failures, round}`.
   - `RunRequest::McpReady { run_id, window_id }`, appended after `Iterate`, with `request::MCP_READY = "mcp ready"` (decision 38).
   - `WindowInfo.placeholder` (decision 44); `FullInfo.held` (decision 45).

   Every client is updated in the same change (AGENTS.md rule 4): the TUI (`app/stats.rs`'s `Stats` request, every `AgentRole` match, the run view), the CLI (`run_cmd.rs`, `mcp_cmd.rs`, `run_cmd/status.rs`, `run_cmd/adapt.rs`), `anthrex mcp`, `anthrex filter-hook` and `fake-agent`. Every new or changed message gets a MessagePack round-trip test, untagged and tagged (`ClientMsg::RunTagged`). Daemon-only types (`RunLimits`, `Run`, `Task`, `OpKind`, `OpResult`, `McpTarget.lane`, `CliCaps`) are not wire.
3. **Configuration** (ruling RH-7; audit F27). One new table, `[orchestrator.tuning]`, and the model lists `[orchestrator.routes.<name>]` (decision 9a), read in a new `config/src/orchestrator/tuning.rs` (the lists in `config/src/orchestrator/routes.rs`) by one call from `orchestrator::read`, into one new field `config::Orchestrator.tuning: TuningConfig { table: Tuning, routes: RouteLists, configured: ConfiguredBudgets }`. `orchestrator.rs` (591) gains that field, its default and the call, nothing else. `tuning.rs` also reads, from the raw table, whether `[orchestrator.budget.s]` and `[orchestrator.budget.m]` are present (`ConfiguredBudgets { s: bool, m: bool }`, ruling RH-5): a value saved from the Settings screen is in the file, so it is explicit. The Settings screen never writes a value equal to its default (`settings/write.rs:307`), so saving 40/15 from Settings does not make S explicit; to hold a class at the defaults against a refit, the user writes the table into `config.toml` by hand. Keys, defaults and ranges are in Interfaces. An invalid value is a `Problem` that keeps the default, in M8a's message format (`orchestrator.tuning.min_samples: must be between 5 and 1000 (using 30)`). Unknown keys are registered in `orchestrator/unknown.rs` (`KNOWN_TUNING_KEYS`, `KNOWN_ROUTE_KEYS`, `KNOWN_CANDIDATE_KEYS`) and reported `unknown key, ignored`. The spec's `adapt.min_samples` is `[orchestrator.tuning] min_samples`. `config/src/lib.rs` is not touched.

### History and refit

4. **Samples** (rulings RH-1, RH-2, RH-3; audit F29–F33). `run::refit` reads history through `run::history_io::read_history`, which keeps the last line per `record_id`. A task record's **class** is `run::refit::class_of`: `hub` when `hub` is true, else `S` or `M` by `final_size`; an `L` record has none. This is M8b's private `stats::class` (`stats.rs:20-27`), moved to `refit::class_of` unchanged and called from both; `stats::median` (`:37`, already the lower middle) is reused as `refit::lower_median`. Three sample sets are drawn per class, each in this order:
   1. **Filter.** Every set takes only records with `origin == Plan` and `kind == Code` (RH-1: engine-made bisect, sync, CI and review fix tasks, and research and review tasks, are never samples) and `pattern != Some(Race)` (a race's `tool_calls` and time add two lanes).
      - *Budget and threshold samples*: `outcome == Merged` (not `MergedWithoutApproval`), and not reverted (decision 33's effective reverts: no revert in effect names the task or, with `task_id = None`, its run). Threshold samples also need `diff: Some(..)`.
      - *Route samples*: `sessions >= 1`, whatever the outcome: the tasks that escalated and never merged are the evidence a routing rule needs.
   2. **Per-(run, round) cap** (RH-3). Sort by `(at, record_id)`. Keep at most `per_run_cap` (default 10) records per `(run_id, round)`, the newest. `TaskRecord.round` is new (`#[serde(default)]`; 0 on an older line reads as round 1). A 9.3 run iterated 20 times contributes up to 10 per round; one pathological round still cannot supply more than a third of the default 30.
   3. **Window.** Keep the newest `window` (default 200).

   A class **qualifies** when its set has at least `min_samples` (default 30) records.

   **Quality evidence** (RH-2), read by decision 9's "down" rule over a class's route samples. A route sample *failed on quality* when any of these holds:
   - a revert in effect names it (decision 33);
   - a `BisectLine` of its run names it as `culprit` (9.1's bisect culprit is a task id: `engine/bisect.rs:514`, `run.task(culprit)`);
   - a CI or review fix task's record (`origin == Ci` or `Review`) has its `run_id` and `stage`. CI and review fix tasks name a stage and PR threads, never a task (`FixOf::Ci { stage, .. }`, `FixOf::Review { stage, pr, threads }`, `model_stages.rs:262-273`), and `TaskRecord` has no `fixes` field, so RH-2's "fix task whose `fixes` names the task" is applied to every plan task of that run's stage. That is conservative: it can only hold a class where it is.
5. **Statistics, in integers.** `lower_median(values)` is the value at index `(n - 1) / 2` of the sorted values. `percentile(values, p)` is nearest rank: the value at index `ceil(p × n / 100) - 1`. Both are `None` for an empty set. Nothing uses floating point. A task's **active seconds** are `phases.preparing + phases.working + phases.proof + phases.check + phases.review + phases.merge` (`PhaseSecs`, `proto/src/adapt.rs:80-88`; tier 1 and tier 2 time lands in `check`; stage-level tier 3 and a PR's human wait are not per task and are not in it, audit F34).
6. **Budgets refit automatically, unless configured** (rulings RH-4, RH-5; audit F35–F38). For each qualifying class, with `f = budget_factor_percent` (default 250, range 200–300):
   - `tool_calls = ceil(lower_median(tool_calls) × f / 100)`, clamped to 10..=2000;
   - `minutes = ceil(lower_median(phases.working) × f / 100 / 60)`, clamped to 5..=1440 (RH-4 keeps `phases.working`; Risk 1);
   - `tokens = ceil(lower_median(worker_usage.billable()) × f / 100)` only when `refit_tokens = true` (default false).

   `TaskRecord.tool_calls` is the task's total over its sessions while a budget counts one session, so the refit is conservative for escalated tasks. The new budget is written to `tuning.toml` only when it differs from the class's current refit (or, with none, the configured default) by at least `min_change_percent` (default 20) on either axis: `|new − cur| × 100 >= min_change_percent × cur`. It is computed at every start (decision 12) and every `run stats`. `refit_budgets = false` turns it off, and `tuning.toml` budgets are then ignored.

   **Precedence** for a task's budget, highest first: the plan's `[task.budget]`; an explicit `[orchestrator.budget.s]` or `[orchestrator.budget.m]` in config (RH-5, `ConfiguredBudgets`); the refit budget of its class; the config default. A hub task uses the `hub` refit budget when there is one, else M's effective budget, as `validate.rs:216` uses `budget_m` today. `ConfiguredBudgets` has S and M only: because a hub task takes M's budget today, an explicit `[orchestrator.budget.m]` also holds hub tasks (the hub refit is computed and shown, never used, while `configured.m`). With an explicit value the refit is still computed and shown, never used: `run stats` shows `budget S: configured 40 calls 15m (refit would be 55 calls 18m)` (Interfaces "CLI"), and the run log line reads `tuning: budget S 40 calls 15m configured (refit would be 55 calls 18m)`.

   **The rung-4 ceiling** (`ladder::ceiling`, `ladder.rs:405-411`, in `engine/ladder_budget.rs` after M9.5.3's split) becomes, per axis: for an S task, the effective M budget (the refit M budget when M is refit and not configured, else the configured M); for an M or hub task, the larger of `[orchestrator.budget.l]` and twice the effective M budget, so a refit M budget never sits above the ceiling meant to be the next size's. Rung 4 compares the spend within the task's current budget epoch (`clock.rs::BudgetEpoch`, ruling T15-C1) against this frozen ceiling.
7. **Critical-path weights from history** (audit F39). With `path_weights = true` (default), weights are written to `tuning.toml` whenever at least one class qualifies, from the lower median of **active seconds** over its budget samples, and rewritten only when a weight changes by `min_change_percent`. A class that does not qualify is derived, in this order: `m_secs` = measured M, else measured hub, else `3 × s_secs`; `hub_secs` = measured hub, else `m_secs`; `s_secs` = measured S, else `ceil(m_secs / 3)`. Derived classes are listed in `PathWeights.derived`. `engine/schedule.rs::weight` (`:213`) changes from `weight(task) -> u32` to `weight(limits: &RunLimits, task: &Task) -> u64`: the class's seconds when `limits.path_weights` is `Some`, else M8a's 1 (S) and 3 (M, L, hub); `critical_lens` (`:245`) returns `Vec<u64>`. There is no second weight function: `estimate.rs` calls `schedule::weight`.
8. **Line thresholds are proposed.** The engine enforces no line count; line counts appear only in the decider prompts (`decider/prompt.rs:30,35`) and the planners' rubric (`orch/context.rs:23,25`). For each of S and M whose threshold samples qualify: `lines = added + removed`; the proposed threshold is `percentile(lines, threshold_percentile)` (default 90) rounded **up** to a multiple of 5 (S) or 10 (M), at least 5 or 10. It is proposed when it differs from the current one (`tuning.toml`'s, else 20 and 100) by at least `min_change_percent`. An M proposal is dropped when it would not exceed the current S threshold. Ids `thresholds.s`, `thresholds.m`. Hunks are not refitted.
9. **Routing is proposed.** Only class defaults are proposed; a planner's explicit route always wins. Fixed ladders inside spec §9's table:
   - S: `fast/low`, `fast/medium`, `standard/low` (M8a's default), `standard/medium`;
   - M: `standard/medium` (M8a's default), `standard/high`, `frontier/medium`, `frontier/high`.

   Hub stays `frontier/high` and is never proposed. Over a class's qualifying **route samples**:
   - **Up** when `escalated × 100 > n × escalate_above_percent` (default 25), where `escalated` counts `max_rung >= 2`: the next step up.
   - **Down** when `escalated == 0` and no route sample failed on quality (decision 4's evidence): the next step down. A single escalation or quality failure holds a class where it is.
   - No step → no proposal.

   Ids `route.s`, `route.m`.
9a. **The user's model lists** (rulings RL-1 to RL-4, RH-5; audit F42–F50; folds X2: FU-F10, FU-F24). *(Added 2026-09-23 at the user's request.)* One table per task class and per role, each an ordered list of **candidates** and an optional `pick`:

    ```toml
    [orchestrator.routes.m]
    candidates = [
      { runtime = "codex",  model = "gpt-6.1-sol",     effort = "high"   },
      { runtime = "claude", model = "claude-opus-5-5", effort = "medium" },
    ]
    pick = "spread"          # "first" (default) | "spread"

    [orchestrator.routes.scout]
    candidates = [
      { runtime = "codex",  model = "gpt-6-luna" },
      { runtime = "claude", model = "claude-sonnet-5", effort = "low" },
    ]
    ```

    The model names are shipped rows (`settings/shipped.rs`: `gpt-6.1-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`); the default roster (`config/src/orchestrator/roster.rs::default_roster`) holds only the three Claude models and Codex's default, so a test adds the Codex rows it names to its roster (task M9.5.7).
    - **Tables.** Task classes `s`, `m`, `hub`; roles `review`, `scout`, `decider`, `planner` (sub-planners) and `orchestrator`. An absent table, or one whose list is empty after validation, leaves that class or role exactly as today.
    - **A candidate** is `runtime` (`claude | codex`), `model` (non-empty; in the merged roster for its runtime), and an optional `effort` (`low | medium | high`). A candidate without `effort` takes the class's or role's default effort. An entry not in the roster is dropped with a `Problem`: `orchestrator.routes.m.candidates[1]: claude/claude-opus-9 is not in the roster (entry skipped)`; a bad runtime or effort likewise (`must be claude or codex`, `must be low, medium or high`). Unknown keys and tables are `unknown key, ignored`.
    - **Skipped candidates** (RL-1, RL-2). At every pick, a candidate is skipped, with its `skipped_reason` recorded in the routing snapshot, when:
      - its runtime is not installed for the run (`run.orch.installed`, the map the start's probe found): `not installed`;
      - it is the route of a session of **this task** that just ended for an environment reason (a `blocked(environment)` from its own failed turn, or ruling F-1's client error), for rung 2 and `run retry`: `failed in this task`. With no list, the same skip applies to the roster pick (`roster::pick_reviewer`, `roster::escalate`): the failed route is excluded and the next qualifying entry taken.
      - Every candidate skipped leaves the class or role to today's resolution (which applies the same two skips).
    - **Worker classes (`s`, `m`, `hub`): which candidate a task gets**, in `run::route_pick` (pure), applied by `build_run` (the one production build, `build_delivered` at `driver/build.rs:264`, its `build_run` call at `:307-317`) and to every task an edit adds:
      1. The task's explicit `route` in the plan wins. A route naming only `runtime` takes that runtime's first unskipped candidate, when the list has one.
      2. `pick = "first"`: the first unskipped candidate.
      3. `pick = "spread"`: the class's tasks with no explicit route are grouped into **overlap groups** (connected by `owns` intersection, so contract rule 22's "two runtimes never share overlapping `owns`" holds by construction, `contract.rs:70`), and the groups take the candidates round-robin in plan order of each group's first task. A task added later joins its overlap group's candidate, else takes the next in the rotation.
      4. The chosen candidate becomes the task's `route` (runtime, model, the roster strength, effort); validation then runs on it unchanged.
    - **Escalation (rung 2) follows the list.** A fresh rung-2 session takes the **next** unskipped candidate after the task's current one, cycling, skipping candidates identical to the current route; with a one-candidate list, `roster::escalate`. A candidate on the other runtime is skipped while any unfinished task on the first runtime overlaps this task's `owns`.
    - **Reviewers (`review`).** The first unskipped candidate that satisfies the reviewer rule (other runtime than the author, strength at or above the author's and the review level's); none qualifying falls back to `pick_reviewer`. The candidate's `effort` wins over the review level's when set. **Review tasks** (M9's `TaskKind::Review`, including engine-made `<e>-int<n>`) route by the `review` list's first unskipped candidate (RL-4).
    - **Roles (`scout`, `decider`, `planner`, `orchestrator`).** `pick = "first"` uses the first unskipped candidate; `"spread"` rotates per session in start order (per run for run scouts and sub-planners; per daemon for deciders). **Research tasks** (M9's `TaskKind::Research`) route by the `scout` list (RL-4). For deciders a list replaces `deciders.mode`'s runtime choice, but `mode = "off"` still turns them off. The resolution sites are `run/orch/launch.rs` (`resolve_orchestrator` `:46`, `scout_route_of` `:181`, `frozen_scout_route` `:186`, `planner_route` `:195`, `research_spec` `:311`, `review_task_spec` `:337`), `run/orch/roles.rs` (`RoleSnapshot`), `scout/spec.rs` (`ScoutRouting`) and `decider/call.rs`.
    - **The orchestrator** (RH-5, RL-3), highest first: an explicit per-run `OrchestratorChoice` (`proto/src/orch.rs:19`: the goal form's roster picker, `run promote --orchestrator`); a continued chain's existing route (9.3 decision 23 keeps it); the first unskipped candidate of `[orchestrator.routes.orchestrator]`; `[orchestrator.agent] runtime/model/effort`.
    - **Onboarding scouts and deciders** (RL-2, FU-F10; review ruling I6) route over what is installed. There is no daemon-level probe to read: `run/orch/installed.rs` is pure, and its I/O is `driver/build.rs::found(claude, codex) -> Found` (`:122`, private), which today only `RunService::make_planned` (`:221`) calls, on `spawn_blocking` and without a timeout. These sites reuse that I/O through one new function, `pub(crate) async fn found_within(claude: String, codex: String, timeout: Duration) -> Option<Found>` in `driver/build.rs` (`found` made `pub(crate)` beside it): `tokio::time::timeout(timeout, spawn_blocking(move || found(&claude, &codex)))`, `None` on the timeout or a panic. `INSTALLED_PROBE_TIMEOUT` is 5 s (it stats two paths; the bound only matters for a hung filesystem). It runs at two moments, never under the engine or manager lock and with no daemon-wide cache:
      - **the onboarding start**: `profile/service_start.rs::scout_checks` (`:31`; `scout_route(ctx)` at `:37`) probes, picks the scout's route over what it found, and keeps it on the onboarding `Job`, which `profile/service_run.rs::scout` (`:253`) spawns the scout with;
      - **each decider call**: `decider/call.rs::decide` (`:38`) probes before it takes `DeciderContext`'s route (built once at daemon start, `decider/mod.rs:247-252`, `DeciderMode::Claude` by default), and a `decider` list's pick runs over what it found.

      Their headless map (`Found.headless`, the map a run records) is what they read. A Codex-only user gets a Codex onboarding scout and Codex deciders; with nothing installed, or `found_within` returning `None`, both resolve exactly as today. `make_planned` keeps its own call unchanged.
    - **Precedence**, highest first: the plan's explicit route; the model list; an applied `route.<class>` in `tuning.toml` (decision 11); M8a's default. A class with a list gets **no** `route.<class>` proposal, and `run stats` shows its route as `list (config): codex/gpt-6.1-sol high, claude/claude-opus-5-5 medium`.
    - **Frozen at start.** `refit::tuned` copies the lists into `Tuned.lists`, and `build_run` into `RunLimits.route_lists`, so edits, rung-2 sessions and reviewers of a running run use the run's copy.
    - **Dispatch-time history** (M8b decision 33a's reserved values, `proto/src/history.rs:112-120`). Fill `RoutingDecision` for every task-bound session using a class or `review` list: the entire ordered, resolved list, the selected index, source `configured_list`, policy version `m9.5-list-v1`, `pick_policy` `first` or `spread` for class lists and `first_qualifying` for reviewer lists, and each skipped candidate's `skipped_reason` (`overlapping owns`, `below the author's strength`, `not installed`, `failed in this task`). An explicit task route records source `explicit_task` and is appended when absent; a fallback route is appended and records the policy that chose it. Rung-2 choices record trigger `escalation`. Racer lanes record trigger `race` with `lane: Some("a" | "b")` (the field is `Option<String>`, `history.rs:111`, and stays a string); test writers trigger `test_writer`. A later edit never changes an earlier decision.
    - **Role history.** M9's `RoleRoutingDecision` (`pick_policy` at `history.rs:154`) snapshots the configured list, its selected index, skips and, for `spread`, the rotation position. An unchosen candidate never gets a failure outcome.
    - **Hub stays hub.** A hub task still runs alone, is `tdd` and is reviewed; only its model and effort come from the list.
    - **The planner sees the lists.** `get_context` returns them under `limits.routes`, and both planner contracts gain `Model lists are set by the user; leave route empty to use them, or name one of the listed models.`
    - With no `[orchestrator.routes.*]` table, every output is byte-identical to today's, apart from the two skips, which apply with or without lists.
10. **The tuning file** (audit F51, F52). `<repo_dir>/tuning.toml`, next to `history.jsonl` (`repo_dir` is `profile::repo_dir(data_dir, project)`, `profile/mod.rs:47`), holds `proto::TuningFile`. Nothing is written into the repository.
    - **Who writes what.** `[budgets.*]` and `[weights]` only by the automatic refit; `[thresholds]` and `[routes.*]` only by `--apply`; `[dismissed]` only by `--dismiss`.
    - **Writes** go through `profile::store::write_atomic` (`store.rs:102`), on `spawn_blocking`, under a per-repository `tokio::sync::Mutex` in `RunService` (`TuningLocks`). The lock is never the manager or engine lock and is never held across a git call. A crashed write's `.tmp` is swept at daemon start (decision 36).
    - **Absent** file: every value is the default. **Unparseable**: renamed to `tuning.toml.bad-<unix secs>`, and the run log and `run stats` say `tuning: tuning.toml did not parse (<error>); it was moved to <path> and tuning starts again from history`. A broken tuning file never stops a run.
    - A run with history off (`!run::history::enabled(run)`, `history.rs:25-27`) reads and writes no tuning.
11. **Proposals in `run stats`** (ruling RH-7; audit F127). `run::refit::report` returns a `TuningReport`; `HistoryStats.tuning` carries it, and `refit_render::render` prints it after the flaky block that ends `stats::render` (`stats.rs:311-337`), under the heading `tuning proposals:` (9.1's flaky lines say `proposal:`; the two never share a heading). A proposal whose proposed value equals its `[dismissed]` entry is not listed.
    - `anthrex run stats --apply <id>…` fetches the stats, prints each named proposal and asks `apply <id>: <text>? [y/N]` unless `--yes`, then sends `RunRequest::Stats { apply }`. The daemon re-derives the proposals under the tuning lock and applies only ids still current. An unknown id refuses the whole request: `no current proposal <id>; run anthrex run stats to see the proposals`.
    - `--dismiss <id>…` records `dismissed[id] = <proposed value>` without asking.
    - Applying writes `[thresholds]` or `[routes.<class>]`, for runs started afterwards.
    - The daemon side is `driver/adapt_history.rs::stats` (`:94`, reached from `driver/requests.rs:144`), not `server/run_api.rs`.
12. **Frozen at start** (ruling RH-8; audit F53). `driver/tuning.rs::tune_for_start` turns the tuning file and config into a `refit::Tuned`, which `BuildContext` (`plan.rs:89-101`) carries in a new `tuning` field (`Tuned::default()` reproduces today exactly). It is called from `build_delivered` only (`driver/build.rs:307-317`), which covers a plan start, a goal start (`driver/adapt_goal.rs`) and 9.3's continued run (`driver/chain_goal.rs`, `StartGoal { continue_from }`): a continued run tunes fresh, because it is a new run. **Rounds (`RunRequest::Iterate`) never re-tune**: every round of a run uses the run's frozen values (KG §2.6). `build_run` copies `Tuned` into `RunLimits`: `budget_s`, `budget_m`, `budget_hub`, `budget_l` (decision 6), `class_routes`, `path_weights`, `thresholds`, `route_lists` and decision 16's settings. `resolve_task` (`validate.rs:69`) reads strength and effort by class from `limits.class_routes`, and the hub budget from `limits.budget_hub` (in `resolve_task_lenient` too). Edits, retries and rung-2 sessions use `run.limits`, never the file. Two line shapes, never mixed: the **refit-write line** `tuning: budget S 40 calls 15m → 55 calls 18m from 34 samples` (`refit`'s log, decision 6) is written only when a refit changes `tuning.toml`, to the daemon log and, at a start, to that run's log first; the **start lines** record what the run uses, one line each, at every start: `tuning: budget S 55 calls 18m from 34 samples`, `tuning: budget S 40 calls 15m configured (refit would be 55 calls 18m)`, `tuning: path weights S 550s, M 1650s (derived), hub 1650s (derived)`, `tuning: thresholds S 35, M 100 lines`, `tuning: route S standard/medium (applied)`. With nothing learned, `tuning: none (history has fewer than <min_samples> samples per class)`.
13. **Where thresholds reach** (audit F54). `decider::{TriageInput, SizeCheckInput}` gain `#[serde(default)] thresholds: SizeThresholds` (default 20 and 100). `decider::prompt::render` writes the numbers where the texts say `about 20 changed lines or fewer`, `about 100 changed lines or fewer` and `more than about 100 lines` (`prompt.rs:30,35`); with the defaults its output is byte-identical. The driver fills triage's from the repository's tuning; the engine fills the size check's from `run.limits.thresholds`. The planners:
    - **`get_context`** renders `limits.sizes.S` and `.M` from `run.limits.thresholds` in place of the `SIZE_S`/`SIZE_M` constants (`context.rs:23,25,93`); with the defaults byte-identical.
    - **Orchestrator rule 10 and planner rule 4** (`contract.rs:52`, `:108`) stay fixed constants; their two numbers are replaced once by a pointer to `get_context`. Exact rule 10: `10. Every task is S or M. S: one file, no interface change, a mechanical check exists, and about as many changed lines as get_context's limits.sizes gives for S. M: one to three files inside one module, a clear spec, a check exists, and about as many changed lines as limits.sizes gives for M. Anything larger is L, and L is never executed: split it.` Planner rule 4 gets the same two sentences in place of its S and M sentences. The exact copies pinned in `orch/contract_tests.rs:33,94` are updated.

### Estimates

14. **Run estimate and bound ratio, round-aware** (rulings RE-1, RE-2, RE-3; audit F55–F58). `run::estimate::estimate(run, now) -> Option<Estimate>`, computed by `run/snapshot.rs` into `RunInfo.estimate_left_secs` and `bound_ratio_permille` (`run_info.rs:387,389`; the TUI already draws them, `inspector/run.rs:184-186, 249-251`). `None` when `run.limits.path_weights` is `None`, before the current round is approved, and while the run is `delivering` with no task running. Over the **counted tasks** (the current round's tasks, plus earlier rounds' unfinished fix tasks; never cancelled ones), with `w(t)` = `schedule::weight(&run.limits, t)` and `done(t)` its active seconds so far (decision 5, plus `now − phase_since` in an active phase):
    - `remaining(t)` = 0 for `merged` or reported, else `max(w(t) − done(t), ceil(w(t) / 10))`.
    - `left = max(RCP, ceil(Σ_writers remaining / max_writers))`, where `RCP` is the longest path of `remaining` over unfinished counted tasks along declared and implicit dependencies, and `Σ_writers` sums only writer tasks: reader tasks (`schedule::is_reader_task`) count on paths but not in the work sum (RE-3).
    - `bound = max(CP, ceil(Σ_writers w / max_writers))`, `CP` the longest path of `w` over the counted tasks.
    - `elapsed` = `now − round_approved_at − paused_secs_since_approval`, less `now − paused_at` while paused or halted (decision 15), and, in `pr` mode, less the sum of the stages' `human_review_secs` (`StagePrInfo`, 9.2) since the round's approval (RE-2). `round_approved_at` is new: `Round.approved_at: Option<u64>` (`#[serde(default)]`, `model_goal_rounds.rs`), set when a round's gate is approved; round 1's is `Run.approved_at`.
    - `bound_ratio_permille = (elapsed + left) × 1000 / max(bound, 1)`.
15. **Pause accounting** (ruling RE-4; audit F59). `Run` gains `paused_at: Option<u64>`, `paused_secs: u64` and `last_step_at: u64` (`#[serde(default)]`). The reducer sets `last_step_at = now` whenever an event changes a run (the condition that bumps its revision), sets `paused_at` when a run enters `paused` or `halted`, and adds `now − paused_at` to `paused_secs` when it leaves. On `Restore`, a run turned from `running` into `paused` gets `paused_at = run.restored` when `Run.restored` (`model.rs:221`) is set, else `last_step_at`, so the downtime counts as paused, not work. Task clocks (`engine/clock.rs::sync`) are unchanged; `Run.paused_from` is unchanged.

### Adaptive concurrency

16. **Per-runtime writer caps inside a run** (rulings RC-1, RC-3, RR-9; audit F60–F64). `Run.concurrency: BTreeMap<String, RuntimeConcurrency { cap, last_rate_limit_at, last_change_at, halvings, recoveries }>`, keyed by the runtime label of `rate_limits`, `cap` starting at `max_writers`. With `limits.adaptive_concurrency` (config `adaptive_concurrency`, default true, frozen at start):
    - **The event** is exactly what increments `Run.rate_limits[runtime]`: one call to `engine/signals.rs::count_rate_limit(run, i, r)` (`:196`; callers `signals.rs:153`, `:344`, `review.rs:459`, `research.rs:282`), which calls `concurrency::on_rate_limit`. **Every task session counts**: worker, racer, test writer, reviewer and research (RC-1). Orchestrator, planner, run scout and decider sessions do not call `count_rate_limit` and are filed for later.
    - **Halving.** On an event, `last_rate_limit_at = now`. If `now − last_change_at >= halve_hold_secs` (default 60), or the cap never changed: `cap = max(1, cap / 2)`, `last_change_at = now`, `halvings += 1`. Otherwise nothing more.
    - **Recovery.** On `Tick`, for each runtime with `cap < max_writers`, if `now − max(last_rate_limit_at, last_change_at) >= recover_after_secs` (config `recover_after_mins`, default 10): `cap += 1`, `last_change_at = now`, `recoveries += 1`. One step per interval. A `Tick` that changes no cap changes nothing.
    - **Scheduling.** In `dispatch_launch::dispatch_writers` (`:90`), a task can take a writer slot when `writers_busy < max_writers` **and** `concurrency::writers_busy_on(run, runtime) < cap(run, runtime)`. Both count **lanes**: `schedule::writers_busy` (`:28`) counts a racing task as one per live lane (RR-9), and `RunInfo.writers_busy` and the TUI's `agents` row read it. A task blocked only by its runtime's cap is skipped. Hub-alone (`hub_holds_slot`) is unchanged.
    - **No preemption.** A lower cap stops nothing.
    - **Visibility.** Each change adds a run log line (`rate limit on codex: writers 3 → 1`, `no rate limit on codex for 10 min: writers 1 → 2`). While a cap is below `max_writers`, `attention` carries `codex writers capped at 1 of 3 after a rate limit`. It is an attention line only: no new Alerts kind (RC-3). `RunInfo.writer_caps` exposes the caps, and the report gets a `## Concurrency` section.
    - `adaptive_concurrency = false` keeps every cap at `max_writers`.

### The race

17. **Opt-in, and what is validated** (rulings RR-5, RR-6, RR-7, RR-8; audit F81–F85). `PlanTask` gains `#[serde(default)] race: bool`, and `PlanEdit::AmendTask` gains `#[serde(default)] race: Option<bool>`. Default off. `run::validate_patterns::validate`, called from `validate_graph::validate_tasks_with` (`:76`), adds, each exact:
    - `task <id>: race: only code tasks can race (rule 4.1.race)`;
    - `task <id>: race: a hub task cannot race, because a hub runs alone (rule 4.1.race)`;
    - `task <id>: race: the roster has no <peer runtime> model at strength <strength> for the second racer (rule 4.1.race)`, the second racer being `roster::first_at(roster, peer(route.runtime), route.strength)` with the installed and model-list skips of decision 9a;
    - `task <id>: pair: a task cannot both race and pair (rule 4.1)`.

    **Amends** (`run/edits_amend.rs`). Changing `race` or `pair` on a task that **has started** is refused in the existing shape: `task <id> has started: its race cannot change` (or `its pair`). "Started" is the stage rule's predicate (review ruling I9; `edits_amend.rs:72-83`, ruling C-14(d)): the task is not `edits_state::not_started` (`Pending | Queued | Blocked`), **or** it is blocked with a `start_commit` (it was dispatched and has a checkout). So a race or pair can change only on a task that was never dispatched, which is what decision 18 needs: the race is decided at dispatch. The order is 9.3's earlier-round refusal (`validate_rounds`) first, then this one (RR-7). A `race` or `pair` set by the orchestrator mid-run, by `add_task` or `amend_task`, goes through M9's gate holds (`Task.orch.gate_hold`) like any orchestrator task change (RR-6). `reach::edits_may_widen` (`reach.rs:26`) counts an amend that sets `race` or `pair` as widening, and `reach::reachable_runtimes` (`:37`) adds lane b's runtime and the test writer's peer runtime, so the project-trust check (M9 decisions 50 and 53) covers them.
18. **Dispatch** (audit F65, F66). A task with `spec.race` races only when, at the moment the scheduler would dispatch it:
    - it is on the critical path (`schedule::critical_path(run).contains(&i)`), else it runs as a single worker with the note `race skipped: not on the critical path at dispatch`;
    - `max_writers >= 2`, else `race skipped: max_writers is 1`.

    It needs **two** writer slots, one on its runtime and one on the peer runtime, each within decision 16's caps. When it is the first runnable task in dispatch order and only one slot is free, **nothing after it dispatches** in that step. `Task.race_wait_since` records when it first waited; after `race_slot_wait_secs` (default 120) it starts as a single worker with `race skipped: no second writer slot within <n> s`. A race task is never pre-warmed (`dispatch_launch::prewarm`): its checkouts are the lanes'.
19. **Lanes are standalone checkouts** (ruling RR-1, RR-4; audit F67–F70). A race is `Task.race = Some(Race { lanes: [a, b], winner: None, adopted: false, .. })`.
    - **Lane a** runs on the task's route; **lane b** on the peer runtime at the same strength and effort.
    - **Each lane is its own standalone task checkout**, named `<task>.a` / `<task>.b`: checkout `<wt>/runs/<run>/<task>.<lane>`, repository `<data>/runs/<run>/tasks/<task>.<lane>/` with its own `git/objects`, `engine/` dir and `TMPDIR`, prepared by `PrepareWorktree` from `Run::head_for(task)` (`model.rs:440`, the stage head) with `setup`. Every path helper keyed by a task id (`task_repo_dir`, `task_objects_dir`, `task_engine_dir`, `task_tmp_dir`, `worker_git_roots`; and the checkout path `Run::task_path`) is called with the lane's checkout name. The checkout's own branch in the user's repository is `anthrex/<run>/<task>.<lane>`, which the import's compare-and-swap moves (`git/import.rs::sync_in`). The task branch `anthrex/<run>/<task>` does not exist until a lane wins.
    - **`Task::checkout_name()`** (new, `model_task.rs`) returns the task id, or, once crowned or adopted, `<task>.<lane>`. Every site that derives the task's checkout from `task.id()` (the path helpers' callers, `worker_spec`'s `output_filter` log dir, proof and review checkouts, cleanup, salvage, the reconcile rows) goes through it, so after the crown the task is an ordinary task whose checkout is the lane's.
    - Lane proof and review checkouts are `<task>.<lane>.proof` and `<task>.<lane>.review`; M8a decision 16 keeps `.` out of task ids, so none can collide.
    - Each lane's racer is a headless session with role `AgentRole::Racer`, the next task session number, and `lane` set on its `AgentRound`, `RunRef` and `McpTarget`. Window `<h4>/<task>.<lane>w<session>` (the task pattern `"{}/{}.w{}"`, `dispatch_launch.rs:239`), reviewers `<h4>/<task>.<lane>r<round>`. `max_windows` (per round since 9.3 decision 14) counts both lanes and their reviewers.
    - A racer gets exactly a worker's contract, tools (`task_done`, `task_blocked`, `task_note`; `mcp/src/tools.rs:20`), sandbox and prompt (`worker_prompt(run, task, extract, notes)` with its lane's checkout). It is not told it is racing.
    - **Messages** (RR-4). `message`, `refresh` and an `amend_task` brief go to every live lane. `stop_and_wait` (`MessagePause`) on a racing task is refused on the request path (review ruling I8): `edits_orch::takes` (`edits_orch.rs:69`), which the MCP `message` tool and `anthrex run message` both reach, returns `task <id> is racing: it cannot stop and wait; message it or cancel it`. The action menu is not changed: `engine/actions/rules.rs::message` (`:292-299`) models `info` and `change` only, and the TUI picks the kind inside the message form (`app/action_forms.rs:131,139`), so a `stop_and_wait` sent from the form gets the same refusal as its reply. A lane's `task_note` is recorded with its lane: `WorkerNote` (`orch/mod.rs:243`) gains `#[serde(default)] lane: Option<RaceLane>`, and the digest's note entry shows `lane a` / `lane b` when set.
    - Both lanes' spend adds to the task's `spent_total`.
20. **Lane gates and the ladder** (ruling RR-3; audit F71, F72). Each lane runs the full pre-merge gates on its own: done; proof; tier 1 scratch (or the check on an untiered profile); the 9.1 test-weakening signals; review. All of it goes through the test slots (`run/slots/`), so racing doubles slot use. Each lane has its own `failures`, `bounces`, `stalls` and `budget_exceeded`, and its own reviewer (`pick_reviewer` on the lane's route). Every op and record of a lane carries `lane: Some(..)` (`PendingOp`, `CheckRecord`, `ProofRecord`, `ReviewRecord`), and an `OpDone` is routed to its lane. Tier 2 (the candidate) runs only for the crowned task in the merge queue.
    - A lane's **first gate failure** is rung 1: the failure text goes to that lane's session.
    - Anything that would take a single worker **past rung 1** takes the lane out of the race: a second gate failure, a stall, a hard budget breach, a spill outside `owns`, `task_blocked` of any kind, or an environment block.
      - While the **other lane is live**, the lane is **eliminated**: `LaneState::Out` with the reason; its sessions are stopped and its checkout salvaged (decision 22).
      - When the **other lane is already out**, the lane is **adopted** (decision 21's crown with `adopt: true` and `lane_head` = the lane's `head`, its last imported head, else its `start_commit`, the stage head its checkout was prepared from; the task branch is created there and the lane's next import moves it on, since the crown re-pins; a lane with neither, whose checkout was never prepared, is not crowned: the task goes `blocked(environment)` with the lane's reason), and the ladder applies to the task the action that took it out, with the task's counters set from the adopted lane: a second failure is rung 2 (a fresh session in the adopted checkout), a question is `blocked(question)`, a spill is rung 3.
21. **Winning** (ruling RR-1; audit F73). A lane **passes** when its last pre-merge gate succeeds: its review approves (or has only minor findings), or its tier 1 / check passes when the task is not reviewed.
    - **One winner.** The reducer handles events one at a time, so exactly one lane passes first. It becomes `Won`, and the reducer emits `Op CrownRacer { adopt: false }`. Anything the other lane does afterwards is recorded and never crowns; its state is `Lost`, and a tool call gets `the race for task <id> is over: racer <lane> won. Stop now.`
    - **The crown op** (`git/crown.rs::crown`). The lane's head has already been imported at its gates (its own branch is at it). The crown is one compare-and-swap creating the task branch at that head: `git update-ref refs/heads/anthrex/<run>/<task> <lane head> <zero oid>`, where the zero oid is `0` repeated for the repository's object format (`git rev-parse --show-object-format`: 40 for sha1, 64 for sha256). **There is no checkout step**: lane checkouts are detached and have no refs. A task branch already at the lane head counts as done (`Crowned`); one at another commit gives `RefMoved` and halts the run, as for any engine ref. `update-ref` cannot tell the two apart: a create over an existing ref fails with exit 128 and `cannot lock ref '<ref>': reference already exists` whether the ref is at the lane head or elsewhere (M9.5.1 item 8). So on a failed create `crown` reads the ref (`read_ref`): at the lane head → `Crowned`, at any other commit → `RefMoved`, absent (a failure of another kind) → the error (ruling T1-2).
    - **The checkout's own branch.** A checkout's pin (`worktree::pinned::pin`, `pinned.rs:96`) holds its own branch (`Pin.own`, given by the caller's `PinAs` when the checkout is prepared; "never recomputed while the daemon runs" applies to a linked worktree's git directory only: every `pin` call replaces `own` with its caller's, M9.5.1 item 9), and the import's compare-and-swap moves exactly that branch (`git/import.rs::sync_in`). So the `CrownRacer` executor (`driver/lane_ops.rs::crown_racer`, new, reached from `driver/ops.rs::run`'s match), right after `git::crown` returns `Crowned` (a fresh compare-and-swap or a branch already at the head), calls `worktree::pinned::repin_own(&checkout, "refs/heads/anthrex/<run>/<task>")` with the op's `checkout` (the lane checkout's path, `Run::task_path` of its checkout name; review ruling I2): a new function, in memory only, no git write; on `RefMoved` or an error it re-pins nothing; after a restart the pin must come from the task's `branch`, which the crown sets to the task branch (task M9.5.1 item 9 finds where a restart takes `PinAs.own` from). From then on every import moves the task branch, and the lane branch stays where the crown found it until accept or discard.
    - **Then** `Task.branch` is the task branch, `checkout_name()` is the lane's, and the task goes to the merge queue with the winner's head.
22. **Stopping and salvaging the loser** (ruling RR-2, RR-1; audit F74–F77). The same happens to an eliminated lane.
    - **Stop.** `KillWindow` for the lane's live racer and reviewer, through the existing kill path, unchanged (`manager/headless_turns.rs::headless_kill`: `SIGTERM` to the session's group, `SIGKILL` after the kill grace unless the leader was reaped first). No new kill code, signal or probe. The lane's proof and review checkouts are removed as today.
    - **Wait before removing.** The lane's `RemoveWorktree` is emitted only when every pending op on the lane has returned and the racer's `ProcessExited { killed_by_engine: true }` has arrived (or its session had already ended). If that exit has not arrived `LANE_EXIT_WAIT_SECS` (kill grace + 30 s) after the `KillWindow`, the checkout is **kept**: the run log says `kept <lane> checkout: its racer did not exit`, salvage still runs, through `RemoveWorktree { keep_head: true, keep_path: true, clear_locks: false }`, which salvages the checkout and leaves it (and every lock in it) in place, and the checkout goes with the run's other checkouts at accept or discard.
    - **What the removal does.** `RemoveWorktree` gains `#[serde(default)] keep_head: bool`, `#[serde(default)] clear_locks: bool` and `#[serde(default)] keep_path: bool` (salvage only):
      - with `clear_locks`, `git::clear_stale_locks(lane_git_dir)` first removes `index.lock` and `HEAD.lock` in the lane checkout's own git directory (`tasks/<task>.<lane>/git/`), the only locks a worker can leave there; there is no ref lock in the user's repository, because a worker writes no ref there. Each removal is recorded `removed a stale <file> left by the stopped racer`.
      - then the existing salvage runs (`salvage.rs:30`, which already uses the engine's own index copy and re-detaches a tampered checkout). With `keep_head: true`, a **clean** checkout also gets its salvage ref, at its head: without it the loser's commits would live only on `anthrex/<run>/<task>.<lane>`, which accept and discard delete with every run branch (`delete_branches`, `salvage.rs:281`; in `pr` mode a run ends by discard, so the same holds). The ref is `refs/anthrex/salvage/<run>/<task>/<seq>` with the task's own `<seq>`, recorded as the lane's `salvage_ref` and in the report.
    - **The lane's branch** is kept until accept or discard, like every run branch.
23. **After the race** (ruling RR-9, RR-4; audit F78–F80). A crowned or adopted task is an ordinary task in the lane's checkout: hand-back on conflict merges the stage head into it and resumes the winning lane's session, and rung 1 after a red candidate goes to that session. There is no second notion of "the task's writer": `ladder::worker_round` (`ladder.rs:21`) is extended to return the last round whose role is `Worker` or `TestWriter`, or `Racer` with its lane `Won` or `Adopted`, and every existing worker check uses it (`edits_state::has_live_worker` `:48`, `clock.rs`, `signals.rs`, `worker_messages.rs`, tool authorisation, stall, budget, fresh sessions; the 69 `AgentRole::Worker` references in 36 non-test files are audited in task M9.5.17a). Other operations:
    - **Cancel** kills both lanes and salvages both.
    - **Pause** lets open turns end. **Restart** resumes each live lane round (lanes are rounds).
    - **`run retry`, `run override`** apply only after adoption, when the task can be `blocked`: their action predicates in `engine/actions/rules.rs` refuse a racing task with `task <id> is racing: it has nothing to retry until one racer is left` (retry) and `task <id> is racing: there is no failed gate to override` (override), shown muted in the action menu.

    A racing task's `TaskState` is `Working` from dispatch until its crown. Each live lane holds a writer slot from `Preparing` through `Check` (decision 16).

### The test writer, then the implementer

24. **Opt-in, and what is validated** (audit F87). `PlanTask` gains `#[serde(default)] pair: bool`, and `AmendTask` gains `pair: Option<bool>`. Validation allows `pair` on any code task that resolves to `tdd` (a hub included); the orchestrator contract recommends it for hub tasks (decision 32). Exact:
    - `task <id>: pair: a paired task needs test mode tdd (rule 4.1.pair)`, checked after test-mode resolution (`validate.rs:181-186`, rule 8.3), so a `tdd` task demoted by a missing `single_test` fails here;
    - `task <id>: pair: name the test the test writer writes in test_to_write (rule 4.1.pair)`.

    Amends, gate holds and widening as decision 17.
25. **The test writer** (ruling RP-1; audit F88). At dispatch, a paired task gets `Task.pair = Some(Pair { phase: Writing, .. })` and, in its normal task checkout, a headless session with role `AgentRole::TestWriter`, window `<h4>/<task>.t<session>`.
    - **Route.** The peer runtime at the same strength and effort when the roster has one, after decision 9a's skips, else the task's route.
    - **Contract and prompt.** `TEST_WRITER_CONTRACT` and `test_writer_prompt` (Interfaces). Tools, sandbox and budget are a worker's.
    - **Its `task_done`** goes through `VerifyDone` as a worker's does, with `test` and `red` required and `red == head`. Otherwise: `task_done rejected: you are the test writer, so red must be your last commit (HEAD is <head7>, red is <red7>). Commit only the failing test, then call task_done again.`
    - **The red check** is a red-only mode of the existing proof: `OpKind::Proof` (`engine/ops.rs:127`) gains `#[serde(default)] red_only: bool`, and `run_proof_scheduled` (`proof.rs:144`) with `red_only` runs only the red run, through the test slots, confinement and the step's `TMPDIR`, in the task's proof checkout (`ProofOp.repo` = `tasks/<checkout>.proof`). `OpResult::Proof` already reports `red_failed`. There is no new runner.
      - The test passes at red: a gate failure of gate `proof` on the ladder, with `red_check_failed_message`. Rung 1 returns it to the same test writer, rung 2 starts a fresh test writer, rung 3 raises the size.
      - It fails, as it should: `pair.test` and `pair.red` are recorded, `red_checked = Some(true)`, the test writer's session is retired, and the run log gets `pair t2: red a1b2c3d fails as it should; implementer started`.
26. **The implementer** (ruling RP-2; audit F89). In the same checkout, after the test writer: a headless session with role `Worker`, the next session number, the task's own route, prompt `worker_prompt` with `pair_implementer_note` before the brief. The test writer's counters move to `pair.writer_failures` and the task's are reset to 0.
    - **Its `task_done`.** `test` and `red` are optional and filled from `pair`; when given they must match: `task_done rejected: this task's test was written by the test writer; name test <test> and red <red7>, or leave both out`.
    - **Its gates** are the full ones (proof, tier 1 / check, weakening signals, review, merge). Its **test-weakening signals are diffed from `red`**, not from the stage head (`engine/weakening.rs`), so a test the test writer committed and the implementer weakened raises a W-signal, numbered in the reviewer prompt and requiring an answer as 9.1 does.
    - **The reviewer prompt** of a paired task adds the one-sentence `pair_reviewer_note` pointing at those signals.
    - **Retry and restart.** `run retry` in `Writing` starts a fresh test writer; in `Implementing`, a fresh implementer. Rung 2 starts a fresh session of the phase's role. Restart resumes the live session.
    - **Slots.** One writer slot throughout.
27. **Reconcile** (audit F91, F116). In `run/reconcile/git.rs`: `CrownRacer` checks the task branch only (task branch at the lane head → `Crowned`; absent → `NotStarted`; at another commit → `RefMoved`). The red-only proof is a `Proof` and keeps its row. `RemoveWorktree` with `keep_head` and the path gone and its salvage ref present replays `Removed`.

### The output filter for Codex workers

28. **Codex gets the filter** (audit F92–F95). `worker_spec` sets `HeadlessSpec.output_filter` for Claude only (`role_launch.rs:244`, `.filter(|_| claude)`). M9.5 sets it for Codex worker-like sessions too (workers, racers, test writers), under the same conditions. The filter log is already inside a Codex worker's writable roots: it is `task_tmp_dir(data_dir, checkout).join(LOG_DIR_NAME)` (`anthrex-logs`), and `task_tmp_dir` is one of `worker_git_roots` (`role_launch.rs:85-90`); nothing is added to the roots. How the filter reaches Codex is `CLI_CAPS.codex_filter`:
    - **`CodexFilter::Hook`**, only when task M9.5.1's facts are recorded (from a user-run probe; agents never run real Codex): `codex exec` and `codex exec resume`, with the flags `codex_user_config_only` adds, run a `PreToolUse` hook given with `-c` and trusted through `hooks.state`, and the hook can replace a shell call's command. `codex_args` then adds `-c hooks.PreToolUse=[{hooks=[{type="command",command=<filter-hook command>}]}]` and `-c hooks.state={"<trust_key(source, "pre_tool_use", 0, 0)>"={trusted_hash="<trust_hash("pre_tool_use", command)>"}}`, where `source` is `default_hook_source()`'s value (it returns `Option<String>`; `None` means the hook cannot be trusted and the filter falls back to `Instruction`). It never adds `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`. `anthrex filter-hook` gains `--runtime codex` (parsed in `filter_hook.rs::parse`, before clap), printing the replacement in the recorded shape (`output_filter::codex_rewrite`).
    - **`CodexFilter::Instruction`**, the default until the facts are recorded: `developer_instructions` gets `codex_filter_note` after the contract, and the report says `codex output filter: by instruction only (the CLI cannot rewrite commands)`.

### The view, the report and the history

29. **The run view draws racers and test writers** (audit F96–F98, F129–F131). Exact labels, glyphs and inspector rows are in Interfaces "Run view".
    - `tree::display_rounds` splits `Racer` and `TestWriter` rounds at `sent_back_at` as it splits workers. It is in `tree/run_rows.rs` (`:45-115`, with `rounds_with` and `role_rank`), not `tree/round_rows.rs` (9.3's goal-round grouping); task M9.5.3 moves the three, move-only, to a new `tui/src/tree/task_rounds.rs`, where the splitting and ordering below land (review ruling I1).
    - Rounds of one task are ordered by start time, ties: test writer, worker, racer a, racer b, then reviewers, lane a's before lane b's.
    - Keys stay unique: a lane's reviewer rounds carry the lane racer's `session`, and the two racers have different session numbers.
    - Glyphs and colours come from 9.0.7's kit (`theme::Glyph` with its ASCII twin, `theme::Role`), never raw characters or modifiers.
    - The task inspector's DETAIL section gains `race` and `pair` after `route` in `DETAIL_ROWS` (`run_task_sections.rs:183`); the round inspector (`inspector/run_round.rs`) gains `race` (racer) and `test` (test writer) fields after `doing`. The run inspector's `agents` row gains each capped runtime's writers.
30. **Status, report and history** (audit F99–F101).
    - **`anthrex run status`** (`cli/src/run_cmd/status.rs`): under a racing task `    race: a claude <lane state> · b codex <lane state>`; under a paired task `    pair: test writer <done, red <red7> | working>; implementer <state>`; the header's `writers 2/3` (`status.rs:50`) gains ` (codex cap 1)` per capped runtime.
    - **`REPORT.md`.** Per task, in `report_task.rs`: `race` and `pair` lines (each lane's route, state, reason, salvage ref and cleared locks; the red commit and the test writer's failures). For the run, in `report.rs`: `## Concurrency` and decision 12's `tuning:` lines.
    - **`TaskRecord`** gains `#[serde(default)]` `pattern: Option<TaskPattern>`, `race_winner: Option<RaceLane>`, `race_adopted: bool`, `writer_failures: u8` and `round: u32` (decision 4), filled by `run/history.rs::task_record`. `HISTORY_VERSION` stays 5: only `#[serde(default)]` fields on `task` lines.

### Test doubles

31. **`fake-agent` and recorded history** (audit F102–F104).
    - `fake-agent`'s role scripts (`roles.rs`, in `<git common dir>/fake-agent/`) gain two roles, read from the MCP server's `--role`: `racer`, script `racer-<task>-<lane>-<n>.jsonl` with the lane from `--lane` (read where `--epic` and `--scout` are, `roles.rs:130-132`); and `test_writer`, script `test_writer-<task>-<n>.jsonl`. Existing steps (`McpCall`, `ReadMessage`, `Sh`, `Capture`, `ApiRetry`, `FailTurn`, `Hang`, …) are enough.
