    - `fake-agent`'s PTY mode, given an MCP server in its arguments and no prompt after `--`, runs `initialize` and `tools/list` once against the server at start (as the real CLIs do), then runs the configured `SessionStart` hook (so the window has a signal, decision 38's gate), then waits for its first message on stdin, which becomes `FAKE_AGENT_MESSAGE`; scripts are unchanged.
    - Refit tests read committed history files under `daemon/tests/fixtures/history/` (new). Each is generated by a rule in its task, and a test regenerates it and compares byte for byte.
    - Every reducer test injects `now` through `Event`.
32. **What the planners are told** (audit F105–F107).
    - `mcp/src/tools_orch.rs`: `plan_task` (`:220`) gains `race` and `pair` booleans; `plan_edit` (`:183`) gains them for `amend_task`. The exact-schema pins in `mcp/src/tools_orch_tests.rs` (`:52`, `:356`) and `mcp/src/fixtures/orch_tools.json` are updated.
    - `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT` rule 20 (`contract.rs:66`) gets, appended: ` A hub task can set pair to true: a separate test writer commits its failing test first, and a different worker makes it pass.` Rule 22 (`:70`) gets: ` A task on the critical path that is not a hub can set race to true: two workers on different runtimes build it at once, and the first to pass every gate wins. It costs twice the tokens and twice the test slots, so use it only where finishing sooner matters.` `PLANNER_CONTRACT` is unchanged apart from decision 13.
    - The `run_status` digest's task entries (`orch/digest.rs`) gain `"race"` (`"a claude working · b codex review"`, `"won by b"`, or `null`) and `"pair"` (`"writing the test"`, `"implementing, red a1b2c3d"`, or `null`); both take part in the fingerprint, and `orch/digest_trim.rs` drops both keys with the rest of a trimmed task. `task_result`'s `task` (`orch/result.rs`) gains `race` and `pair`.

### Folded follow-ups: history and stats correctness (X3)

33. **A revert of a revert** (FU-F2). `history_io::detect_reverts` (`:299-355`) also treats every recorded revert commit as a candidate: a commit whose message says `This reverts commit <sha>` of a recorded `revert_commit` is recorded as a `RevertRecord` too, with `reverted` = that earlier revert commit and the same `run_id` and `task_id`. **Effective reverts** (`history_io::effective_reverts(lines) -> Vec<&RevertRecord>`, pure): a revert record is in effect unless a revert record in effect names its `revert_commit` as `reverted`, evaluated from the newest; so a revert of a revert reinstates the task, and a third revert reverts it again. `run/stats.rs` and `refit.rs` count only effective reverts. No new history line type.
34. **A later round that never completes ends its round** (FU-F37). `goal_rounds_end::pass` (`:168-175`) also ends an open round ≥ 2 when its run becomes terminal (`Discarded` or `Failed`) without completing, with outcome `cancelled` (no new `RoundOutcome`), before the run's own history line, so `run stats`' rounds count has every round.
35. **A quarantined test is not proposed again** (FU-F17). `history_io::summarise` (`:398`) takes the repository's stored profile's `slow_tests` (`Option<String>`, `proto/src/profile.rs:91`), and `stats::flaky_proposals` (`:180`) drops a proposed test whose name occurs in it as a whole token (split on whitespace, `|`, `,`, `(`, `)` and quotes). Matching the runner's filter semantics (nextest `-E`, `--skip`) is not attempted; a test named exactly in `slow_tests`, as the proposal's own `add <t> to slow_tests` text puts it, is never proposed again.
36. **Leftover temporary files are swept** (FU-F15). `profile::store::sweep_leftovers` (`store.rs:124-150`) also removes the `.tmp` leftovers of `test-cache.jsonl`, `module-graph.json` and `tuning.toml` (each `<file>.<pid>.<n>.tmp`), at the same daemon start.
37. **Orchestrator usage across a chain** (FU-F40, F43). An adopted orchestrator session keeps its launch run's OTLP attributes (`engine/chains.rs:100` copies `otlp_token`), so its usage is posted under the chain's first run.
    - `Metered::refresh_live` (`driver/usage.rs:40-59`) maps each run with `continued_by` set to its chain's current run (following `continued_by` to the last run): such a run is not live itself (FU-F40: a delivered previous run is no longer credited), but `is_live`, `token` and `post` for its id resolve to the current run.
    - The current run credits only what the session spent since it adopted the window: `chains::adopt` records `usage_at_adopt` on the new run's orchestrator record (`#[serde(default)]`), the session's counter at the adoption (the previous run's credited usage less its `orchestrator_base`, plus its own `usage_at_adopt`); `EventKind::OrchestratorUsage` (`engine/mod.rs:402-413`) subtracts it per field, saturating at 0 (`TokenUsage::saturating_sub`, new). A fresh session is metered as today.

### Folded follow-ups: orchestrator first-turn reliability (X1)

38. **A Claude orchestrator's first turn waits for the anthrex MCP server** (FU-F12). Today the first prompt is passed as argv at launch (`orch/launch.rs:159`, `initial_prompt: Some(first_prompt)`), so Claude reads it whether or not `anthrex mcp` has connected, and in the user's first live run the first turn saw no `edit_plan`. **Claude only** (ruling T5a-2): Claude runs no hook before its workspace trust prompt is answered, and FU-F12 was Claude's. A Codex orchestrator keeps 9.3's launch: its first prompt is on its command line (`initial_prompt`), `first_turn_pending` is never set for it, nothing is pasted, and its restarts, fresh or resumed, are 9.3's (`orch/launch.rs::first_turn_pasted`). Everything below is about Claude.
    - **The readiness signal.** The orchestrator's `anthrex mcp` (role `orchestrator`, `--run`, `--window`) answers its first `tools/list` (`mcp/src/lib.rs`'s `list_tools`) and then sends `RunRequest::McpReady { run_id, window_id }` once over the daemon socket (a fresh connection, like `forward`; any failure ignored). The driver checks it comes from the run's orchestrator window and sends `OrchEvent::McpReady`; the engine sets `RunOrch.mcp_ready` (`#[serde(skip)]`, reset at every launch and relaunch).
    - **The first turn** (review ruling C1). A fresh orchestrator launch or relaunch (`engine/orch_window.rs::launch`, `relaunch`) passes no `initial_prompt`. The prompt itself stays where it is today, `OrchestratorRecord.first_prompt` (`orch/mod.rs:148`; set by `launch`, `orch_window.rs:22-41`, and copied by `chains::adopt`); the record gains `#[serde(default)] first_turn_pending: bool`, set by the launch and cleared by the `OrchestratorWoken` for the first turn. There is no second copy of the text. Once `mcp_ready` is set, the engine emits the first turn through 9.3's whole-paste path (`Effect::WakeOrchestrator` with `#[serde(default)] first_turn: true`, held until `OrchestratorWoken` for it), and the driver's gate (`driver/wake.rs::ready`) delivers it only when **both** hold: the engine's `mcp_ready`, and the window's `WindowInfo.signals_seen` (it has sent at least one hook or title signal), besides `ready`'s existing conditions (`Idle` or `Done`, no open attention, no input for the quiet time). **A window that has sent no signal is never pasted into**, whatever `mcp_ready` says: a quiet unsignalled window is `Idle` (`status.rs:92`) and is exactly a start or folder-trust prompt, which raises no `Attention`, so the paste's `\r` would answer it. Real Claude's order (trust prompt, MCP connect, `tools/list`) is not known (M9.5.1 item 7), so the gate does not rely on it.
    - **The bound.** `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS` (60) after the launch or relaunch that set `first_turn_pending` (`RunOrch.first_turn_since`, `#[serde(skip)]`), with the first turn still pending, **nothing is pasted**: on the next `Tick` the engine sets `RunOrch.first_turn_late` (`#[serde(skip)]`), the snapshot's attention shows decision 39's `START_PROMPT` line, the run log says `first turn still waiting 60 s after launch: no MCP ready notice yet` (or `: the window is not ready yet`, when `mcp_ready` is set and the driver holds the paste), and the engine keeps waiting. The paste happens once both `mcp_ready` and `signals_seen` have arrived; its `OrchestratorWoken` clears `first_turn_pending` and `first_turn_late`, and with them the attention line.
    - **A restart.** `mcp_ready` and `first_turn_since` are `#[serde(skip)]` and reset at every launch and relaunch. After a daemon restart the orchestrator's window is dormant (`orch_window.rs::restored`), and `run resume` relaunches it as a new session with a new `anthrex mcp`, which sends its own `McpReady` on its first `tools/list`; so a restart never leaves the first turn waiting on a notice that cannot come. A relaunch with `first_turn_pending` still set delivers `first_prompt` through the same gate, before any wake-up note.
    - **The contract.** `orchestrator_first_prompt` (`contract.rs:246`) ends `Start with get_context, then scout, then plan. If an anthrex tool is reported missing, call get_context again before anything else: the server may still be connecting.` and the contract's rule 4 (`4. Call get_context (in Claude: mcp__anthrex__get_context) first: …`, `orch/contract.rs:44`) gains the same retry sentence as its last sentence (ruling T1-1: the contract has no rule about tools as such; rule 4 is where it first names `get_context`).
    - A chain's adopted window (9.3) and a promoted run's first prompt (`promoted_first_prompt`) already reach a live session through `request_wake`; they are unchanged.
39. **A start prompt is an attention line** (FU-F13). The TUI alerts `orchestrator waits at a start prompt` (`app/alerts.rs:109-110`), but `run status`, the run inspector and the `C-b T` header say nothing. Following `report_held` (`driver/wake.rs`), the driver's `check_orchestrators` reports `OrchEvent::StartPrompt { run_id, waiting }` on each change of the TUI's predicate for the run's live orchestrator window (`Claude` or `Codex`, `!signals_seen`, status `Idle`, `Done` or `Attention`) held for the run's `wake_quiet_secs`. The engine keeps `RunOrch.start_prompt` (`#[serde(skip)]`), and the snapshot's attention (`snapshot.rs:240-256`) adds `START_PROMPT` (once) while `start_prompt` or decision 38's `first_turn_late` is set: `orchestrator waits at a start prompt; focus its window (C-b T, Enter) to answer it`. The manual check verifies it against real Claude (9.0.5's outstanding item).
40. **A Codex orchestrator's own question raises `Attention`** (FU-F27). Two paths, chosen by task M9.5.1:
    - **Off-switch.** If the user's probe (M9.5.1 item 6) records a Codex config key that turns off its user-question tool, `launch/role.rs::codex_role_args` (`:116-142`) adds it for the orchestrator, and the orchestrator asks through anthrex.
    - **Footer detection**, otherwise (review ruling I11). Not on the quiet tick: `Quiet` is applied only to a `Working` PTY window (`manager/mod.rs:344-349`), and a Codex orchestrator waiting on its question need not be `Working`. Instead, `WindowManager::tick` (`manager/mod.rs:329`, once a second, under the manager lock as today) checks, for every entry with `role.is_some()` (the orchestrator, `manager/entry.rs:121`) whose runtime is `Codex` and whose process is a PTY, **whatever its status**, the screen's last three non-empty rows (`Window::screen_text`, `window.rs:370`: the window's own parser lock, a copy of the screen's text, no I/O, so nothing blocks) against `codex_question_footer` (pure, new `daemon/src/status_codex.rs`). The entry keeps the last result (`Entry.codex_question: bool`, not persisted), and only a change from no match to a match applies the event, so input that clears the attention is not undone by the next tick while the same footer stays on screen; the footer leaving and coming back raises it again. The pattern is `? <n> question` (`\?\s+\d+\s+questions?\b`), as the user's try-out showed (`Queued follow-up inputs · ? 1 question · shift+← to answer`, follow-ups file, "From milestone 9.0.7"). A new match is `StatusEvent::CodexQuestion` → `Attention` (`status.rs::next`, from any status but `Exited`), which `app/alerts.rs::orchestrator_text` already alerts as `orchestrator asks for permission`; input clears it as for any attention. Tested on a captured screen fixture (`daemon/tests/fixtures/screens/codex-question-footer.txt`, written from the quoted text and marked hand-written until a real capture replaces it).
41. **Why a wake-up waits is logged** (FU-F26). `driver/wake.rs::check_orchestrators` logs at `info`, once per pending wake-up and change of reason, why it waits (`window working`, `window starting`, `input <n>s ago`, `attention open`, `no hook signal yet`; the last only for a first turn, decision 38: a missing MCP ready notice holds the first turn in the engine, which says so in its own line), and the delivery itself (`wake-up delivered to window <n> (<bytes> bytes)`). `ready` (`:336-345`) returns the reason (`Result<(), WaitReason>`) instead of a bool.
42. **An alert count under the plan review** (FU-F11). The status bar draws `⚑ <n>` (the top alert's glyph and role, the count, no key) whenever the plan review is open, whatever the sidebar; today it draws it only with the sidebar hidden (`statusbar.rs:79-100`, pinned by `statusbar_modal_tests.rs:137`).

### Folded follow-ups: small fixes riding protocol 16 (X4)

43. **A failed turn in the snapshot** (FU-F23). `AgentRoundInfo` gains `#[serde(default)] failed_error: Option<String>` and `failed_until: Option<u64>`, filled by `run/snapshot.rs` from `AgentRound.failed_error` (`model_rounds.rs:98`) and `FailedTurn::WaitingContinue { at, rate_limit: false }` (`:34`) as `at + rate_limit_retry_secs`. The TUI draws such a live round like a rate-limited one (`graph/paint/style.rs::live`'s `Glyph::Blocked` in `Role::Paused`), and the round inspector's status reads `failed: <error> · retries <hh:mm>`, the error one line, cut to 80 characters.
44. **A placeholder headless window can be closed from the TUI** (FU-F7). `WindowInfo` gains `#[serde(default)] placeholder: bool`, set from the manager's `placeholder` flag (`manager/headless.rs:104`, M9 decision 11a). The TUI's `headless_control_refusal` (`app/headless.rs:24-28`) returns no refusal for a placeholder, so `C-b x` and `C-b X` remove it as the daemon already allows.
45. **Three tier-3 states raise alerts** (FU-F21). `FullInfo` gains `#[serde(default)] held: bool` (ruling C-18's executor hold, `engine/full.rs:236`). `app/alerts.rs::alerts` adds, per stage:
    - held, priority 3 (with a halted run): `stage <n> tier 3 held after executor failures; anthrex run resume retries`;
    - `propagate_red`, priority 3: `stage <n> propagate red at <sha7>` (review ruling I7: `StageInfo.propagate_red` is the red commit's sha, `engine/propagate.rs:90,440`, not a text; `<sha7>` is its first seven characters);
    - `full.state == Red`, priority 3: `stage <n> tier 3 red`, then ` · fix task <id>` when the stage has one.

    The two reds are skipped while the run's orchestrator lives (it is woken for them), as `blocked_text` does for tasks.
46. **Limit texts name the round** (FU-F36). In round 2 or later: `round <r> of run <id> already has <n> scouts, the most max_scouts allows` (`engine/run_scouts.rs:47`) and `round <r>'s window limit (<n>) reached` (`engine/dispatch.rs:169`). Round 1 keeps today's texts.
47. **A later round pre-warms from its start** (FU-F39). *Obsolete (ruling T6-2): no later round's task is ever pre-warmed; Implementation notes, Task M9.5.6.* `dispatch_launch::prewarm` (`:82`) pre-warms from `propagate_sync::start_of(run, task)` (`propagate_sync.rs:59`, `pub(in crate::run::engine)`, imported by `propagate.rs:25`; the base dispatch would use), not `run.base_sha`, and records it in `Task.prewarm_base: Option<String>` (`#[serde(default)]`); dispatch launches a pre-warmed task when `prewarm_base` equals its start (`:126`), instead of comparing with `run.base_sha`.

### What the TUI shows of tuning

48. **Read-only, with the CLI's hint** (rulings RH-5, RH-6; audit F16).
    - **The stats screen** (`app/stats.rs`, `ui/stats.rs`) draws `HistoryStats.tuning` after the flaky block: the same lines `refit_render::render` prints, the `*` note and `budget S: configured …` lines in `Role::Muted`, and, when proposals exist, the hint `apply: anthrex run stats --apply <id>` in place of the CLI's `apply with …` line. No apply, no dismiss, no new `ActionKind`.
    - **The Settings screen** (`ui/settings.rs`), when the TUI has a project (`goal_project()`'s, the selection's, else `App.default_dir`), sends one tagged `Stats { dir, read_only: true }` on opening and draws, from its `TuningReport`, a muted `refit: <calls> calls <m>m` beside the S and M budgets whose class has a written refit and is not configured (`ClassTuning.refit_budget`, `ClassTuning.configured`), and a muted `overridden by [orchestrator.routes.orchestrator]` beside the orchestrator default when `TuningReport.orchestrator_list` is set. With no project, or before the reply, neither is drawn. Saving in Settings writes the budget into `config.toml`, which makes it explicit (decision 3), unless the value equals the default, which Settings does not write. **Opening Settings writes nothing**: a plain `Stats` records reverts (git work and a history append, `adapt_history.rs:90-93`) and refits `tuning.toml` (decision 6), so `RunRequest::Stats` gains `#[serde(default)] read_only: bool`; with it the daemon records no revert and writes no file, and reports the refit it would write as the class's `refit_budget` with `RefitState` as the file has it. The stats screen (`app/stats.rs`) keeps sending a plain `Stats`, as `anthrex run stats` does.

### `proto`

`proto/src/run.rs`, additions:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]   // unchanged
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum AgentRole { Orchestrator, Worker, Reviewer, Scout, Planner, Decider,
                     Racer, TestWriter }                  // appended: "racer", "test_writer"
// PlanTask gains:       #[serde(default)] pub race: bool, #[serde(default)] pub pair: bool
// PlanEdit::AmendTask:  #[serde(default)] race: Option<bool>, #[serde(default)] pair: Option<bool>
// RunRef gains:         #[serde(default)] pub lane: Option<RaceLane>
```

`proto/src/tuning.rs` (new; everything derives `Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize`, `Eq` where it compiles, enums `snake_case`):

```rust
#[derive(Copy, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)] #[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
pub enum RaceLane { A, B }                                       // "a" | "b"
impl RaceLane { pub fn label(self) -> &'static str; pub fn other(self) -> RaceLane; }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum LaneState { Preparing, Working, Proof, Check, Review, Won, Adopted, Lost, Out }
pub struct LaneInfo { pub lane: RaceLane, pub route: Route, pub state: LaneState, pub checkout: String,
                      pub head: Option<String>, pub reason: Option<String>, pub salvage_ref: Option<String> }
pub struct RaceInfo { pub lanes: Vec<LaneInfo>, pub winner: Option<RaceLane>, pub adopted: bool }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum PairPhase { Writing, Implementing }
pub struct PairInfo { pub phase: PairPhase, pub writer_route: Route, pub test: Option<String>,
                      pub red: Option<String>, pub red_checked: Option<bool>, pub writer_failures: u8 }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum TaskPattern { Race, Pair }

// tuning.toml (decision 10); deny_unknown_fields at every level
pub const TUNING_VERSION: u32 = 1;
pub struct TuningFile {
    pub v: u32,
    #[serde(default)] pub budgets: BTreeMap<String, ClassBudget>,      // "s", "m", "hub"
    #[serde(default)] pub weights: Option<PathWeights>,
    #[serde(default)] pub thresholds: Option<SizeThresholds>,
    #[serde(default)] pub routes: BTreeMap<String, ClassRoute>,        // "s", "m"
    #[serde(default)] pub dismissed: BTreeMap<String, String>,         // proposal id -> proposed value
}
pub struct ClassBudget { pub tool_calls: u32, pub minutes: u32, #[serde(default)] pub tokens: Option<u64>,
                         pub samples: u32, pub at: u64 }
pub struct PathWeights { pub s_secs: u64, pub m_secs: u64, pub hub_secs: u64,
                         #[serde(default)] pub derived: Vec<String>, pub at: u64 }   // "S" | "M" | "hub"
#[derive(Copy)] pub struct SizeThresholds { pub s_lines: u32, pub m_lines: u32 }  // Default: 20, 100
#[derive(Copy)] pub struct ClassRoute { pub strength: Strength, pub effort: Effort }

// run stats (decision 11)
pub struct ClassTuning { pub class: String,                 // "S" | "M" | "hub"
                         pub samples: u32, pub budget: Budget, pub refit: RefitState,
                         pub configured: bool,                       // RH-5: [orchestrator.budget.<c>] in config
                         pub refit_budget: Option<Budget>,           // the refit, used or not
                         pub weight_secs: Option<u64>, pub weight_derived: bool,
                         pub route: String }                         // "standard/low", or "list (config): …"
pub enum RefitState { NotYet, Kept, Written { at: u64 }, Configured, Off }
pub enum TuningChange { Threshold { class: String, lines: u32 }, Route { class: String, route: ClassRoute } }
pub struct TuningProposal { pub id: String, pub text: String, pub current: String, pub proposed: String,
                            pub change: TuningChange }
pub struct TuningReport { pub path: PathBuf, pub min_samples: u32, pub refit_budgets: bool,
                          pub classes: Vec<ClassTuning>, pub proposals: Vec<TuningProposal>,
                          pub moved_bad_file: Option<PathBuf>, pub applied: Vec<String>,
                          pub dismissed: Vec<String>,
                          pub orchestrator_list: Option<String> }    // RH-5: the list's first candidate
```

`proto/src/run_info.rs`, new fields, each `#[serde(default)]`:

```rust
// RunInfo:        pub writer_caps: BTreeMap<String, u8>,    // runtimes whose cap is below max_writers
// TaskInfo:       pub race: Option<RaceInfo>, pub pair: Option<PairInfo>      (skipped when None)
// AgentRoundInfo: pub lane: Option<RaceLane>, pub failed_error: Option<String>, pub failed_until: Option<u64>

```

`proto/src/run_wire.rs`:

```rust

    Stats { dir: PathBuf, #[serde(default)] apply: Vec<String>, #[serde(default)] dismiss: Vec<String>,
            #[serde(default)] read_only: bool },          // read_only: no revert recorded, no file written
    McpReady { run_id: String, window_id: u32 },    // appended after Iterate; answered RunReply::Done
}
// ToolCall gains, after `chain`: #[serde(default)] pub lane: Option<RaceLane>
```

`proto/src/history.rs`:


`proto/src/types.rs`: `WindowInfo` gains `#[serde(default)] pub placeholder: bool`. `proto/src/tiers.rs`: `FullInfo` gains `#[serde(default)] pub held: bool`. `lib.rs` re-exports the new types and sets `PROTO_VERSION = 16`.

### `config` (`config/src/orchestrator/tuning.rs` and `routes.rs`, new)

```rust
pub struct Tuning {                         // [orchestrator.tuning]
    pub min_samples: u32,                   // 30, 5..=1000
    pub per_run_cap: u32,                   // 10, 1..=1000 (per run and round)
    pub window: u32,                        // 200, 10..=5000; below min_samples is a problem and uses min_samples
    pub budget_factor_percent: u32,         // 250, 200..=300
    pub threshold_percentile: u32,          // 90, 50..=99
    pub min_change_percent: u32,            // 20, 1..=100
    pub escalate_above_percent: u32,        // 25, 1..=100
    pub refit_budgets: bool,                // true
    pub refit_tokens: bool,                 // false
    pub path_weights: bool,                 // true
    pub adaptive_concurrency: bool,         // true
    pub recover_after_mins: u64,            // 10, 1..=240
    pub halve_hold_secs: u64,               // 60, 0..=3600
    pub race_slot_wait_secs: u64,           // 120, 0..=3600
}
#[derive(Default)] pub struct ConfiguredBudgets { pub s: bool, pub m: bool }   // RH-5
#[derive(Default)] pub struct TuningConfig { pub table: Tuning, pub routes: RouteLists,
                                             pub configured: ConfiguredBudgets }
pub(crate) fn read_tuning(orchestrator: &toml::Table, roster: &[proto::ModelEntry],
                          problems: &mut Vec<Problem>) -> TuningConfig;
// routes.rs

#[derive(Default)] pub enum Pick { #[default] First, Spread }
#[derive(Default)] pub struct RouteList { pub candidates: Vec<Candidate>, pub pick: Pick }
#[derive(Default)] pub struct RouteLists {                 // [orchestrator.routes.<name>]
    pub s: RouteList, pub m: RouteList, pub hub: RouteList,
    pub review: RouteList, pub scout: RouteList, pub decider: RouteList,
    pub planner: RouteList, pub orchestrator: RouteList }
```

**Reconcile rows** (`run/reconcile/git.rs`):

| `OpKind` | Reality checked | Replay | Otherwise |
|----------|-----------------|--------|-----------|
| `CrownRacer` | `refs/heads/<task_branch>` in the user's repository | task branch at `lane_head` → `Crowned { head }` (no re-pin here: reconcile runs at a restart, where the checkout's pin is built from `Task.branch`, which `Crowned` sets; M9.5.1 item 9) | absent → `NotStarted` (the op re-runs; it is a compare-and-swap); at another commit → `RefMoved` |
| `Proof` with `red_only` | none | — | `NotStarted` (the existing `Proof` row) |
| `RemoveWorktree` with `keep_head` | the path; the salvage ref | path gone (or, with `keep_path`, present) and the ref present → `Removed { salvage_ref }` | `NotStarted` (the existing row) |

### MCP (`crates/mcp`) and `anthrex mcp` (`cli/src/mcp_cmd.rs`)

`McpOptions` gains `lane: Option<RaceLane>` after `chain`. `anthrex mcp` accepts `--role racer`, `--role test_writer` and `--lane a|b`; validation in `mcp_cmd.rs`, next to `--chain`'s, with the existing sentence shape (`mcp_cmd.rs:73-87`): `--role racer requires --lane <a|b>` and `--lane <a|b> is accepted only with --role racer`. `tools_for(Racer)` and `tools_for(TestWriter)` (`tools.rs:17-30`) are the worker's tools, `task_done`, `task_blocked` and `task_note`, with the same schemas; `role_name` (`:38`) gains `racer` and `test_writer`, spelt as `anthrex mcp --role` takes them (its doc comment, `tools.rs:37`). The orchestrator's server sends `RunRequest::McpReady` once after its first `list_tools` (decision 38); no other role does.

Engine-side refusals (`ToolResult { ok: false }`), exact:
- `the race for task <id> is over: racer <lane> won. Stop now.`
- `racer <lane> of task <id> is out: <reason>. Stop now.`
- decisions 25's and 26's `task_done` rejections.

### Contracts and texts (exact)

`TEST_WRITER_CONTRACT`, in `run/contract_patterns.rs`, in the worker contract's form and words (`WORKER_CONTRACT`, `run/contract.rs:28-40`; M9 rule 62 names each tool as `<tool> (in Claude: mcp__anthrex__<tool>)`). Lines 1, 6 and 8 to 12 are the worker contract's lines word for word; line 3 is the worker's line 3 with a different first sentence:

```text
You are the test writer in an anthrex orchestration run. A different agent writes the implementation after you.
1. Work only in this worktree and only in the paths this task owns. Changing files outside them stops the task.
2. Write only the test named in your task prompt. It must check the behaviour the task asks for, and fail now because that behaviour does not exist yet. Do not implement the behaviour.
3. Commit the test: that commit is the red commit, and it must be your last commit. Its HEAD is detached: commit on it, and the engine records your commits on the task's branch. Never create, switch or push branches, and never rewrite commits already there. Commit new files: untracked files are not part of your work.
4. Run the single-test command from your task prompt once, and confirm the test fails.
5. Call the anthrex tool task_done (in Claude: mcp__anthrex__task_done) with a summary, test set to the test's name, and red set to your commit. Then stop.
6. If you cannot continue, call task_blocked (in Claude: mcp__anthrex__task_blocked): kind question if you need an answer, mis_sized if the task is bigger than one task, environment if a tool or setup is broken. Then stop.
7. Do not write the implementation, even to check your test: the next agent does.
8. Messages that start with [anthrex] come from the orchestration engine. Do what they say, commit, and call task_done again.
9. Nobody can answer a permission prompt. If a tool is denied, work without it or call task_blocked with kind environment.
10. If you learn something that affects other tasks or the plan, such as another place that must change, a wrong assumption in the brief, or a risk, report it with task_note (in Claude: mcp__anthrex__task_note) and keep working. Use task_blocked only when you cannot continue.
11. A message of kind change means the plan or the code around this task changed: in your next task_done summary, start with Changes applied: and say how you applied it. A message of kind stop_and_wait means finish your current step, commit anything worth keeping, and end your turn without calling task_done; wait for the next message.
12. After a commit, git may print Unable to create '.../packed-refs.lock': Operation not permitted. That is expected: the commit succeeded, and it needs no action. Do not try to fix it or change git settings.
```

| Name | Text |
|------|------|
| `test_writer_prompt` | `[anthrex] Test for task <id>: <title>`, `Run goal: <goal>`, `Worktree: <path>`, `Branch: <branch>`, `Start commit: <sha7>` (as `worker_prompt`, `contract.rs:105-109`), `Test to write: <test_to_write>`, `Single-test command: <single_test>`, blank line, `Your job: write and commit only the failing test <test_to_write>. A different agent implements the behaviour after you.`, blank line, `This task owns:` with `- <glob>` lines, `Acceptance criteria:` with `- <item>` lines, blank line, the brief last. |
| `pair_implementer_note` | `The failing test <test> was committed in <red7> by a separate test writer. Make it pass without weakening it; you may add more tests. Call task_done with test <test> and red <red7>, or leave both out.` |
| `pair_reviewer_note` | `A separate test writer committed the test <test> at <red7>; the weakening signals above are measured from that commit, so a W-signal on its files means the implementer changed the test.` |
| `red_check_failed_message` | `[anthrex] The red check failed: at your red commit <red7> the test <test> passed, so it does not check missing behaviour. Change the test so it fails without the implementation, commit, then call task_done again.` / `Command: <command>` / `Last 40 lines:` / tail |
| `codex_filter_note` | `Test output: run every command that starts with <prefixes joined ", "> through the output filter, as <exe> filter-run --mode <mode> --log-dir <log_dir> -c '<command>'. It prints the failures and the path of the full log.` (`<log_dir>` is the checkout's `TMPDIR`'s `anthrex-logs`.) |
| `START_PROMPT` | `orchestrator waits at a start prompt; focus its window (C-b T, Enter) to answer it` |
| first prompt's last line | `Start with get_context, then scout, then plan. If an anthrex tool is reported missing, call get_context again before anything else: the server may still be connecting.` |
| racing refusals | `task <id> is racing: it cannot stop and wait; message it or cancel it`; `task <id> is racing: it has nothing to retry until one racer is left`; `task <id> is racing: there is no failed gate to override` |
| amend refusals | `task <id> has started: its race cannot change`; `task <id> has started: its pair cannot change` |
| race and lane log lines | `race <task>: racer <lane> won`; `race <task>: racer <lane> out: <reason>`; `race <task>: racer <lane> adopted after racer <other> went out`; `kept <lane> checkout: its racer did not exit`; `removed a stale <file> left by the stopped racer` |
| wake log lines | `wake-up for run <id> waits: <reason>` with `<reason>` one of `window working`, `window starting`, `input <n>s ago`, `attention open`, `no hook signal yet`; `wake-up delivered to window <n> (<bytes> bytes)`; `first turn still waiting 60 s after launch: no MCP ready notice yet` / `first turn still waiting 60 s after launch: the window is not ready yet` |
| round-aware limits | `round <r> of run <id> already has <n> scouts, the most max_scouts allows`; `round <r>'s window limit (<n>) reached` |
| tier-3 alerts | `stage <n> tier 3 held after executor failures; anthrex run resume retries`; `stage <n> propagate red at <sha7>`; `stage <n> tier 3 red` [` · fix task <id>`] |

Notes, log lines and attention lines not listed are the exact texts in decisions 6, 12, 16, 18 and 25.

### CLI

```
anthrex run stats [--dir <d>] [--json] [--apply <id>]... [--dismiss <id>]... [--yes]
anthrex mcp --role <orchestrator|planner|scout|worker|reviewer|racer|test_writer> --run <run> [--task <task>]
            [--scout <id>] [--epic <epic>] [--chain <c>] [--lane <a|b>] --window <id> [--socket <path>]   (hidden)
anthrex filter-hook --mode <m> --log-dir <dir> [--prefix <p>]... [--runtime <claude|codex>]   (hidden, before clap; default claude)
```

The flags go in `cli/src/run_cmd.rs` (`RunCommand::Stats`, `:192`), `cli/src/mcp_cmd.rs` and `cli/src/filter_hook.rs`; `cli/src/main.rs` is not touched. `--role decider` stays refused.

`run stats` prints M8b's text (`stats::render`, ending with the flaky block), then `refit_render::render(tuning)`. For the recorded history `refit.jsonl` (task M9.5.8) with no `tuning.toml` and the default config, at `now = 1_790_500_000` (2026-09-27 09:06:40 UTC, the value M9.5.8's tests inject; review ruling I5), the tuning part is exactly:

```
tuning: <repo_dir>/tuning.toml  (refit after 30 samples per class)
  CLASS  SAMPLES  BUDGET          WEIGHT  REFIT
  S      34       55 calls 18m    9m      2026-09-27 09:06
  M      12/30    150 calls 60m   27m*    not yet
  hub    3/30     150 calls 60m   27m*    not yet
  * derived from another class's median
tuning proposals:
  thresholds.s  S line threshold 20 → 35 (p90 of 34 merged S tasks)
  route.s       S route standard/low → standard/medium (14 of 34 S tasks, 41%, reached rung 2 or higher)
apply with anthrex run stats --apply <id>; dismiss with anthrex run stats --dismiss <id>
```

With `[orchestrator.budget.s] tool_calls = 40, minutes = 15` in the config, the S row is `  S      34       40 calls 15m    9m      configured` and a line follows the `*` line (or the rows, with no `*` line): `  budget S: configured 40 calls 15m (refit would be 55 calls 18m)`.

Rules:
- **Rows.** `format!("  {:<5}  {:<7}  {:<14}  {:<6}  {}", class, samples, budget, weight, refit)`: fixed widths, unlike M8b's table, which computes them from content (`stats.rs:265-268`).
- **`samples`.** `<n>` when the class qualifies, else `<n>/<min_samples>`.
- **`budget`.** The effective budget: `<calls> calls <minutes>m`, then ` <tokens> tok` when set, tokens in M8b's `k`/`M` notation.
- **`weight`.** `<secs / 60>m`, or `<secs>s` under a minute, `*` when derived, `-` without weights. The `* derived …` line only when a weight is derived.
- **`refit`.** `yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm` UTC (`report::format_utc`, `report.rs:292`), `not yet`, `kept`, `configured`, or `off`. With `refit_budgets = false` the header's parenthesis reads `(budget refit off: [orchestrator.tuning] refit_budgets = false)`.
- **A listed class's route** adds the line `  route <C>: list (config): <runtime>/<model> <effort>, …` after the rows.
- **Proposals.** Under `tuning proposals:`, each `format!("  {:<12}  {}", id, text)`. With none: `tuning proposals: none`, and no `apply with` line.
- **Threshold text.** `<C> line threshold <cur> → <new> (p<pct> of <n> merged <C> tasks)`.
- **Route text, up.** `<C> route <s>/<e> → <s>/<e> (<k> of <n> <C> tasks, <p>%, reached rung 2 or higher)`, `p = k × 100 / n` rounded down.
- **Route text, down.** `<C> route <s>/<e> → <s>/<e> (none of <n> <C> tasks reached rung 2 or higher or failed on quality)`.
- **A moved bad file** adds the first line `tuning: tuning.toml did not parse (<error>); it was moved to <path> and tuning starts again from history`.

`--apply` asks `apply <id>: <text>? [y/N]` per id unless `--yes`, then prints `applied <id>: new runs in <project> use it` per id before the stats. `--dismiss` prints `dismissed <id>: it is not proposed again while it would propose <value>`. A refusal prints the daemon's message and exits 1. `--json` prints `HistoryStats` with `tuning`.

`run status` additions: decision 30.

### Run view (TUI)

| Round | `round_label` | Node content example |
|-------|---------------|----------------------|
| `Racer`, lane a, display round 1 / 2 | `racer a` / `racer a r2` | `racer a claude`, `racer a r2 claude` |
| `Reviewer` with a lane | `review a#<round>` | `review b#1 claude` |
| `TestWriter`, session 1, display round 1 / 2 | `test writer #1` / `test writer #1 r2` | `test writer #1 codex` |

`tree::round_label(role, session, number)` (`run_rows.rs:32`) keeps its signature; `tree::round_label_with_lane(role, lane: Option<RaceLane>, session, number)` is new, `round_label` calls it with `None`, and every caller that labels a `DisplayRound` (the tree, `graph/run_text.rs`, `inspector/run_round.rs`) passes the round's lane. Every node stays within the 24-column limit (`graph/mod.rs`).

**Glyphs**, through 9.0.7's kit (`theme::glyph(Glyph, ascii)` and `theme::Role`), in `graph/paint/style.rs::round_glyph` (`:216`):
- **A live racer or test writer** follows a worker's rules (`live`: `Glyph::Live` in `Role::Working`, or the window's status look, or `Glyph::Blocked` in `Role::Paused` while rate-limited or waiting out a failed turn, decision 43).
- **An ended racer:** `Glyph::Passed` in `Role::Done` when its lane is `Won` or `Adopted`; `Glyph::Ended` in `Role::Muted` when `Lost` or `Out`.
- **An ended test writer:** `Glyph::Passed`, or `Glyph::Failed` in `Role::Failed` when it is the last test-writer round of a task `blocked` in phase `Writing`.

**Inspector** (`inspector/run_task_sections.rs` for the task's DETAIL rows, `inspector/run_round.rs` for round fields; a new `inspector/run_patterns.rs` holds the row builders):
- **Task, `race` row** (after `route` in `DETAIL_ROWS`): `a <runtime> <glyph> · b <runtime> <glyph>`, lane glyphs `Glyph::Live` live, `Glyph::Checking` in proof or check, `Glyph::Review` in review, `Glyph::Passed` won or adopted, `Glyph::Ended` lost or out; then ` · won by <lane>` or ` · <lane> adopted`. ASCII: `a claude * · b codex %`.
- **Task, `pair` row** (after `race`): `test writer <Passed|Live|Failed>`, then ` red <red7>`, then ` → implementer <mark>`, the mark `Glyph::NotStarted` before it starts, `Glyph::Live` while live, `Glyph::Passed` once merged. Unicode: `test writer ✓ red a1b2c3d → implementer ●`; ASCII: `test writer + red a1b2c3d -> implementer *`.
- **Task, state word** (right of the title): `racing` while a race has no winner, `writing the test` in phase `Writing`.
- **Racer round, `race` field** (after `doing`): `lane <x> · racing`, `lane <x> · won`, `lane <x> · adopted after lane <y> went out`, `lane <x> · lost to <y> · salvaged <ref>`, `lane <x> · out: <reason> · salvaged <ref>`, with ` · salvage pending` until the ref exists and ` · checkout kept` when kept.
- **Test-writer round, `test` field** (after `doing`): `writing <test_to_write>`, `<test> · red <red7> · fails at red ✓`, or `<test> · red <red7> · passed at red ✗` (ASCII `+`, `x`).
- **Round status** (decision 43): `failed: <error> · retries <hh:mm>` while a failed turn waits.
- **Run, `agents` row** (`inspector/run.rs:191-213`): each runtime part gains ` (writers <cap>/<max>)` when that runtime is in `writer_caps`.

The inspector scrolls (`inspector.rs:23`, `INSPECTOR_HEIGHT = 8`); no row is dropped.

## Tasks

Do them in this order. Each task's tests are written first and must fail before its change (AGENTS.md rule 6), except those marked **pinning**. One commit per task (M9.5.15 and M9.5.17a may commit per part); a move-only split is its own commit. Each task names the rulings and decisions it implements.

**Shared test rules.**

- **Agents.** Every test uses `fake-agent` only. Every fixture pins `ANTHREX_CLAUDE_BIN`, `ANTHREX_CODEX_BIN`, the decider binary and `ANTHREX_GH_BIN` (`pin_agents`, `TEST_GH_BIN`), and `ANTHREX_CONFIG` at a temp file; `daemon/tests/test_rigs_pin_agents.rs`'s file-level check covers every new test file that starts a daemon. No test changes program or host selection, except a `pr` test's `ANTHREX_CODE_HOST=fake` with its own `ANTHREX_FAKE_HOST_DIR` (`PrRig::env`). Kill tests scope to their own recorded pids; no test sends a signal by pattern.
- **Reducer tests** use `engine/tests/fixture.rs` (`Fixture::new`, `fx.send`, `fx.op`, `fx.done`, `fx.task`, `fx.signal`, `fx.tick`, `fx.approve`, `fx.tool`). M9.5 adds `Fixture::with_tuning(plan_toml, Tuned)`, `fx.lane(task, lane) -> &Lane`, `fx.lane_window(task, lane) -> u32` and `fx.caps() -> BTreeMap<String, u8>`.
- **History fixtures** live in `daemon/tests/fixtures/history/`: `refit.jsonl`, `too-few.jsonl`, `outlier-run.jsonl` and `quality.jsonl`. Their rules are in M9.5.8; `run/refit_tests.rs::fixture_records(name)` builds each in memory.
- **End-to-end tests** use `RunHarness` with deciders off and onboarding manual. M9.5 adds, beside `stored_profile` in `cli/tests/support/run_adapt.rs`: `history(file)` (copies a fixture to `<repo_dir>/history.jsonl`), `tuning() -> Option<TuningFile>`, and `release(name)` (creates `<tmp>/release/<name>`, the hand-off a scripted `sh` step polls for).
- **Timing.** Every new wall-clock bound names its derivation per `docs/timing-budgets.md`'s rules and gets a row there in the task that adds it. Expected: one task path within `RUN_WAIT`; `k` task paths in sequence within `k * RUN_WAIT`; a race or a paired task within `2 * RUN_WAIT` (a race's lanes serialise their git writes through one `GitQueue` and double the slot use; a pair adds a session and a red-only proof to one task's path); an accept within `FINISH_WAIT`; the first-turn bound within `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS` plus one wake check. None may be a fixed sleep as the only synchronisation.
- **Temp paths** are under `/tmp` (`tempfile::Builder::new().prefix("ax-…").tempdir_in("/tmp")`), never `std::env::temp_dir()`.

### Scenario map (spec §21)

| §21 scenario | Test here |
|--------------|-----------|
| A rate-limit event halving writers | `a_rate_limit_halves_the_runtimes_cap_never_below_one` (M9.5.13), `e2e_a_rate_limit_halves_writers_for_that_runtime` (M9.5.22) |
| A race whose loser is stopped and salvaged | `the_loser_is_stopped_then_salvaged_after_its_ops_and_exit` (M9.5.17b), `e2e_race_loser_is_stopped_and_salvaged` (M9.5.22) |
| A test writer's red commit handed to an implementer | `red_confirmed_starts_the_implementer_in_the_same_checkout` (M9.5.16), `e2e_test_writer_red_commit_is_handed_to_an_implementer` (M9.5.22) |
| *(M9.5)* budgets refit from recorded history; proposals applied only on confirmation | `refit_fixture_budgets_weights_and_proposals` (M9.5.8), `e2e_history_refits_budgets_and_a_confirmed_proposal_applies` (M9.5.22) |

Every other §21 scenario belongs to an earlier milestone and must stay green.

### M9.5.1 Verify facts and reconcile names

**Files.** `docs/milestones/M9.5-tuning.md` ("Implementation notes", and every reference the code moved, corrected in place). No code. Agents never run the real `claude` or `codex` (SAFETY.md); items 4–6 are written as exact commands for the user, and the controller asks the user to run them.

**Tests first.** None (a verification task).

**Acceptance.** Recorded under "Implementation notes" as a dated "M9.5.1" entry, each item with its command and output:
1. `PROTO_VERSION` is 15 (`proto/src/lib.rs:96`) and `HISTORY_VERSION` 5 on the branch's base; otherwise stop and re-derive the header.
2. Every `file:line` of "Starting point" and every row of "Names" exists with that spelling and shape; every difference and the name used instead.
3. `wc -l` of every file in "File sizes"; a file within 10 lines of 600 that a task grows gets a named move-only split, added to M9.5.3.
4. *(user-run)* **Codex `PreToolUse` in `exec`.** In a scratch repository with a standalone checkout made the way `run::git::checkout` makes one, `codex exec --json -s workspace-write -c approval_policy="never" <codex_user_config_only flags, if the CLI has them> -c 'hooks.PreToolUse=[{hooks=[{type="command",command="<script>"}]}]' -c 'hooks.state={…}'`, the trust entry from `trust_key(<default_hook_source()>, "pre_tool_use", 0, 0)` and `trust_hash`, no `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, `<script>` appending its stdin to a file, asked for `ls`. Does the hook fire, with what payload (fixture `daemon/tests/fixtures/headless/codex-<version>-pretooluse.jsonl` and its `.meta.json`)? `codex_shell_tool_names` and `codex_command_pointer`? Again for `codex exec resume`?
5. *(user-run)* **Command replacement.** Which output shape, if any, makes Codex run a replaced command (the stream's `command_execution.command`, and a marker file); that sets `codex_filter` and the shape `codex_rewrite` prints.
6. *(user-run)* **Codex's user-question tool.** Whether the installed Codex CLI has a config key (`-c …`) that turns off its own user-question tool for an interactive session; the key and the version (decision 40).
7. **MCP readiness** (decision 38), from code: `mcp/src/lib.rs`'s `list_tools` runs on the client's `tools/list`; `fake-agent`'s PTY mode never lists tools today (`fake-agent/src/mcp.rs` spawns a server only per `mcp_call`); real Claude's order (trust prompt, MCP connect, `tools/list`, first turn) is checked in the manual check.
8. **git** (on temp repositories; any process killed only by its own recorded pid): `update-ref refs/heads/x <sha> <zero oid>` creates the ref when absent and fails, with its message recorded, when present; `rev-parse --show-object-format` prints `sha1`, and whether `git init --object-format=sha256` works; in a checkout made like `run::git::checkout`'s (detached, no refs), a `git commit` with `GIT_EDITOR='sleep 30'` killed by `SIGKILL` leaves `index.lock` in the checkout's own git directory, and one sent `SIGTERM` removes it. Killing the `git` leaves its editor, an unrecorded orphan `sleep 30`: let it expire on its own; never kill it, or any process, by pattern (a past mutant's pattern kill SIGTERMed every `sleep` on the machine).
9. **Pins.** Where `PrepareWorktree` and a restart take `PinAs.own` from (`worktree/pinned.rs::pin`, its callers), and whether anything else caches a checkout's own branch (decision 21's re-pin).
10. **The headless kill** (`headless/session.rs:268`, `manager/headless_turns.rs:547`): `SIGTERM` to the group, `SIGKILL` at the kill grace unless the leader is reaped first; when `ProcessExited { killed_by_engine: true }` reaches the engine relative to that.

Fallbacks, until the user records items 4–6: `codex_filter = Instruction` (items 4–5); footer detection (item 6). If item 8 fails, stop work on decisions 21–22 and record it; if item 9 finds no restart path for the pin, record it and continue (the re-pin is still required for a live daemon).

**Commit.** `docs: verify milestone 9.5's facts against the code`

### M9.5.3b Daemon model for race, pair and concurrency

Review ruling I3: the daemon-only types the reducer tasks share are added once, here, before any task uses them. Every later task (M9.5.13, M9.5.15, M9.5.16, M9.5.17a, M9.5.17b, M9.5.20's snapshot side, M9.5.21) uses these types and adds none of its own for the same data.

**Files.** Create `run/model_tuning.rs` (pure types; `#[path = "model_tuning.rs"] mod tuning; pub use tuning::*;` in `model.rs`, as `model.rs:19-49` does for its other parts) with `Race`, `Lane`, `Pair` and `RuntimeConcurrency` exactly as in Interfaces "daemon" (M9.5.9 adds `ClassRoutes` to it later). Modify `model_task.rs` (211: `Task.{race: Option<Race>, pair: Option<Pair>, race_wait_since: Option<u64>}`), `model.rs` (466: `Run.concurrency: BTreeMap<String, RuntimeConcurrency>`), `model_rounds.rs` (`AgentRound.lane`), and the records that gain `lane: Option<RaceLane>`: `CheckRecord`, `ProofRecord`, `ReviewRecord` and `PendingOp` (`model.rs:121`). Every new field is `#[serde(default)]`, and an `Option` or empty map is skipped when unset (`skip_serializing_if`), so a run with no race, pair or cap writes the same `run.json` bytes as today. Every struct literal of these types gets the default. Nothing reads the new fields yet. Tests in `run/model_tuning_tests.rs` (new).

**Tests first:**
- `a_run_with_race_pair_and_caps_round_trips`: a `Run` whose task has a `Race` with two `Lane`s (one `Out` with a reason and a salvage ref), another task with a `Pair` in `Implementing`, `concurrency = {"codex": RuntimeConcurrency { cap: 1, .. }}`, and `lane: Some(B)` on an agent round, a check, a proof, a review and a pending op: written as `run.json` (the persisted form, through the same serializer the driver uses) and read back, equal.
- `an_old_run_json_still_loads`: `daemon/tests/fixtures/run/m93-run.json` (captured in M9.5.2 with the base's code) loads with `race`, `pair` and `race_wait_since` `None`, `concurrency` empty, every `lane` `None`; written again, it has none of the new keys.

**Acceptance.** Tests pass; `model_tuning.rs` is pure (the Verification grep). **Implements** the model half of decisions 16, 19, 20 and 25; review ruling I3.

**Commit.** `feat(daemon): add the race, pair and per-runtime concurrency model`

Tests in `engine/tests/first_turn.rs` (new), `driver/wake_first_turn_tests.rs` (new), `mcp/tests/stdio.rs`, `fake-agent/tests/pty_first_message.rs` (new), `orch/contract_tests_prompts.rs`.

**Tests first.**
- `a_fresh_orchestrator_launches_without_a_prompt`: `CreateOrchestrator`'s window spec has `initial_prompt: None`; the orchestrator record has `first_turn_pending` and `first_prompt == orchestrator_first_prompt(run)`.
- `the_first_turn_waits_for_mcp_ready`: no `WakeOrchestrator` before `McpReady`; after it, one with the first prompt and `first_turn: true`; `OrchestratorWoken` for it clears `first_turn_pending`.
- `mcp_ready_from_another_window_is_refused` (driver): a `McpReady` naming a window that is not the run's orchestrator changes nothing.
- `an_unsignalled_window_gets_no_first_turn_even_with_mcp_ready` (driver, review ruling C1): a first-turn wake for a window that is `Idle`, quiet past the quiet time, with no open attention and `signals_seen: false`: nothing is pasted over three wake checks, and the wait reason `no hook signal yet` is logged once; a signal then delivers it on the next check.
- `past_the_bound_nothing_is_pasted_and_the_start_prompt_shows` (engine, C1): no `McpReady` for `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS`: no `WakeOrchestrator`; the snapshot's attention has `START_PROMPT` exactly, once; the run log has `first turn still waiting 60 s after launch: no MCP ready notice yet`. Then `McpReady`: the wake is emitted; its `OrchestratorWoken` clears the attention line.
- `a_relaunch_before_the_first_turn_delivers_it_after_the_new_servers_notice`: a restore with `first_turn_pending`, then `run resume`: the relaunch resets `mcp_ready`; the new session's `McpReady` and a signal deliver `first_prompt`.
- `the_orchestrator_server_announces_its_tools_once` (`mcp/tests/stdio.rs`): two `tools/list` give one `McpReady` to the fake daemon; a worker's server sends none.
- `fake_agent_takes_its_first_message_from_stdin` (`fake-agent`): a PTY session with an MCP server and no `--` prompt lists tools, runs its `SessionStart` hook (recorded by a hook script that appends to a file), then a script's `expect` matches the pasted first message.
- `the_first_prompt_tells_how_to_retry_a_missing_tool`: the exact last line; rule 4 of `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT` ends with the retry sentence (ruling T1-1), and the byte-for-byte pin `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPECTED` (`orch/contract_tests.rs`) is updated to match.
- Every existing orchestrator end-to-end test passes unchanged (**pinning**; the fake orchestrator's `SessionStart` hook sets `signals_seen` before the first paste).

**Acceptance.** Tests pass; the paste never happens while `attention_open` (a test with an open attention, `mcp_ready` and `signals_seen`), and never into a window with no signal. **Implements** decision 38 (X1: FU-F12); review ruling C1.

- `refit_fixture_budgets_weights_and_proposals`, `refit.jsonl` at `now = 1_790_500_000`, default config:

### M9.5.15 Git: lane checkouts, the crown, stale locks, `keep_head` and the red-only proof

One task, five parts (review ruling I10); its tests are listed by part so one implementer can take them in order, one commit per part allowed.

**Files.** Create `run/git/crown.rs`, `run/driver/lane_ops.rs` and `daemon/tests/run_git_crown.rs`. Modify `model_task.rs` (`Task::checkout_name`, over M9.5.3b's `Race`), `run/driver/restore.rs` (`run_worktree_paths`, `:347-381`: every lane checkout, ruling T1-3), every site that derives a checkout from `task.id()` (`role_launch.rs`'s helpers' callers, `run/plan.rs`, `run/edits.rs`, `engine/fixes.rs`, `engine/integration.rs`, `run/model.rs::task_path`, `driver/cleanup.rs`), `run/git/checkout.rs` (346, +10), `worktree/pinned.rs` (439, +25: `repin_own`), `run/git/salvage.rs` (387, +25: `keep_head`), `run/git/mod.rs` (+3, re-exports only), `run/proof.rs` (283, +30: `red_only`), `engine/ops.rs` (`CrownRacer` with `checkout`, `Proof.red_only`, `RemoveWorktree.{keep_head, clear_locks, keep_path}`), `engine/op_result.rs` (`Crowned`, `Removed.cleared_locks`), `run/driver/ops.rs` (541, +10: the match arms that call `lane_ops`), `run/reconcile/git.rs` (544, +30).

**Tests first**, on `TempRepo`s with `recording_git`:

*Part 1, the `checkout_name` sweep.*
- `checkout_name_is_the_task_until_a_lane_is_crowned` (unit): the task id; `t1.b` once lane b is `Won`; `t1.a` once lane a is `Adopted`.
- `a_lane_checkout_has_its_own_repository`: preparing `t1.a` creates `tasks/t1.a/` with `git/objects`, `engine/`, its own `TMPDIR`, and `worker_git_roots(data, "t1.a")` names them; an import moves `anthrex/r/t1.a`.
- `every_checkout_site_follows_checkout_name`: for a task whose `checkout_name()` is `t1.b`, the worker spec's `cwd`, `TMPDIR` and output-filter log dir, the proof and review checkouts (`t1.b.proof`, `t1.b.review`), `Run::task_path`, cleanup's paths and salvage's worktree are all `t1.b`'s; for an unraced task every one is unchanged (**pinning**: the existing path tests pass).

*Part 2, the crown and the re-pin* (review ruling I2).
- `crown_creates_the_task_branch_at_the_lane_head`: afterwards `refs/heads/anthrex/r/t1` is the lane head; the lane checkout's `HEAD` file is unchanged (still detached); every write carried the engine's git flags (`--no-optional-locks`, hooks off).
- `after_the_crown_imports_move_the_task_branch`: the `CrownRacer` op is executed **through the driver's executor** (`driver/ops.rs::run` → `lane_ops::crown_racer`), not `git::crown` alone; then a commit in the crowned lane checkout and `sync`: `anthrex/r/t1` moves and `anthrex/r/t1.a` does not. Without the executor's `repin_own` call the lane branch moves instead, so the test fails.
- `a_moved_branch_is_not_repinned`: an executor run that gets `RefMoved` leaves the pin's own branch the lane's.
- `crown_is_idempotent_and_refuses_a_moved_branch` (ruling T1-2): a second `crown` at the same lane head, whose `update-ref` create fails with `reference already exists`, reads the ref and returns `Crowned`; with the task branch at another commit the same failure returns `RefMoved`; `recording_git` shows the `update-ref` then the read in both cases.
- `crown_uses_the_repositorys_zero_oid` (M9.5.1 found sha256 supported: 64 characters).
- `a_restart_repins_every_lane_checkout` (ruling T1-3; M9.5.1 item 9): `run_worktree_paths` (`driver/restore.rs:347`) pins, for a racing task, each prepared lane checkout `t1.a`/`t1.b` with `own = refs/heads/anthrex/<id>/t1.<lane>` and its `objects`, `engine` and git dirs, and each lane's `.proof`/`.review` read-only; for a crowned or adopted task the lane's checkout (`task.worktree`) with `own = refs/heads/<task.branch>`, the task branch. After `pin_worktrees` on such a snapshot, `import::task_pin` accepts each lane checkout, and a `sync` in the crowned lane moves `anthrex/<id>/t1`. Without the change a lane checkout is unpinned after a restart and its import is refused, so the test fails.

*Part 3, locks and salvage.*
- `stale_locks_are_cleared_only_in_the_lanes_git_dir` (review focus 4): a lane checkout with one commit, a dirty file, and `index.lock` and `HEAD.lock` in `tasks/t1.a/git/`; `clear_stale_locks` removes both and reports them; then `salvage` with `keep_head` gives a ref whose tree has the dirty file and whose parent is the lane commit; a lock anywhere in the user's repository is untouched.
- `keep_head_salvages_a_clean_checkout_at_head`, and `without_keep_head_a_clean_checkout_gets_no_ref` (**pinning**).
- `keep_path_salvages_and_leaves_the_checkout`: with `keep_path`, the salvage ref exists, the checkout and a lock in its git directory are untouched.

*Part 4, the red-only proof.*
- `the_red_only_proof_runs_only_the_red_run` (RP-1): a red whose test exits 1 gives `red_failed: true` and no head run; exit 0 gives `false` with the tail; it went through the slot hook once.

*Part 5, reconcile.*
- `reconcile_crown_done_and_moved`, `reconcile_keep_head_removal`.

**Acceptance.** Tests pass. Every git call goes through `run::git` and none runs under the engine or manager lock (AGENTS.md rules 10, 11). `git diff --stat main -- crates/daemon/src/run/git/worktrees.rs crates/daemon/src/process.rs crates/daemon/src/headless/session.rs crates/daemon/src/manager/headless_turns.rs crates/daemon/src/manager/remove.rs` shows no change (RR-2: no kill-path change; the same file list as Verification). A daemon restart re-pins every lane checkout (ruling T1-3). **Implements** decisions 19 (checkouts), 21, 22 (git half), 25 (the red-only proof), 27; rulings RR-1, RR-2, RP-1, T1-2, T1-3; review ruling I2.

**Commit.** `feat(daemon): lane checkouts, crown a racer by compare-and-swap, clear a stopped lane's locks, keep a loser's head, and a red-only proof` (or one per part, each `feat(daemon): …` naming its part)

### M9.5.17a The race in the reducer I: dispatch, lanes, gates per lane, elimination

**Files.** Create `engine/race.rs`, `engine/tests/race.rs`, `engine/tests/race_lanes.rs`. 6. stops its daemon and removes both paths in `finally`.

## Verification

Run on the branch before opening the pull request (the full suite and the smoke run are the merge gate; during a task, run only the touched tests):

```bash
cargo build --workspace --all-targets
cargo test --workspace
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo +<CI's version> clippy --workspace --all-targets --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu -- -D warnings
cargo fmt --all --check
python3 scripts/pty-smoke.py
```

Milestone-specific checks, each printing nothing unless stated:

```bash
# The new pure files do no I/O.
grep -nE "std::(fs|process|net|thread)|tokio::|Command::new|SystemTime" \
  crates/daemon/src/run/{refit,refit_render,estimate,validate_patterns,route_pick,contract_patterns,role_launch_patterns,model_tuning,snapshot_patterns}.rs \
  crates/daemon/src/run/engine/{concurrency,race,race_end,pair}.rs crates/daemon/src/status_codex.rs
# No floating point in the statistics.
grep -nE "\bf32\b|\bf64\b" crates/daemon/src/run/{refit,estimate}.rs crates/daemon/src/run/engine/concurrency.rs
# No bypass of Codex's hook trust.
grep -rn "dangerously-bypass-hook-trust" crates/daemon/src/headless crates/daemon/src/output_filter.rs
# The client's new state and renderers do no I/O (AGENTS.md rule 5).
grep -nE "std::(fs|process|net)|tokio::" crates/tui/src/inspector/run_patterns.rs crates/tui/src/tree/task_rounds.rs crates/tui/src/ui/stats.rs crates/tui/src/ui/settings.rs
# No test reaches a real agent or gh.
grep -rnE "ANTHREX_(CLAUDE|CODEX|GH)_BIN\"?, *\"(claude|codex|gh)\"" crates/*/tests scripts
# No kill-path change (ruling RR-2).
git diff --stat main -- crates/daemon/src/run/git/worktrees.rs crates/daemon/src/process.rs crates/daemon/src/headless/session.rs crates/daemon/src/manager/headless_turns.rs crates/daemon/src/manager/remove.rs
```

- `PROTO_VERSION` is 16 and `proto_version_is_sixteen` passes; `HISTORY_VERSION` is 5.
- `grep -rn "tuning.toml" crates/ scripts/` shows only paths under the data directory (`repo_dir`), never a repository or checkout.
- `git diff --stat main -- crates/config/src/lib.rs crates/cli/src/main.rs crates/daemon/src/run/git/worktrees.rs` is empty.
- `wc -l` of every file in "File sizes" and every new file, recorded under "Implementation notes": nothing new above 600, nothing grown past its budget.
- Every git call this milestone adds goes through `run::git`, and no git call and no tuning lock is held under the engine or manager lock (AGENTS.md rules 2, 10 and 11).
- After the tests and the smoke script, nothing of the implementer's shows in `pgrep -fl "anthrex daemon"` or `pgrep -fl fake-agent`, and `/tmp/ax-*` and `/tmp/anthrex-smoke-tuning-*` are gone. Leaked pids are reported, never killed by pattern.

## Manual check

This step uses real Claude and Codex, so the **user** runs it (agents never run the real CLIs), with an isolated daemon, config and repository:

```bash
export ANTHREX_SOCKET=/tmp/anthrex-m95/daemon.sock ANTHREX_DATA_DIR=/tmp/anthrex-m95/data ANTHREX_CONFIG=/tmp/anthrex-m95/config.toml
mkdir -p /tmp/anthrex-m95 && cd /tmp/anthrex-m95 && git init -b main demo && cd demo \
  && mkdir tests && printf '#!/bin/sh\ngrep -q hello hello.sh 2>/dev/null && echo "PASS $1"\n' > tests/run.sh \
  && printf 'echo hi\n' > hello.sh && printf '#!/bin/sh\nsh -n hello.sh\n' > check.sh && git add . && git commit -m init
```

1. Store a profile (`anthrex profile detect --dir /tmp/anthrex-m95/demo`, then `anthrex profile confirm`). Copy `crates/daemon/tests/fixtures/history/refit.jsonl` to `<repo_dir>/history.jsonl` (`anthrex profile status --json`'s `repo_dir`).
2. `anthrex run stats --dir /tmp/anthrex-m95/demo` shows the tuning block with S refitted to `55 calls 18m` and two proposals under `tuning proposals:`. `cat <repo_dir>/tuning.toml` shows `[budgets.s]` and `[weights]`. `ls -a demo` shows nothing new. In the TUI, the stats screen shows the same block read-only, and Settings shows `refit: 55 calls 18m` muted beside S.
3. `anthrex run stats --dir … --apply route.s`, answer `n`: nothing changes; again with `y`: `applied route.s`.
4. Start a goal run in a **new** folder (a fresh clone of `demo`) with the Claude orchestrator. When Claude's folder-trust prompt shows, `C-b T` shows `orchestrator waits at a start prompt; focus its window …` in the run's attention, and `anthrex run status` prints it. Nothing is pasted while the prompt is open. Answer the prompt: the first turn is pasted only after the anthrex server has listed its tools and the window has sent a hook signal (the daemon log's `wake-up …` lines say why it waited), and the orchestrator's first `edit_plan` is accepted. With a Codex orchestrator, note whether a native question raises the alert. A real Claude orchestrator in a new folder: the trust prompt is answered only by you, then the first turn arrives (via the MCP ready notice or the 30 s grace; the run log's `first turn sent without the MCP ready notice after 30 s` says which). A real Codex orchestrator's first turn arrives as in 9.3.
5. Write a plan with `t1`, an M code task `race = true` owning `hello.sh` (Claude route), and `t2`, a `tdd` task owning `tests/run.sh`, `pair = true`, `test_to_write = "greets"`, depending on `t1`. `anthrex run start --plan … --dir …` and approve.
6. `C-b T`: under `t1`, `racer a claude` and `racer b codex` work side by side; the task's `race` row shows both lanes. When one lane passes review, the other's node turns to the muted `–` and its round shows `lost to … · salvaged refs/anthrex/salvage/…`. `git -C demo for-each-ref refs/anthrex/salvage` lists it, and `<data>/runs/<id>/tasks/t1.<loser>` is gone.
7. Under `t2`, `test writer #1 codex` commits the red test and finishes; `worker #2 claude` starts in the same checkout, and its first turn names the red commit. The `pair` row reads `test writer ✓ red … → implementer ●`.
8. The run inspector shows `est. left ~…` and `… × the bound`. If a runtime reports a rate limit, the `agents` row shows `(writers 1/3)` and the run log `rate limit on …`; it cannot be triggered on demand, so note whether it happened.
9. When the run is `complete`, read `REPORT.md` for the race, pair and tuning sections; `anthrex run accept <id> --yes`; `anthrex run stats` shows two more task records.
10. `anthrex daemon stop`. `pgrep -fl "anthrex daemon"`, `pgrep -fl "claude -p"` and `pgrep -fl "codex exec"` show nothing of yours. Remove `/tmp/anthrex-m95`.

## Review focus

The input classes and failure modes most likely to bite a user that the task tests would not exercise without being told to. Each has a test in its owning task; the reviewer checks those tests exist and fail without the change they guard.

1. **History with too few samples.** Nothing may be refitted, weighted or proposed below `min_samples`, and `run stats` says how many each class still needs. Test: `too_few_samples_refit_nothing_and_say_so` (M9.5.8).
2. **An outlier run or round.** One round with a stuck toolchain produces 25 tasks at 900 tool calls. Without the per-(run, round) cap the S budget moves from 55 to 88. Test: `one_outlier_run_cannot_move_a_budget_past_its_cap` (M9.5.8), both numbers.
3. **Both racers pass at once.** Two approvals in consecutive events, or one while the other's crown is in flight. A second crown would point the task branch twice. Tests: `both_racers_passing_in_one_step_crowns_exactly_one` (M9.5.17b) with its variants, and `e2e_race_both_pass_crowns_one` (M9.5.22).
4. **A loser stopped mid-commit.** A killed `git commit` can leave `index.lock` or `HEAD.lock` in the lane checkout's own git directory; salvage must then neither fail forever nor remove the checkout without its work, and nothing outside that directory may be touched. Tests: `stale_locks_are_cleared_only_in_the_lanes_git_dir` (M9.5.15), `the_loser_is_stopped_then_salvaged_after_its_ops_and_exit` and `a_loser_that_does_not_exit_keeps_its_checkout` (M9.5.17b).
5. **Rate-limit flapping.** Test: `flapping_rate_limits_halve_once_per_hold_and_recover_one_step_at_a_time` (M9.5.13).
6. **The crowned task's branch.** After a crown every later import must move `anthrex/<run>/<task>`, not the lane branch, or the merge queue merges a stale head. Test: `after_the_crown_imports_move_the_task_branch` (M9.5.15).
7. **Constructed inputs.** History fixtures are generated by their rules and compared byte for byte; "old messages still decode" and "an old `run.json` still loads" use the protocol-15 fixtures M9.5.2 captures with the base's code before any change (`m93_run_info.json`, `m93_history.jsonl`, `m93-run.json`), never ones written to agree with the new code.
8. **Move-only commits.** The test count before and after each is equal (M9.5.3).

## Risks and gotchas

1. **A refit is only as good as `phases`** (ruling RH-4). Working minutes come from phase accounting, which counts daemon downtime while a task was `working`. The per-(run, round) cap limits the damage; a pause-aware phase is filed.
2. **Budgets per session against totals per task.** A refit from task totals is generous for tasks that escalated. Deliberate.
3. **Thresholds change prompts, not rules.** Nothing in the engine enforces a line count.
4. **The head of the line is held by a race** for up to `race_slot_wait_secs`; bounded, and the price of putting the critical path first.
5. **Racing doubles tokens and test slots** (ruling RR-3). Both lanes' spend shows in `spent_total`, and both lanes run tier 1 and the weakening signals through the slots, so a racing task competes with every other task's checks. Opt-in, and the contract says so.
6. **A lane checkout costs disk.** Each lane is a full standalone checkout with its own object store and `setup` output; a race on a large repository with a heavy `setup` (a `cargo build`) doubles that until the loser's checkout is removed or, if its racer never exits, until accept or discard.
7. **The loser's commits survive only through the salvage ref.** Accept and discard delete every `anthrex/<run>/*` branch, lane branches included, in local and `pr` mode alike. `keep_head` keeps a clean loser's work reachable; do not "optimise" it away.
8. **Stale locks** (ruling RR-1, RR-2). Only `index.lock` and `HEAD.lock` in the lane checkout's own git directory are removed, and only after the racer's `ProcessExited`. The headless kill (`headless/session.rs:268`) sends `SIGTERM` to the whole group, and a `git` that receives it removes its own lock; `SIGKILL` to the group comes only if the leader outlives the grace. A process in the group that ignores `SIGTERM` and outlives a reaped leader could still hold a lock: that residual case is accepted rather than add a probe (RR-2). The engine's own git never uses the worker's index (it has its own copy in `engine/`), so a cleared lock cannot corrupt an engine operation.
9. **`pr`-mode revert blindness** (ruling RH-2). A revert that lands through GitHub as a squash or rebase merge names no task merge commit, so the refit never sees it, and a class can step down despite it. Bisect culprits and CI or review fix tasks still hold a class.
10. **Codex hook trust.** The filter hook is trusted by hash of its exact command; never pass `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`.
11. **Adaptive caps are per run**, and only task sessions count (ruling RC-1): orchestrator, planner and scout rate limits on the same subscription do not lower the writer cap.
12. **The first turn's paste** (a Claude orchestrator's; ruling T5a-2). Delivering the first prompt as a paste (decision 38) reuses the wake path's `ready` gate and adds two conditions, `mcp_ready` and `signals_seen`, so a window that never goes `Idle`, never connects its anthrex server or never sends a hook signal never gets its first turn. That is deliberate (review ruling C1): a paste into an unsignalled window could answer a start or trust prompt. Past `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS` the run's attention shows the start-prompt line, so the user is told; the wake log (decision 41) and the engine's `first turn still waiting` line say which condition is missing. Fix round 1 (ruling T5a-1) relaxes only `mcp_ready`: `MCP_READY_GRACE_SECS` (30 s) after the window's first signal the first turn goes without the notice, and the run log says so. A Codex orchestrator has none of this (ruling T5a-2): its first prompt is on its command line as in 9.3, so it keeps 9.3's risk that the first turn starts before its anthrex server has connected; the first prompt's and rule 4's retry sentence (ruling T1-1) is its mitigation.
13. **Socket and temp paths.** Every new test directory is under `/tmp`.
14. **Two OTLP keys for one run's credit (decision 37).** `OrchestratorUsage` sets a run's credit from one ledger total, and the driver keeps the latest total per credited run, so two ledger keys that resolve to one run overwrite each other. The alias of an adopted session resolves only while the run still holds that session (`usage_at_adopt` is `Some`), and a lost adoption, a launch or a restart ends it first (`orch_window::own_session`), so the old window's later totals are never credited. What remains is a total the driver coalesced under the alias before that step and drained just after it: it is credited on top of the new base until the run's own next total replaces it (one coalescing interval; accepted).

15. **A late notice from a superseded session** (fix round 1, m4; a Claude orchestrator's). `McpReady` carries no session or launch, so the engine can tell only that a restart is in flight, and ignores a notice then. The old session's server, killed with its window, could still deliver a notice after the restart's op result; that would release the new session's first turn before its own server is up. The paste still needs a signal, and the contract's retry line ("if an anthrex tool is reported missing, call `get_context` again") covers a tool the session does not have yet. Closing it fully needs the launch in the notice (an argv and wire change); not done.

## Follow-ups handled

M8a's "Out" table assigns to M9.5 adaptive concurrency, the threshold and budget refit, racing and the test-writer-then-implementer pattern: decisions 4–13, 16, 17–23 and 24–27. From the follow-ups file (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-17-anthrex-foundation-followups.md`), this milestone takes:

- From M8b's whole-branch review: the output filter for Codex workers (decision 28); routing and threshold proposals in `run stats` (decisions 8, 9, 11).
- From M8c (recorded by M8c.10): `RunInfo.estimate_left_secs` and `bound_ratio_permille` (decision 14); racer and test-writer rounds (decision 29).
- **X3, history and stats correctness:** FU-F2, a revert of a revert (decision 33, task M9.5.4a); FU-F37, a later round that never completes (decision 34, M9.5.4a); FU-F17, a quarantined test proposed again (decision 35, M9.5.4a); FU-F15, the tier caches' (and `tuning.toml`'s) temporary files (decision 36, M9.5.4a); FU-F40 and FU-F43, orchestrator usage across a chain (decision 37, M9.5.4b).
- **X1, orchestrator first-turn reliability:** FU-F12, `edit_plan` invisible on the first turn (decision 38, M9.5.5a); FU-F13, the start prompt's attention line (decision 39, M9.5.5b); FU-F27, a Codex orchestrator's native question (decision 40, M9.5.5b); FU-F26, why a wake-up waits (decision 41, M9.5.5b); FU-F11, the plan review's alert count (decision 42, M9.5.5b).
- **X2, routing:** FU-F10, onboarding scouts and deciders over what is installed; FU-F24, `run retry` re-picking a failed route (decision 9a, M9.5.10b).
- **X4, small fixes riding protocol 16:** FU-F23, a failed turn in `AgentRoundInfo` (decision 43, M9.5.6); FU-F7, a placeholder headless window (decision 44, M9.5.6); FU-F21, three tier-3 alerts (decision 45, M9.5.6); FU-F36, round-aware limit texts (decision 46, M9.5.6); FU-F39, a later round's pre-warm (decision 47, M9.5.6).
- From 9.2's Out table: excluding human-review time from `bound_ratio_permille` (decision 14, M9.5.12).

Found already fixed by the refresh, marked so in the follow-ups file: FU-F14 (the plan gate shows `owns` and `done when`, M9.0.5), FU-F16 (the two 1 s-margin tests, the `GitBudget` seam), FU-F22 (the STATUS stage labels, M9.0.7 decision 15), FU-F35 (the action menu's round lines, M9.3.10a).

New follow-ups to record under "From milestone 9.5" (task M9.5.22): stub-then-fill; a merge queue wider than 1; coordinating writer caps across runs; rate-limit events from orchestrator, planner, scout and decider sessions (RC-1); the Codex usage limit's reset time (RC-2, FU-F1); phase accounting that excludes downtime (Risk 1); pruning `tuning.toml.bad-*`; Codex filter by hook, if the user's probe has not recorded the facts by then; and every item of the Out table filed for 9.5 that stays open.
