## Spec and earlier-brief defects

Items 1–13 were found while writing the first brief (2026-09-22) and are settled in the spec or the briefs; they are kept for the record. Items 14–19 were found by the 2026-10-03 refresh. Items 20–31 are the minor findings of the 2026-10-03 review of the refreshed brief, each applied; its critical and important findings are the review rulings under "Controller rulings".

1. **§21 against §4.1, §13 and M8a.** §21's M9.5 row lists only refitting and adaptive concurrency; §4.1, §21's scenarios, M8a's Out table and the roadmap put racing and pairing here too. This brief follows the latter.
2. **§13 item 9: "halves `max_writers` for that runtime".** `max_writers` is one number per run; decision 16 adds a per-runtime cap inside it.
3. **§13 item 9 gives no `recover_after`, and §15 no rule for how `min_samples` counts runs.** Decisions 16 and 4 set 10 minutes and a cap of 10 per run (per round since the refresh).
4. **§15's "new line thresholds" have nothing to act on** in the engine; decision 13 routes them to the prompts.
5. **§15 budgets are per task; M8a's budget is per session.** Decision 6 refits from task totals knowingly.
6. **§11.6 "salvages its worktree" against M8a decision 20** (only a dirty worktree is salvaged). Decision 22's `keep_head`.
7. **§4.1 and §19: a test writer has no tool.** Decision 25 reuses `task_done`.
8. **§16.4 "1.2× the bound" is never defined.** Decision 14 defines it.
9. **M8b and M8c both named their smoke stage `11d`.** Resolved in the briefs.
10. **M8c's scout snapshot type differed from M8b's.** Resolved in the briefs.
11. **M8c's `TaskInfo.diffstat` against M8b's `TaskInfo.diff`.** Resolved in the briefs.
12. **M8a's `AgentRole` was `lowercase`.** Resolved: `snake_case` (`proto/src/run.rs:15-20`).
13. **M8a counted a rate-limit event only per retry streak.** Resolved in M8a decision 32.
14. **§11.6's race worktrees are linked worktrees with branches checked out; the code's task checkouts are standalone repositories with detached `HEAD`s** (M8a final fix batches F1b/F1c, `git/checkout.rs:1-31`). Ruling RR-1: each lane is a standalone checkout and the crown is a compare-and-swap with no checkout step (decisions 19, 21).
15. **The crowned lane's imports would move the lane branch, not the task branch.** A checkout's own branch is held in its pin (`worktree/pinned.rs:96`; the pin's `own` is replaced by every later `pin` call, and only a linked worktree's git directory is "never recomputed", M9.5.1 item 9) and `git/import.rs::sync_in` moves exactly that branch. RR-1's crown alone would leave the task branch at the crown head while later commits moved the lane branch. Fix: the crown re-pins the lane checkout's own branch to the task branch in memory, and `Task.branch` becomes the task branch (decision 21).
16. **RR-2's "the escalation polls the group until it is gone" holds for a PTY child (`process::escalate`, `process.rs:25`), not for a headless session**, which racers are: `HeadlessHandle::kill` (`headless/session.rs:268`) sends `SIGTERM` to the group and `SIGKILL` at the grace only if the leader is still unreaped, and reports nothing about the group. Within "no new kill code, signal or probe", decision 22 reads "finished" as the lane's `ProcessExited { killed_by_engine: true }`, and keeps the checkout when it does not arrive within `LANE_EXIT_WAIT_SECS`; Risk 8 records the residual case.
17. **RH-2's "a CI or review fix task whose `fixes` names the task" has no task to name.** `FixOf::Ci` and `FixOf::Review` name a stage and PR threads (`model_stages.rs:262-273`), and `TaskRecord` has no `fixes` field. Decision 4 applies such a fix task's record to every plan task of the same run and stage, the conservative reading.
18. **RH-3's per-(run, round) cap needs the round on a task record**, which `TaskRecord` lacks. Decision 4 adds `TaskRecord.round` (`#[serde(default)]`, no history version bump).
19. **RE-1's "the current round's approval" is not recorded** (`Round` has `started_at` only; `Run.approved_at` is round 1's). Decision 14 adds `Round.approved_at`.
20. **One log line had two shapes** (review M1). Decision 12 named the start line `tuning: budget S 55 calls 18m from 34 samples`, and M9.5.8 the refit-write line `tuning: budget S 40 calls 15m → 55 calls 18m from 34 samples`, without saying which is which. Decision 12 now names both: the refit-write line only when a refit changes `tuning.toml`, the start lines at every start; M9.5.8 tests the first, M9.5.9 the second.
21. **One role spelt two ways** (review M2). `role_name` is "the role as `anthrex mcp --role` … spell[s] it" (`mcp/src/tools.rs:37`), and the flag is `--role test_writer`, so `role_name` gains `test_writer`, not `test writer` (Interfaces "MCP").
22. **The no-kill-change diffs listed different files** (review M3). M9.5.15 and Verification now both check the union: `run/git/worktrees.rs`, `process.rs`, `headless/session.rs`, `manager/headless_turns.rs` and `manager/remove.rs`.
23. **M9.5.1 item 8 left an orphan** (review M4). Killing a `git commit` whose editor is `sleep 30` leaves that `sleep` running unrecorded; the item now says to let it expire and never to kill anything by pattern.
24. **Names and paths that were slightly off** (review M5): `start_of` is `propagate_sync::start_of` (`propagate_sync.rs:59`, `pub(in crate::run::engine)`, imported by `propagate.rs:25`; decision 47); `TokenUsage` is in `proto/src/run_info.rs:38`, not `adapt.rs` (M9.5.4b, Starting point); `engine/tests/rounds_limits.rs` is new (M9.5.6); `build_delivered` starts at `driver/build.rs:264`, and `:307-317` is its `build_run` call (Starting point, decisions 9a and 12 cite both); the hub budget is `validate.rs:216`.
25. **No protocol-15 fixture existed** (review M6): `m912_run_info.json` and `m9_history_v2.jsonl` predate protocol 15. M9.5.2 now first captures `proto/src/m93_run_info.json`, `proto/src/m93_history.jsonl` and `daemon/tests/fixtures/run/m93-run.json` with `c8d77428`'s code, in their own commit; M9.5.2's and M9.5.3b's "old … still decode" tests read them (review focus 7).
26. **Gaps and overlaps in ownership** (review M7). M9.5.2's Files now name the `RunRequest::Stats { dir }` constructors (`tui/src/app/stats.rs`, `cli/src/run_cmd/adapt.rs`); the `--lane` argv belongs to M9.5.2 alone, and M9.5.18 only uses it.
27. **Opening Settings wrote files** (review M8). A plain `Stats` records reverts (git work and a history append, `adapt_history.rs:90-93`) and refits `tuning.toml`. Rather than accept that on every Settings open, `RunRequest::Stats` gains `read_only` (protocol 16 already changes `Stats`), which records and writes nothing; Settings sends it, the stats screen and `run stats` do not (decision 48, M9.5.11).
28. **RH-5's "explicit" is narrower than it read** (review M9). The Settings screen never writes a value equal to its default (`settings/write.rs:307`), so a user pins the defaults against a refit only by editing `config.toml` (decision 3); and `ConfiguredBudgets` has S and M only, so decision 6 states that an explicit `[orchestrator.budget.m]` also holds hub tasks, which use M's budget today (`validate.rs:216`).
29. **Budgets were tight** (review M10). `driver/ops.rs` (541) now takes match arms only (+20 across M9.5.15 and M9.5.18), the executors going to a new `driver/lane_ops.rs`; `engine/ladder.rs` (553), grown by four tasks, and `run/contract.rs` (595), grown by decision 26's notes, are split move-only first in M9.5.3 (`engine/ladder_budget.rs`, `run/contract_clamp.rs`), with per-task budgets (File sizes).
30. **The first-turn state was under-specified** (review M11). `RunOrch.first_turn` duplicated `OrchestratorRecord.first_prompt` (`orch/mod.rs:148`, copied by `chains::adopt`); the text now stays in `first_prompt` alone, with a persisted `first_turn_pending` flag, and a relaunch delivers `first_prompt`. `mcp_ready` stays `#[serde(skip)]`: after a daemon restart the window is dormant and `run resume` relaunches it with a new `anthrex mcp`, which sends its own `McpReady`, so no first turn waits on a notice that cannot come (decision 38).
31. **The adoption crown had no defined head** (review M12). A lane taken out before any gate has no imported head. Decision 20 now crowns an adopted lane at its last imported `head`, else its `start_commit` (the stage head its checkout was prepared from), the next import moving the task branch on; a lane whose checkout was never prepared is not crowned, and the task goes `blocked(environment)`. Test `adoption_crowns_at_the_lanes_last_head` (M9.5.17b).

**8. git** (Apple Git 2.50.1, `/usr/bin/git`; temporary repositories under the scratchpad's `m951-git/`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`; removed afterwards by its literal path). The facts decisions 21–22 rest on hold, with three corrections for the tests.
- `git update-ref refs/heads/x <sha> 0000000000000000000000000000000000000000` with `x` absent → exit 0, `x` at `<sha>`. Again with `x` present → exit 128, `fatal: update_ref failed for ref 'refs/heads/x': cannot lock ref 'refs/heads/x': reference already exists`. **The message and exit are the same whether the branch is already at `<sha>` or at another commit** (checked with `y` at a second commit). So `git::crown` cannot tell `Crowned` from `RefMoved` by the failure: on a failed create it reads the ref (`read_ref`) and returns `Crowned` when it equals the lane head, `RefMoved` otherwise. M9.5.15's tests cover both (ruling T1-2, now in decision 21 and M9.5.15's `crown_is_idempotent_and_refuses_a_moved_branch`).
- `git rev-parse --show-object-format` → `sha1`. `git init --object-format=sha256 s256` → exit 0; a commit there has a 64-character id, and `update-ref refs/heads/x <head> <64 zeros>` creates (exit 0) then fails with the same `reference already exists`. The 64-zero form is right for sha256.
- **The lock experiment needs a different editor and commit on macOS.** In a checkout made like `checkout::ensure` (own git dir `data/runs/r1/tasks/t1/git` with `objects/info/alternates` → the user's store, the same `config` text, `HEAD` a detached sha, no refs, `.git` file; `git for-each-ref | wc -l` → 0): `GIT_EDITOR='sleep 30' git commit` fails at once, `sleep: invalid time interval: …/git/COMMIT_EDITMSG` then `error: there was a problem with the editor 'sleep 30'` (git appends the message file, and BSD `sleep` rejects a second operand), so the experiment used `GIT_EDITOR='sleep 30 #'`. Results, each `git` started in the background with its pid recorded (`$!`), killed by that pid only:

  | Commit | Lock files in the checkout's own git dir while the editor runs | After `SIGKILL` to the `git` pid | After `SIGTERM` |
  |--|--|--|--|
  | `git add b.txt; git commit` | none (waited 10 s) | none | none |
  | `git commit -a` | `index.lock` | `index.lock` left (exit 137) | removed (exit 143) |
  | `git commit -- a.txt` | `index.lock`, `next-index-<pid>.lock` | both left (exit 137) | both removed (exit 143) |

  In every case nothing appeared under the user repository's `.git` (`find user/.git -name '*.lock' | wc -l` → 0), and the editor (`sleep`, a child of the `git`) was left running reparented to pid 1; it was not signalled, expired on its own, and `ps -A` showed no `sleep 30` before the directory was removed. So: a plain commit of staged changes takes no index lock while the editor runs; a test that needs a held `index.lock` uses `git commit -a` (or `--only` paths). A partial commit also leaves `next-index-<pid>.lock`, which `clear_stale_locks` (only `index.lock` and `HEAD.lock`) does not remove; it is harmless (git never waits on another pid's `next-index`, and it is inside the git dir, not the work tree), so decision 22 is unchanged and the leftover is noted under Risks 8 by M9.5.15 if it wants it named.
- Decisions 21–22 stand; nothing in item 8 failed.

**9. Pins.** Where `PinAs.own` comes from, and what else holds a checkout's own branch.
- **`PrepareWorktree`.** `engine/dispatch_launch.rs::prepare` (`:31-46`) emits `OpKind::PrepareWorktree { branch: task.branch.clone(), path: task.worktree.clone(), .. }` (`engine/ops.rs:30-37`); `driver/ops.rs:201-221` calls `git::prepare_task_worktree(g, &root, &branch, &from, &at, &repo, t)` with `repo = checkout_repo_dir(&ctx.data_dir, &path)`; `git/worktrees.rs::ensure_task_worktree` (`:256-288`) sets `own = refs/heads/{branch}` and calls `checkout::ensure(.., Kind::Task { own: &own })`, which pins with `PinAs { own, objects, engine, repo: Some(git_dir), .. }` (`checkout.rs:201-214`). `prepare` runs at dispatch, at pre-warm (`dispatch_launch.rs:83`), and again for a started task that waited (`dispatch.rs:245`, `dispatch_launch.rs:131,150`).
- **A restart.** `driver/restore.rs:88` calls `git::pin_worktrees(&snapshot.git_common_dir, &run_worktree_paths(&snapshot))`; `run_worktree_paths` (`restore.rs:347-381`) pins, per task, `task.worktree` with `own = refs/heads/{task.branch}` (and its objects, engine and git dirs), plus `run.review_path(task.id())` and `run.proof_path(task.id())` read-only. So a restart takes `own` from the persisted `Task.branch`, as decision 21 assumes.
- **"Never recomputed" is about the git directory only.** `pinned::pin` (`pinned.rs:96-148`) builds a new `Pin` from the `PinAs` it is given and **replaces** the entry on every call (`:147`), for a linked worktree and a standalone checkout alike; only a linked worktree's `git_dir` is reused from the earlier pin (`:122-129`). `own` is whatever the latest caller passed. So `repin_own` holds until the next `pin` of that checkout, which is any later `PrepareWorktree` (it passes `task.branch`) or a restart (it passes `task.branch`). Both are right after the crown only if the crown also makes `Task.branch` the task branch, as decision 21 says.
- **Facts the race tasks must add** (no restart path is missing, but two are lane-blind today; ruling T1-3 assigns them: the restart re-pin to M9.5.15, `Task.worktree` to M9.5.17a, the racer's prompt to M9.5.18, each with its test there):
  - `run_worktree_paths` pins only `task.worktree` and the task-id `.review`/`.proof` paths. Lane checkouts `<task>.a`/`<task>.b` and their `.proof`/`.review` are never pinned at a restart, and `import::task_pin` refuses an unpinned task checkout (`import.rs:67-69`). M9.5.15 adds every live lane (own `anthrex/<run>/<task>.<lane>` before the crown; the crowned lane under the task branch after it) to `run_worktree_paths`, with a restart test.
  - The checkout is identified by the persisted `Task.worktree: PathBuf` (`model_task.rs:47`), not only by `task.id()`: 36 non-test files under `run/` read `task.worktree` or `t.worktree` (prepare, review, merge, hand-back, salvage, reconcile, `checkout_repo_dir(&run.data_dir, &task.worktree)`), against five calls of the id-keyed path helpers (`role_launch.rs:138,225,232,258`, `driver/ops.rs:143`). RR-1's `Task::checkout_name()` therefore also has to drive `Task.worktree`: the crown and adoption set `task.worktree = run.task_path(checkout_name)` together with `task.branch`, or every `task.worktree` site goes through the helper. M9.5.15/M9.5.17a decide which; the first is the smaller change.
  - `worker_prompt` prints `Worktree: {task.worktree}` and `Branch: {task.branch}` (`run/contract.rs:105-109`), so a racer given the task's prompt unchanged would be told the task's (not yet existing) checkout and branch. Decision 19's "with its lane's checkout" needs the lane's path and branch in those two lines (M9.5.17a/M9.5.18).
- Nothing else caches the own branch: `pin.own` is read by `import.rs:69,123,477` (`sync_in`), `git/done.rs:166` and `merge_state.rs:249` (`own_ref`), all through `pinned::pinned(worktree)`; the engine's ops carry `task.branch` as it is when the op is emitted.

**10. The headless kill** (from code; nothing run, nothing changed).
- `manager/headless_turns.rs:547-552` `headless_kill` (fn at `:547`): records `Cancel::Killed`, marks the session ending, `handle.kill(self.config.kill_grace)`; production `kill_grace` is `process::KILL_GRACE` = 3 s (`manager/config.rs:130`, `process.rs:7`).
- `headless/session.rs:266-297` `HeadlessHandle::kill(grace)`: closes stdin; `SIGTERM` to the group unless the leader is already reaped; a thread waits on `process.exited` until `reaped` or `grace`, and sends `SIGKILL` to the group at the deadline if the leader is still unreaped. Returns at once.
- `headless/session/pipes.rs:118-171` `wait_leader`: sees the leader exit with `waitid(WNOWAIT)`, **sends `SIGKILL` to whatever is left of the group while the leader is still an unreaped zombie** (`:145-147`), then reaps it and notifies. So no group member outlives the leader's reaping; Risk 8's residual case is narrower than written (only a process that left the group can survive), but the group `SIGKILL` can catch a `git` still in its own `SIGTERM` clean-up, so `clear_locks` after the exit stays needed.
- `pipes.rs:174-253` (the dispatcher): `ProcessExited` is the session's last event, after every stdout/stderr line, or `OUTPUT_GRACE` (1 s, `run/exec.rs:50`) after the exit when an escaped process holds a pipe.
- The driver: `kill_effect` (`driver/effects.rs:329-351`) calls `kill_window` (`driver.rs:547-556`: records `book.killed[window] = pid`, then `headless_kill`); when the window has no live process it sends a synthetic `ProcessExited { killed_by_engine: true, pid: <round pid or 0> }` at once. Otherwise the real exit arrives through the manager feed, and `mark_killed` (`driver.rs:403-434`) sets `killed_by_engine` when the recorded pid matches.
- So `ProcessExited { killed_by_engine: true }` reaches the engine after `KillWindow`: at once for a window between turns; else when the leader exits on `SIGTERM`, or at the latest `kill_grace` (3 s) plus `OUTPUT_GRACE` (1 s) plus queueing, unless the leader cannot die (uninterruptible sleep), when it never comes. `LANE_EXIT_WAIT_SECS` = kill grace + 30 s covers every case but the last, which is the "kept" path.

**4–6. For the user to run** (agents never run the real `codex`; SAFETY.md). Until the outputs are recorded here, the fallbacks hold: `codex_filter = Instruction` (items 4–5) and footer detection (item 6).

Everything happens under `/tmp/ax-m95-codex/` (mode 700): a scratch repository and a standalone checkout made the way `run::git::checkout::ensure` makes one, the hook scripts, and the outputs. Codex runs with `CODEX_HOME=/tmp/ax-m95-codex/codex-home`, a separate login you make there yourself in Step 0 (ruling T1-4): the probe never reads, copies or writes `~/.codex`, so your real login and its refresh token are never used (a copied ChatGPT login would rotate the refresh token and log the real `codex` out). The script refuses to start while that home holds no login. The environment is scrubbed (`env -i` with `HOME PATH USER LOGNAME SHELL TERM LANG`), as the committed `codex-*` fixtures were captured. Every run is `codex exec` with `-s workspace-write` (on resume `-c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"`, since `exec resume` rejects `-s`), `-c approval_policy="never"`, network off, and (except run 4c) anthrex's `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR` exclusions; no `--dangerously-*` flag anywhere. The hook is passed exactly as `launch/codex.rs` passes one: `-c hooks.PreToolUse=[{hooks=[{type="command",command="<script>"}]}]` and `-c hooks.state={"/<session-flags>/config.toml:pre_tool_use:0:0"={trusted_hash="<trust_hash("pre_tool_use", <script>)>"}}`, the hash computed by the same canonical-JSON SHA-256 (checked against the test vector in `launch/codex.rs:282`: `sha256:cb3a9626…ad7e`). No `codex_user_config_only` flag is passed (`CLI_CAPS` has none); run 4 records `codex exec --help` so the flag can be found if the CLI has one. The script was run end to end against a stub `codex` (`AX_BASE`/`AX_CODEX` overrides, an empty fake `HOME`, used only for that test) before being written here: no argument or a wrong one prints the usage and creates nothing; a missing or empty `codex-home` refuses with the login command; every directory it makes is mode 700; `pack` writes only the named tarball; Step 5's logout and removals leave nothing, and a logout with no session does not stop them. `native_codex`'s search for the CLI's native binary is bounded (`find … -maxdepth 4`).

Step 0, once: log in to Codex inside the isolated home (a new, separate session; it opens the usual browser login): `mkdir -p -m 700 /tmp/ax-m95-codex /tmp/ax-m95-codex/codex-home && CODEX_HOME=/tmp/ax-m95-codex/codex-home codex login` (both directories mode 700, so the separate login is readable by you alone).

Step 1, once: write the probe script.

```sh
mkdir -p -m 700 /tmp/ax-m95-codex && cat > /tmp/ax-m95-codex/probe.sh <<'AXEOF'
#!/bin/sh
# anthrex M9.5.1 items 4-6: Codex facts only the real CLI can give. Run by the user.
# Usage: sh /tmp/ax-m95-codex/probe.sh 4|5|6|pack
# Everything lives under $B. No --dangerously-* flag; approval_policy never with the
# workspace-write sandbox, network off. codex runs with CODEX_HOME=$B/codex-home, a
# separate login the user made there first (CODEX_HOME=$B/codex-home codex login);
# nothing reads or writes ~/.codex, and the real login's tokens are never used.
set -u
B=${AX_BASE:-/tmp/ax-m95-codex}
CODEX=${AX_CODEX:-codex}
OUT=$B/out
CO=$B/wt/t1
PY=$(command -v python3) || { echo "python3 is required"; exit 1; }
SRC='/<session-flags>/config.toml'

setup() {
  umask 077
  mkdir -p -m 700 "$B" "$B/bin" "$OUT"
  if [ ! -f "$B/user/.git/HEAD" ]; then
    export GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1
    git init -q -b main "$B/user"
    echo one > "$B/user/a.txt"
    git -C "$B/user" add a.txt
    GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=ax GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL=ax@example.invalid GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=ax \
      GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL=ax@example.invalid git -C "$B/user" commit -qm init
  fi
  if [ ! -f "$CO/.git" ]; then
    # A standalone checkout made the way run::git::checkout::ensure makes one: its own
    # git dir (private objects, the user's store as alternate), detached HEAD, no refs.
    common=$B/user/.git
    repo=$B/data/tasks/t1
    sha=$(git -C "$B/user" rev-parse HEAD)
    mkdir -p "$repo/git/objects/info" "$repo/git/objects/pack" "$repo/git/refs/heads" \
      "$repo/git/refs/tags" "$repo/git/info" "$repo/engine" "$CO"
    cop=$(cd "$CO" && pwd -P)
    printf '[core]\n\trepositoryformatversion = 0\n\thooksPath = "%s"\n[include]\n\tpath = "%s"\n[core]\n\tbare = false\n\tworktree = "%s"\n\tlogAllRefUpdates = false\n[gc]\n\tauto = 0\n' \
      "$common/hooks" "$common/config" "$cop" > "$repo/git/config"
    echo "$common/objects" > "$repo/git/objects/info/alternates"
    echo "$sha" > "$repo/git/HEAD"
    echo "gitdir: $repo/git" > "$CO/.git"
    GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 git -C "$CO" reset -q --hard HEAD
  fi
}

# trust_hash(label, command) exactly as daemon/src/launch/codex.rs computes it.
th() {
  "$PY" -c 'import hashlib,json,sys; print("sha256:"+hashlib.sha256(json.dumps({"event_name":sys.argv[1],"hooks":[{"async":False,"command":sys.argv[2],"timeout":600,"type":"command"}]},sort_keys=True,separators=(",",":"),ensure_ascii=False).encode()).hexdigest())' "$1" "$2"
}

# The two -c values that give Codex a trusted PreToolUse hook running $1.
hook_flags() {
  printf '%s\n' "hooks.PreToolUse=[{hooks=[{type=\"command\",command=\"$1\"}]}]"
  printf '%s\n' "hooks.state={\"$SRC:pre_tool_use:0:0\"={trusted_hash=\"$(th pre_tool_use "$1")\"}}"
}

# codex with a scrubbed environment (as the committed fixtures were captured).
cx() {
  env -i HOME="$HOME" PATH="$PATH" USER="${USER:-}" LOGNAME="${LOGNAME:-}" SHELL="${SHELL:-/bin/sh}" \
    TERM="${TERM:-xterm}" LANG="${LANG:-en_US.UTF-8}" CODEX_HOME="$B/codex-home" "$CODEX" "$@"
}

thread_of() {
  "$PY" -c 'import json,sys
for l in open(sys.argv[1]):
    try: e=json.loads(l)
    except Exception: continue
    if e.get("type")=="thread.started": print(e["thread_id"]); break' "$1"
}

# exec (or exec resume when $2 is a thread id) in the checkout with hook command $1.
# $3: "pins" adds anthrex's sandbox pins (no /tmp, no $TMPDIR, no network).
run_exec() {
  hook=$1 thread=$2 pins=$3 prompt=$4
  f1=$(hook_flags "$hook" | sed -n 1p)
  f2=$(hook_flags "$hook" | sed -n 2p)
  if [ "$pins" = pins ]; then
    set -- -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false \
      -c sandbox_workspace_write.exclude_tmpdir_env_var=true \
      -c sandbox_workspace_write.exclude_slash_tmp=true
  else
    set -- -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false
  fi
  if [ -z "$thread" ]; then
    (cd "$CO" && cx exec --json -s workspace-write -c 'approval_policy="never"' \
      -c 'model_reasoning_effort="low"' "$@" -c "$f1" -c "$f2" -- "$prompt")
  else
    (cd "$CO" && cx exec resume "$thread" --json -c 'sandbox_mode="workspace-write"' \
      -c 'approval_policy="never"' -c 'model_reasoning_effort="low"' "$@" -c "$f1" -c "$f2" -- "$prompt")
  fi
}

native_codex() {
  r=$("$PY" -c 'import os,sys;print(os.path.realpath(sys.argv[1]))' "$(command -v "$CODEX")")
  if file "$r" | grep -q Mach-O; then echo "$r"; return; fi
  find "$(dirname "$r")/.." -maxdepth 4 -type f -name codex 2>/dev/null | while read -r f; do
    file "$f" | grep -q Mach-O && { echo "$f"; break; }
  done
}

info() {
  {
    echo "== codex --version"; cx --version
    echo "== codex exec --help"; cx exec --help
    echo "== codex exec resume --help"; cx exec resume --help
    echo "== codex features list"; cx features list
  } > "$OUT/info.txt" 2>&1
}

item4() {
  hook=$B/bin/log-pretooluse.sh
  printf '#!/bin/sh\n{ cat; echo; } >> "%s"\nexit 0\n' "$OUT/4-pretooluse.jsonl" > "$hook"
  chmod 700 "$hook"
  hook_flags "$hook" > "$OUT/4-flags.txt"
  run_exec "$hook" "" pins "Run the shell command ls in the current directory, then reply with its output." \
    > "$OUT/4a-exec.jsonl" 2> "$OUT/4a-exec.stderr"
  echo "4a exit $?" >> "$OUT/4-exit.txt"
  t=$(thread_of "$OUT/4a-exec.jsonl")
  echo "thread $t" >> "$OUT/4-exit.txt"
  echo '--- resume' >> "$OUT/4-pretooluse.jsonl"
  if [ -n "$t" ]; then
    run_exec "$hook" "$t" pins "Run the shell command ls -a, then reply with its output." \
      > "$OUT/4b-resume.jsonl" 2> "$OUT/4b-resume.stderr"
    echo "4b exit $?" >> "$OUT/4-exit.txt"
  fi
  echo '--- no /tmp pins' >> "$OUT/4-pretooluse.jsonl"
  run_exec "$hook" "" nopins "Run the shell command ls in the current directory, then reply with its output." \
    > "$OUT/4c-exec-nopins.jsonl" 2> "$OUT/4c-exec-nopins.stderr"
  echo "4c exit $?" >> "$OUT/4-exit.txt"
}

item5() {
  cat > "$B/bin/rewrite.py" <<'PYEOF'
import json, sys
shape, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
raw = sys.stdin.read()
with open(out + "/5-" + shape + "-payload.jsonl", "a") as f:
    f.write(raw.strip() + "\n")
payload = json.loads(raw)
new = "echo AX_REPLACED_%s > ax-marker-%s.txt && echo AX_REPLACED_%s" % (shape, shape, shape)
upd = dict(payload.get("tool_input") or {})
for key in ("command", "cmd"):
    if key in upd:
        v = upd[key]
        if isinstance(v, list):
            upd[key] = v[:-1] + [new] if len(v) >= 3 and v[-2] in ("-lc", "-c") else ["bash", "-lc", new]
        else:
            upd[key] = new
answers = {
    "S1": {"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "updatedInput": upd}},
    "S2": {"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "permissionDecision": "allow", "updatedInput": upd}},
    "S3": {"updatedInput": upd},
    "S4": {"hook_specific_output": {"hook_event_name": "PreToolUse", "updated_input": upd}},
}
answer = json.dumps(answers[shape])
with open(out + "/5-" + shape + "-answer.json", "a") as f:
    f.write(answer + "\n")
print(answer)
PYEOF
  for s in S1 S2 S3 S4; do
    hook=$B/bin/rewrite-$s.sh
    printf '#!/bin/sh\nexec "%s" "%s" %s "%s"\n' "$PY" "$B/bin/rewrite.py" "$s" "$OUT" > "$hook"
    chmod 700 "$hook"
    run_exec "$hook" "" pins "Run the shell command ls in the current directory, then reply with its output." \
      > "$OUT/5-$s-exec.jsonl" 2> "$OUT/5-$s-exec.stderr"
    echo "$s exit $?" >> "$OUT/5-exit.txt"
  done
  ls -la "$CO" > "$OUT/5-checkout-files.txt" 2>&1
  n=$(native_codex)
  echo "native binary: $n" > "$OUT/5-strings.txt"
  [ -n "$n" ] && strings -a "$n" | grep -E 'updatedInput|updated_input|hookSpecificOutput|hook_specific_output|permissionDecision|permission_decision' \
    | cut -c1-300 | sort -u | head -200 >> "$OUT/5-strings.txt"
}

item6() {
  n=$(native_codex)
  {
    echo "native binary: $n"
    echo "== codex features list"; cx features list
    echo "== strings"
    [ -n "$n" ] && strings -a "$n" | grep -iE 'request_user_input|ask_user|user_question|askuserquestion|questions?\b' \
      | grep -iE 'feature|tool|config|enable|disable|request_user_input|ask_user' | cut -c1-300 | sort -u | head -300
  } > "$OUT/6-question-tool.txt" 2>&1
}

# The isolated home must hold its own login: never ~/.codex's.
need_login() {
  if [ -d "$B/codex-home" ]; then
    if [ -f "$B/codex-home/auth.json" ] || cx login status > /dev/null 2>&1; then
      return 0
    fi
  fi
  echo "No Codex login in $B/codex-home. Run this first, then try again:"
  echo "  mkdir -p -m 700 $B $B/codex-home && CODEX_HOME=$B/codex-home codex login"
  exit 1
}

case "${1:-}" in
  4|5|6) ;;
  pack)
    tar czf "$HOME/Desktop/ax-m95-codex-out.tgz" -C "$B" out && echo "wrote $HOME/Desktop/ax-m95-codex-out.tgz"
    exit ;;
  *) echo "usage: sh $B/probe.sh 4|5|6|pack"; exit 2 ;;
esac
need_login
setup
info
case "$1" in
  4) item4 ;;
  5) item5 ;;
  6) item6 ;;
esac
echo "done: item $1; outputs in $OUT"
AXEOF
```

Step 2, item 4 (does a `PreToolUse` hook fire in `codex exec` and `codex exec resume`, with what payload): `sh /tmp/ax-m95-codex/probe.sh 4`. It runs three turns that each ask Codex to run `ls`: 4a a fresh `exec`, 4b `exec resume` of 4a's thread, 4c a fresh `exec` without the `/tmp` exclusions (in case the hook itself runs inside the sandbox and cannot write its log under `/tmp`). **Send back** the whole `/tmp/ax-m95-codex/out/` directory, packed by `sh /tmp/ax-m95-codex/probe.sh pack` into exactly `~/Desktop/ax-m95-codex-out.tgz`: `info.txt` (version and help texts), `4-pretooluse.jsonl` (each payload the hook saw, `--- resume` and `--- no /tmp pins` separating the runs), `4a-exec.jsonl`, `4b-resume.jsonl`, `4c-exec-nopins.jsonl` with their `.stderr`, and `4-exit.txt`. From them: whether the hook fires in each, the payload (it becomes `daemon/tests/fixtures/headless/codex-<version>-pretooluse.jsonl` with a `.meta.json`), the shell tool's `tool_name` values (`codex_shell_tool_names`) and the JSON pointer to its command (`codex_command_pointer`).

Step 3, item 5 (which hook output makes Codex run a replaced command): `sh /tmp/ax-m95-codex/probe.sh 5`. Four fresh `exec` turns, each with a hook that answers one shape and replaces the command with `echo AX_REPLACED_<shape> > ax-marker-<shape>.txt && echo AX_REPLACED_<shape>`: S1 `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","updatedInput":…}}` (anthrex's Claude shape), S2 the same with `"permissionDecision":"allow"`, S3 a top-level `{"updatedInput":…}`, S4 the snake-case `{"hook_specific_output":{"hook_event_name":"PreToolUse","updated_input":…}}`; `updatedInput` is the payload's `tool_input` with `command` (or `cmd`) replaced (for an argv list, its last element after `-lc`/`-c`). It also greps the native `codex` binary's strings for those key names. **Send back** the same `out/` directory again, packed the same way (now with `5-<shape>-payload.jsonl`, `5-<shape>-answer.json`, `5-<shape>-exec.jsonl`, `5-exit.txt`, `5-checkout-files.txt`, which lists any `ax-marker-*` file, and `5-strings.txt`). A shape works when its `command_execution.command` in `5-<shape>-exec.jsonl` is the replaced one and its marker file exists; that sets `CodexFilter::Hook` and the shape `codex_rewrite` prints.

Step 4, item 6 (can Codex's own user-question tool be turned off for an interactive session): `sh /tmp/ax-m95-codex/probe.sh 6`. It records `codex features list` and the native binary's strings that name a question or user-input tool or feature. **Send back** `out/6-question-tool.txt` and `out/info.txt` (or the packed tarball), and, if you know it, the key that turns the tool off (and whether the `? 1 question` footer then stops appearing in an interactive `codex` session).

Step 5, clean-up, after the outputs are recorded: `CODEX_HOME=/tmp/ax-m95-codex/codex-home codex logout || true; rm -rf /tmp/ax-m95-codex && rm -f "$HOME/Desktop/ax-m95-codex-out.tgz"`. The logout ends the separate session (it touches only the isolated home; `|| true` lets clean-up go on when that session is already gone); then the isolated home goes with everything else under `/tmp/ax-m95-codex`, and only that one tarball is removed from the Desktop. (The isolated session stays listed in your ChatGPT account's devices until it expires or you sign it out there.)

**Deviations from the brief.** Items 4–6 are written, not run (SAFETY.md; the task's addenda). Item 8 used `GIT_EDITOR='sleep 30 #'` and `git commit -a` / `git commit -- <path>` besides the plain commit, because on macOS the brief's literal `GIT_EDITOR='sleep 30'` fails before git takes a lock, and a plain commit of staged changes holds no lock while its editor runs. Item 3 added two move-only splits to M9.5.3 (`config/src/orchestrator.rs` → `orchestrator/budget.rs`, `run/driver.rs` → `driver/stop.rs`), as the item directs. Decision 38's "tools rule" did not exist in `ORCHESTRATOR_CONTRACT`; ruling T1-1 puts the retry sentence at the end of rule 4 (`Call get_context … first`, `orch/contract.rs:44`), applied in decision 38 and M9.5.5a.

### Task M9.5.2 (2026-10-03): protocol 16

- **The protocol-15 fixtures** were captured first, in their own commit (`test: capture protocol-15 fixtures`), on `30c08821` (whose `crates/` are byte-identical to `c8d77428`), by a throwaway `#[ignore]` engine test run once and deleted: the engine fixture's run with `t1` (S, tdd) driven through `task_done`, `VerifyDone`, `Proof`, `Check`, `PrepareReview` and an approving review to the merge queue, and `t2` (M) with its `VerifyDone` pending. `m93-run.json` is that `Run`, `m93_run_info.json` its `snapshot::snapshot` `RunInfo`, and `m93_history.jsonl` `history::task_record(t1)` and `goal_rounds::end_round`'s `round` line. The run is in round 1, so the snapshot has no `rounds` list (9.3 leaves a single round out); its "rounds" are the tasks' agent rounds (`t1` has a worker and a reviewer round).
- **New fields left out while unset** (beyond the brief's `TaskInfo.{race, pair}`): `PlanTask.{race, pair}` while false, `RunRef.lane`, `AgentRoundInfo.{lane, failed_error, failed_until}` and `ReviewInfo.lane` while `None`, `RunInfo.writer_caps` while empty, `FullInfo.held` and `WindowInfo.placeholder` while false; and in `TuningFile`, its empty tables and `ClassBudget.tokens` when `None`. Every one is still `#[serde(default)]`, so decoding is as the brief says; the skip keeps a protocol-15 snapshot, window list, `run.json` and plan re-serialising byte for byte (`delivery_tests.rs::old_snapshot_and_profile_still_decode` and `messages.rs::windows_changed_carries_the_project_through_messagepack` pin that), as 9.2 did for `delivery` and `pr`, and keeps `SNAPSHOT_BOUND`'s sizes unchanged.
- **`HistoryStats.tuning` is `Option<Box<TuningReport>>`**, not `Option<TuningReport>`: unboxed, clippy 1.99's `large_enum_variant` fails `DaemonMsg` (328 bytes against 124). A `Box` is invisible on the wire.
- **File-size budgets overrun** (File sizes table, whole-milestone growth; the table now states what is left for later tasks so none passes 600): `proto/src/run.rs` 557→581 (+24 of +20), `run_info.rs` 438→464 (+26 of +12), `run_wire.rs` 405→425 (+20 of +10), `history.rs` 398→418 (+20 of +12), `types.rs` 439→445 (+6 of +3), `tiers.rs` 106→110 (+4 of +3), mostly doc comments on the new fields; `cli/src/mcp_cmd.rs` 111→154 (+43 of +12: the lane check and `LaneArg`); `mcp/src/tools.rs` 283→303 (+20 of +10, 15 of them its inline test). Three test files reached exactly 600 through the new fields' literal defaults: `daemon/src/headless/argv_tests.rs` (596), `daemon/src/run/history_tests.rs` (595), `proto/src/delivery_tests.rs` (600, its 9.5 comment folded into the old one); and two reached 599 (`tui/src/inspector/run_task_outcome_tests.rs`, `tui/src/ui/tests.rs`). None of them takes more growth.
- **Minimum arms** for the two new roles, until their tasks: `mcp::tools_for` gives them the worker's three tools (the brief's interface); `orch::tools::parse_call` lets them call `task_note` as a worker does; `snapshot::run_usage` credits their usage to `worker`; `orch::roles::role_name` spells `racer` and `test_writer`. TUI: `round_glyph` and the round inspector's fields treat them as workers; `round_label` gives `racer` / `racer r<n>` (M9.5.20 adds the lane) and `test writer #<s>` / `test writer #<s> r<n>`; `rounds_with` keeps each as one display round and `role_rank` puts them last (M9.5.20 splits and orders them).
- **`not available yet`.** `validate::check_fields` refuses `race = true` and `pair = true` (`task <id>: race: not available yet`, rule `4.1`), which covers a plan start, `edit_plan`'s `add_task`, and `amend_task`, which writes `race`/`pair` into the spec before validating it as today (`race_and_pair_are_not_available_yet`, `amend_race_or_pair_is_not_available_yet`). `RunRequest::McpReady` is answered `Done` under `mcp ready` with an empty message and changes nothing; `Stats`' `apply`, `dismiss` and `read_only` are ignored (`daemon/tests/server_runs.rs::mcp_ready_is_answered_and_new_stats_fields_are_ignored`). `history::task_record` writes the new `TaskRecord` fields' defaults (`round: 0`); decision 30's task fills them.
- **Test files.** `tuning_tests.rs` is split in two (`tuning_tests_wire.rs` holds `every_new_or_changed_message_round_trips`, `old_messages_decode_with_defaults` and `appended_variants_keep_their_indices`) to stay under 600 lines. `headless/argv_lane_tests.rs` is registered from `argv.rs`, not `argv_tests.rs`, which the new fields' defaults bring to exactly 600. The `forward` test is `mcp/tests/stdio.rs::a_racer_call_carries_its_lane`. The earlier appended-variant tests (`orch_tests.rs`, `orch_tests_detail.rs`, `settings_tests.rs`, `delivery_tests.rs`, `rounds_tests.rs`) now expect `Racer`, `TestWriter` and `McpReady` last.

### Task M9.5.3 (2026-10-03): move-only splits

The crate's whole lib suite passed after each commit.

Edits beyond moving, all of the kind the brief allows (visibility or a re-export), recorded because the brief names only one direction:
- **Old-file items the moved code calls became `pub(super)`**: `review.rs` `give_up`, `drop_mail`; `done.rs` `reply`, `REPLACED`, `session_window`, `rejection`, `spill_exempt`; `ladder.rs` `breach`, `rung4`.
- **Callers are untouched; the old file re-exports**: `merge.rs` re-exports `handed_back`, `hand_back_due`, `start_due_hand_backs` (callers use `merge::…`); `review.rs` `exited`, `resume_failed`, `turn_ended`, `watch`; `done.rs` `checked`; `ladder.rs` `breached`, `ceiling`, `check_budget`, `reached` (`pub(super)`) and `round_spend`, `total_spend` (`pub(crate)`); `engine` `AgentSignal`, `EventKind`; `contract.rs` the four `pub` clamp items and `floor_boundary` (`pub(crate)`).

Not run here (the test-scope policy: the full suite and smoke run once before the PR): `cargo test --workspace` and `scripts/pty-smoke.py`. Run: `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo +1.99 clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (host and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`), `cargo fmt --all --check`, the three crates' lib suites, `anthrex-tui`'s integration tests, and `anthrex-daemon`'s `lifecycle`, `server_runs` and `server_restore_git` (the stop and restore paths `driver/stop.rs` serves).

### Task M9.5.3b (2026-10-03): the daemon model for race, pair and concurrency

`run/model_tuning.rs` (89 lines, pure: the Verification grep prints nothing) holds `RuntimeConcurrency`, `Lane`, `Race` and `Pair` with the Interfaces' fields; `model.rs` re-exports it (`#[path] mod tuning; pub use tuning::*;`). The fields: `Task.{race, pair, race_wait_since}` (`model_task.rs` 211→220), `Run.concurrency` and `PendingOp.lane` (`model.rs` 466→477, +11 of the milestone's +12), `AgentRound`, `CheckRecord`, `ProofRecord` and `ReviewRecord`'s `lane` (`model_rounds.rs` 266→278). Each is `#[serde(default)]` with `skip_serializing_if` (`Option::is_none`, `BTreeMap::is_empty`), so the captured 9.3 `run.json` writes back as the same JSON. Nothing reads them.

- **`model.rs` has +1 line left** of its +12. Decision 15's `Run.{paused_at, paused_secs, last_step_at}` (M9.5.12) need about seven: that task must split something out of `model.rs` first (the `Run` path helpers, `:395-466`, are a candidate) or record the overrun.
- **The new types' own fields are not `#[serde(default)]`**: they are new in 9.5, so no older `run.json` has a `Lane`, `Race`, `Pair` or `RuntimeConcurrency`. A later task adding a field to one of them makes it `#[serde(default)]`.
- **`engine/restore.rs::lost`** destructured `PendingOp` exhaustively; it now takes `op`, `task_id` and `kind` by field, and re-emits a lost op through `emit_op`, which has no lane. A lane's lost op therefore comes back without its lane: the task that routes ops to lanes (M9.5.17a) gives `emit_op` (`engine/mod.rs:362`) the lane and keeps it here.
- **Defaults in literals.** 64 struct literals got `lane: None` (or `concurrency: Default::default()`, or the three `Task` fields `None`): `plan.rs::build_run`, `validate.rs::new_task`, the engine's record builders (`gates.rs`, `merge.rs`, `review.rs`, `rounds.rs`, `tiers.rs`, `engine/mod.rs::emit_op`, `reconcile/mod.rs`'s two tests), and test fixtures in 25 test files. `history_tests.rs` was at 600: its one `ProofRecord` literal moved into `history_tests_fixtures.rs::passed_proof(at)` (same values), so the file went to 590. `delivery/tests.rs` reached 600 exactly (598 + 2) and takes no more growth; `engine/tests/delivery_open.rs` is 597.
- **Tests** (`run/model_tuning_tests.rs`, registered from `model_tuning.rs`): both round-trips go through `journal::save_run` and `journal::load_all`. The "none of the new keys" check counts `lane` rather than looking for it, because a routing decision (milestone 9) already has a `lane` key; it then compares the written JSON with the captured fixture's (only `data_dir` replaced). A mutation dropping `skip_serializing_if` on `ProofRecord.lane` fails `an_old_run_json_still_loads` (lane keys 4 against 3).

### Task M9.5.4a (2026-10-03): history and stats correctness

Decisions 33–36 as written. `detect_reverts` reads the base branch's log oldest first and makes each revert it finds a candidate at once, so one read records a merge's revert and the revert of that revert together (the test's "detect_reverts records both"); a revert record of an accepted run on that branch is a candidate on every later read too. `effective_reverts` returns the records in effect that revert a merge or an accept merge, not another revert: after a revert of a revert it holds none, after a third revert it holds the first (one record, as the test asks). "In effect" is resolved per chain, newest end first, by commit rather than by line order, so a re-appended record cannot reorder it; a cycle (only a hand-edited file can make one) stops at the number of records.

- **No request reaches decision 34's halted-then-discarded case today.** `rules::finish` refuses `run discard` on a halted run unless `run.cancelled`, and `run cancel` on an open round ≥ 2 is always the round's cancel (`complete::cancel` → `cancel_round`), which leaves `run.cancelled` false and the run halted; a second cancel is the round's again. So a halted later round is left by `run reject` (its round ends `rejected`, line written) or by a rebaseline. A run fails only at its start (`requests::run_branch_done`), in round 1. `goal_rounds_end::pass` ends the open round `cancelled` whenever the run is `Discarded` or `Failed`, as decision 34 says; the test (`engine/tests/goal_rounds_end_terminal.rs`) cancels the round through the request, then sets `run.cancelled` in place and discards through the request and op path, and sets `Failed` in place. Whether a halted later round should be discardable is a question for the controller, not changed here.
- **Test locations.** `a_revert_of_a_revert_reinstates_the_task` is in `daemon/tests/history_reverts.rs` (269→330), not a new `run/history_tests_reverts.rs`: `TempRepo` and the `accepted()` repository live in the integration tests' `support`, and a new integration binary would link the daemon again. `profile/store_tests.rs` did not exist (`store.rs` keeps its inline `mod tests`): it is new, registered from `store.rs`. `a_quarantined_test_is_not_proposed` is in the new `driver/adapt_history_tests.rs`, through the new blocking `adapt_history::stats_of` (what `RunService::stats` runs on `spawn_blocking`) against a `git init` repository and a `profile.toml` written in its data directory, so the stored-profile read is covered.
- **Signatures.** `summarise` gains `slow_tests: Option<&str>` and `stats::flaky_proposals` gains `slow_tests: Option<&str>` (the token split: whitespace, `|`, `,`, `(`, `)`, `'`, `"`); every existing caller passes `None` (`stats_tests.rs` and `stats_tests_flaky.rs` keep their lengths; `tests/history_reverts.rs` binds one `testing` for its three calls).
- **Sizes.** `history_io.rs` 417→471 (+54 of +60), `stats.rs` 358→365 (the base was 358, not 357), `goal_rounds_end.rs` 252→258, `profile/store.rs` 437→446. `driver/adapt_history.rs` 123→147: +24 against the task's +10 (the extracted `stats_of` with six parameters, and the test module's registration), within the file table's +30, which leaves +6 for later tasks. The sweep's three extra names are literals in `store.rs` (`WRITTEN_ELSEWHERE`): `driver/graph.rs`'s `GRAPH_CACHE_FILE` is private to the driver and `run/tuning_io.rs` does not exist yet; M9.5.9 may point its `TUNING_FILE` at the same name.
- **Fix round 1 (review m1–m6).** The sweep of the run-written files moved: `ProfileService::restore` runs after `RunService::restore` has spawned the restored ops (`lifecycle.rs`), so its sweep could unlink the temp file of a cache write already in flight. `profile::store::sweep_leftovers` sweeps the profile's own files again, through the new `store::sweep_named(repo_dir, files)`; `run/driver/restore.rs::sweep_run_leftovers` sweeps `CACHE_FILE`, `GRAPH_CACHE_FILE` and `tuning.toml` in every `<data>/repos/*`, in the same blocking step that loads the journals, before any reconcile or op (restore.rs 456→483). Tests in the new `run/driver/restore_sweep_tests.rs` (the brief's sweep test, now through `RunService::restore`, and `the_profile_sweep_leaves_the_run_files_leftovers`); `profile/store_tests.rs` is gone. The stats test's `git init` takes the driver tests' scrubbed environment and `--no-optional-locks`; an unparseable stored profile is logged at `warn` (`adapt_history.rs` 147→150); the terminal round end logs `round <n> ended cancelled: the run is <state>` (`goal_rounds_end.rs` 258→264). A revert record whose first revert's line was skipped as unreadable is still counted as a task's revert (accepted).

### Task M9.5.4b (2026-10-03): orchestrator usage across a chain

Decision 37 as written: `Metered::refresh_live` resolves every run with `continued_by` to its chain's live current run (`chain_end`, bounded by the number of runs, so a hand-made cycle resolves to nothing); the continued run's id is in the live set, its token is the current run's, and `post` offers its totals under the current run's id. `chains::adopt` sets `usage_at_adopt` to the previous run's credit less its base plus its own `usage_at_adopt`; `EventKind::OrchestratorUsage` subtracts it per field (`TokenUsage::saturating_sub`). No lock of `Metered`'s is held across another or across the engine's: `post` takes `via`, releases it, then `pending`.

- **Also dropped in the engine.** `OrchestratorUsage` for a run with `continued_by` is dropped, like one for a terminal run: a total the driver coalesced under the previous run's id before the adoption would otherwise be credited to it and again to the new run (whose `usage_at_adopt` already holds the earlier credit). Tested in `chains_usage.rs::a_delivered_previous_run_is_not_credited`; the driver-level test of the same name covers the resolution.
- **`usage_at_adopt` goes back to zero** when the run's orchestrator is launched (`orch_window::launched`: a session the run launched posts under its own id, from zero) and at a restore (`orch_window::restored`: the new daemon's ledger counts from zero on top of the restored usage, as `orchestrator_base` already assumes). Without these an adopted run whose adoption was lost, or any adopted run after a daemon restart, was under-credited by the old counter. Tests `a_launch_after_a_lost_adoption_counts_from_zero` and `a_restore_drops_the_adoption_point` (each fails with its reset removed). A user's `anthrex restart` of the window keeps it: the ledger key, and its total, carry on.
- **Serialized only when not zero** (`skip_serializing_if`), so a `run.json` without the field round-trips unchanged (`delivery/tests.rs::old_run_json_loads_with_local_delivery`, a 600-line file, needed no edit).
- **`live_orchestrators`** counts only current runs (a continued run's session is its current run's), consistent with "not live itself".
- **Test locations.** As listed; plus the two reset tests above in `engine/tests/chains_usage.rs` (155).
- **Sizes.** `driver/usage.rs` 111→153 (+42 of +40), `usage_tests.rs` 256→342, `engine/chains.rs` 281→288, `engine/mod.rs` 393→396, `engine/orch_window.rs` 236→241, `orch/mod.rs` 495→505, `proto/src/run_info.rs` 464→474 (+10, the whole `saturating_sub` allowance).
- **Open defect, for a ruling (not changed here; fixed in fix round 1, ruling T4b-1).** The OTLP ledger keys a session's total by its launch run and evicts that key (`metering::server::record` → `retain_runs`) on the first request after the run stops being live. Claude's exporter sends **delta** points (M8b's recorded fact, `aggregationTemporality: 1`), so after an eviction the key's total starts again at zero. A delivered `pr` run stays live up to the adoption and then resolves to the new run, so its key is never evicted and decision 37 is exact. An **accepted or discarded** run is terminal, so between its end and the next goal's adoption it is not live, and the first OTLP request in that gap, from any orchestrator (exports run on a 1 s interval), evicts its key: the adopted run's `usage_at_adopt` then holds the previous run's credit while the ledger counts from zero, and the new run is credited nothing until the session has spent that much again. Whether the eviction happens depends on export timing, so the outcome is not deterministic. A fix that keeps decision 37's counter exact: keep the current run of an idle, not-ended chain live for the receiver (with its token), so its key is never evicted between the end and the adoption; the engine already drops its totals as a terminal run's, and the idle spend is credited to the run that adopts the window. That also means counting it in `live_orchestrators` (its exporter would hold a tokened connection). Not done: it changes which runs are live beyond the decision.

**Fix round 1 (review 0C 2I 3m; rulings T4b-1, I1, I2).**
- **T4b-1 and I2.** `Metered::refresh_live` also keeps live the current run of an idle chain that is in the table and has not ended (`idle_current`), with its token and counted once in `live_orchestrators`. `chain_end` resolves to that run even when it is terminal, so every alias of an idle chain stays live too. The engine already drops a terminal run's totals, and the session's spend while the chain is idle goes to the run that adopts it. Tests: `an_idle_chains_session_stays_metered_until_it_is_adopted` (accepted and discarded) and `every_alias_of_an_idle_chain_stays_metered` (three runs). Each runs a delta export between the end and the adoption through a receiver stand-in that evicts as `metering::server::record` does. The stand-in drives the real `engine::step` and `refresh_live`; the adoption is applied by hand with `chains::adopt`'s formula.
- **I1.** `usage_at_adopt` is now `Option<TokenUsage>`, a deviation from the interface's `TokenUsage`. `Some` means the run's session is an adopted one that posts under an earlier run's id. `orch_window::own_session` handles a launch (`launched`), a restart (`restarted`) and a lost adoption (`chains::adopt_lost`): it takes the `Some` and sets `orchestrator_base` to the credit so far. Each of those leaves a session that posts under the run's own id from zero, because `refresh_otlp` and `create_orchestrator` re-key the window. A restore keeps `Some` but zeroes it, since the new daemon's ledger restarts. Tests: `a_restarted_adopted_session_keeps_its_credit` (adopted at 1000, credited 500, restarted, 300 spent → 800) and `a_launch_after_a_lost_adoption_counts_from_zero` (now with 500 credited before the loss → 540).
- **m1.** An alias resolves only while its chain's current run holds an adopted session. The adopt-lost fallback retires the old key at `AdoptLost` itself, not at the later launch. Test: `an_alias_resolves_only_while_the_session_is_adopted`. The remaining coalescing race is Risks item 14.
- **m2.** The invariant comment at `metering/server.rs` (`record`) is restated.
- **Sizes.** `driver/usage.rs` is 171 (+60 against +40; the +42 was accepted, and the idle-chain predicate and alias filter add 18). `usage_tests.rs` is 545, `engine/orch_window.rs` 254, `engine/chains.rs` 290, `orch/mod.rs` 501, `engine/tests/chains_usage.rs` 200, `metering/server.rs` 351.

### Task M9.5.5a (2026-10-03): the orchestrator's first turn

Decision 38 and review ruling C1 as written: every launch (`orch_window::launch`) starts the window with no prompt (`orchestrator_window_spec(run, route)` lost its prompt argument) and sets `first_turn_pending`. Every launch and relaunch resets `mcp_ready` and `first_turn_late` and sets `first_turn_since`. The orchestrator's `anthrex mcp` sends `McpReady` once after its first `tools/list`. While the first turn is pending, `wake::effect` emits nothing but the first turn, and that only once the window is live and `mcp_ready` is set. The driver pastes it only into a window with `signals_seen`, on top of `ready`. Past `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS` the run logs the line once and shows `START_PROMPT`, and nothing is pasted until both conditions hold. A paste's `OrchestratorWoken { first_turn: true }` clears the pending flag, the bound and the line. The relaunch delivers `first_prompt` before any note, the restart's note included. Notes held during the wait follow as a plain wake-up.

- **The driver keeps a first turn as round 0's request wake** (`driver/wake_first_turn.rs`, `FIRST_TURN = 0`; rounds count from 1). 9.3's request machinery then carries it unchanged: a whole paste up to `REQUEST_WAKE_MAX_BYTES`, kept while the engine holds it (`Seen::request` is `FIRST_TURN` while the first turn is pending and `mcp_ready` is set), never dropped by a digest read, and never pasted twice across the `OrchestratorWoken` race. `OrchestratorWoken` gains `first_turn: bool`, which the Interfaces do not list; the brief's "the `OrchestratorWoken` for it clears `first_turn_pending`" needs it. A first-turn wake leaves the notes and `last_wake_rev` alone (`first_turn::woken`).
- **The caller check** (`driver/requests.rs`) uses the manager's own record of the window: its `RunRef` must name the run and the orchestrator role. The engine also accepts the notice while a `CreateOrchestrator` is in flight and the record has no window yet, because the server's notice can arrive before the launch's `Window` result. The reply is always `Done` with an empty message.
- **`WaitReason` stays task M9.5.5b's.** `ready` still returns a bool. The no-signal wait is checked and logged by `Wakes::signalled` (`wake-up for run <id> waits: no hook signal yet`, once per reason, through `Wakes::log_wait`, which M9.5.5b can reuse for its reasons). `START_PROMPT` (decision 39's constant) is added here, because the bound's attention line needs it. `RunOrch.start_prompt` and its report stay M9.5.5b's.
- **Engine code is in a new pure `engine/first_turn.rs`** (`mcp_ready`, `woken`, `tick`, `restored`, `FIRST_TURN_WAIT_SECS`), not in `engine/wake.rs`, to keep both within budget. `wake::effect` holds only the first-turn branch.
- **A restore resets the in-memory fields** (`first_turn::restored`, from `orch_window::restored`). This matches what `#[serde(skip)]` gives a real restart, and the engine's in-memory restore test exercises it. `first_turn_pending` is serialized only when true, so `delivery/tests.rs::old_run_json_loads_with_local_delivery` (600 lines) round-trips unchanged.
- **`anthrex mcp`** spawns `notify_ready` (new, `forward.rs`; `READY_TIMEOUT` 5 s over the connect, the handshake and one reply) from the first `list_tools` of an orchestrator server. It runs beside the answer, not strictly after the answer is written, because rmcp has no hook after the write; the driver's gate makes the order immaterial. `serve_on` awaits it at the client's EOF, so a server that is closed at once still sends it.
- **`fake-agent`** (`orch_start.rs`, new): in PTY mode with an MCP server and no prompt after `--`, the session starts in four steps. It lists tools on a fresh server process (`mcp::list_tools`, the same conversation as `mcp_call`). It runs the `SessionStart` hook with `source` `startup`, or `resume` when resumed. A Codex session starts instead with its `Ready` title, which is its signal; as the daemon launches it its hooks are configured (`codex_hook_source`), but real Codex fires `SessionStart` only with its first turn (M3.11), so the fake runs it just before `UserPromptSubmit` (corrected in fix round 1, m3: this note first said the daemon launches Codex with no hooks). Unless resumed, it then reads its first message from the terminal with no `Stop` before it, and runs `UserPromptSubmit`. The message is recorded in `FAKE_AGENT_STDIN_FILE` as `{"at", "first_message"}`, with no `raw` key, so `read_messages` and `user_texts` skip it and every existing e2e count is unchanged. It is also kept as `FAKE_AGENT_RESULT` `{"first_message": <text>}` until the first `mcp_call`. That is how "a script's `expect` matches the pasted first message" is tested. A resumed session lists tools and runs `SessionStart`, but reads nothing: a resume never carried a prompt. One residual case is fake-only: the fake kills its listing server after `EXIT_GRACE` (2 s), so a daemon whose handshake takes longer would lose that session's notice. The run then shows `START_PROMPT` at the bound.
- **Existing tests updated, not weakened.** Five engine tests asserted the launch's `initial_prompt`; they now assert `None` plus the record's `first_prompt` and `first_turn_pending` (`orch.rs`, `promote.rs`, `chains_continue.rs` ×2, `chains_wave.rs`). `chains_continue.rs`'s handoff text gains the retry sentence, and the contract pins match it (ruling T1-1). `engine/tests/orch.rs::launched` now delivers the first turn (`McpReady`, then `OrchestratorWoken { first_turn: true }`), so every test built on it keeps its meaning; `launched_waiting` does not deliver it. Six `OrchestratorWoken` literals gain `first_turn: false`. The MCP and `fake-agent` stub daemons answer `McpReady` and record it. The comment of `daemon/tests/server_runs.rs::mcp_ready_is_answered_…` is updated; its body is not.
- **Extra tests** beyond the list: `a_lost_ready_notice_changes_nothing` (mcp); `an_open_attention_holds_the_first_turn` (driver, the acceptance's open-attention case: a `PermissionRequest` gives `Attention` with a signal, nothing is pasted, then `Stop` lets it go); `past_the_bound_with_the_notice_the_window_is_not_ready` (engine); `a_codex_fake_sets_its_ready_title_before_its_first_message` and `a_prompt_after_the_separator_is_still_the_first_message` (fake-agent). The relaunch test also checks what a `run.json` round trip keeps.
- **Pinning.** Every orchestrator e2e binary passed unchanged: `run_e2e_orch` 16, `run_e2e_orch_ops` 11, `run_e2e_orch_wake` 7, `run_e2e_chain` 3, `run_e2e_rounds` 2, `run_cli_orch` 10, `run_e2e_adapt` 8, `run_e2e_adapt_profile` 8, `run_e2e_bisect` 2, `run_e2e_large` 9, `run_e2e_pr_land` 5, `run_e2e_pr_review` 5, `run_e2e_pr_rounds` 3. Smoke stage 11j passed alone (`ANTHREX_SMOKE_ONLY=11j`). Stage 11f cannot run alone (`ANTHREX_SMOKE_ONLY` takes only 11i and 11j); the full smoke runs before the PR.
- **Timing.** The new wall-clock bounds are in `docs/timing-budgets.md`, "Recorded, from M9.5.5a".
- **Fix round 1 (2026-10-03).**
  - **Ruling T5a-1, the grace.** `signals_seen` stays required (C1). The driver reports a live orchestrator's window whose first turn waits without the notice once it has a signal (`OrchEvent::FirstSignal { run_id, window_id, launch }`, in-process only, so no protocol change; `RunService::report_first_signal`, on every check). The engine keeps the first report of the current launch (`RunOrch.first_signal_at`, `#[serde(skip)]`, reset with the other first-turn fields) and, on the `Tick` `MCP_READY_GRACE_SECS` (30 s) after it, sets `mcp_ready` and logs `first turn sent without the MCP ready notice after 30 s` once (`first_turn::grace`); both runtimes. Tests: `a_signalled_window_gets_its_first_turn_after_the_mcp_ready_grace` (a wrong window and a stale launch are ignored; 29 s nothing, 30 s the paste and the line, once), `the_notice_inside_the_grace_sends_the_first_turn_without_the_line`, `the_first_signal_is_reported_while_the_notice_is_missing` (driver).
  - **m1 and m2, a restart's session.** `RestartOrchestrator` (driver, `orch_ops.rs::restart_resumes`) resumes only when the first turn was delivered and the window knows a session. With the first turn pending it forgets the window's session first (`WindowManager::forget_session`), so the relaunch is fresh, never `--resume` of a session that had no turn. Either fresh case answers the new `OpResult::RestartedFresh`, on which the engine sets `first_turn_pending` again, starts its wait, and removes the restart's "resumed" note. Tests: `a_restart_that_cannot_resume_sends_the_first_prompt_again` (engine), `a_restart_resumes_only_a_session_that_had_its_first_turn` (driver: pending → fresh; delivered with a session → `--resume`; no session → fresh). `run_e2e_orch_ops.rs::waiting` now also waits for the first turn's delivery before its daemon restarts: without it, `restart_orchestrator_op_resumes_it` restarted a session whose first turn was still pending, which is now (correctly) fresh.
  - **m3, fake Codex.** As the daemon launches it (hooks configured, the status title on), a fake Codex session starts with its `Ready` title and runs `SessionStart` (`startup`) only with its first turn, just before `UserPromptSubmit`; a resumed one shows the title only. Claude is unchanged. `pty_first_message.rs`'s Codex test now runs with the daemon's hook flags (RED: the title never came, the hook ran instead). Every orchestrator e2e binary passes.
  - **m4, a superseded notice.** Fixed in the engine rather than filed: while a `RestartOrchestrator` is in flight, a `McpReady` naming the window is the old session's and is ignored (`first_turn::mcp_ready`); the new session's server sends its own after the restart, and the grace covers a notice that races the op result. Test: `a_notice_during_the_restart_is_the_old_sessions`. A notice from the old session that arrives after the op result would still count; Risks item 15.

- **Fix round 2 (2026-10-03), ruling T5a-2: the first-turn gate is Claude's only.** Claude runs no hook before its workspace trust prompt, and FU-F12 was Claude's. `orch/launch.rs::first_turn_pasted(route)` (the route's runtime is Claude) decides it. `orchestrator_window_spec(run, route, first_prompt)` takes the first prompt back and passes it as `initial_prompt` for a Codex orchestrator only. `orch_window::launch` sets `first_turn_pending` only when it is pasted, and a `RestartedFresh` re-arms the first turn only then: a Codex `RestartedFresh` is 9.3's `Restarted` (its window's spec carries the first prompt, so a fresh relaunch gets it on its command line again, and the restart's note stays as in 9.3).
